<a id="japan-migration-guide"></a>

# Japan: migration guide

Sources checked up to 23 Nov 2026.

How to go to Japan from Bangladesh, visa by visa, step by step, for Bangladesh passport holders, as of November 2026. A raised number such as 3 is an official source; N3 is a news, NGO (non-governmental organisation), research or guide source, listed under "Needs an official source"; a year, like N3 (2022), marks a source older than 2025. "Need to know" marks a point no source has yet confirmed. "You'll need:" lists what to have before starting a procedure.

JPY (¥) is the Japanese yen; Tk is the Bangladeshi taka (1 lakh is Tk 100,000). At Bangladesh Bank's rate for 19 October 2026, ¥100 was about Tk 78 and ¥1,000 about Tk 782 <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.

<a id="start-here"></a>

## 1 Start here

If you have never been abroad, read this first. Every point is set out in full further down.

<a id="can-you-go-now"></a>

### 1.1 Can you go now?

Yes, but few go: BMET (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) cleared 1,945 workers for Japan from 1 January to 18 November 2026 <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>. The main work visa, Specified Skilled Worker (SSW), needs a Japanese test (the JLPT at N4, or the Japan Foundation's JFT-Basic) and a skills test for your field <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. A visit visa never lets you work <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>.

<a id="your-first-five-steps"></a>

### 1.2 Your first five steps

1. Learn Japanese and pass JFT-Basic (Tk 3,800) or JLPT N4 ([Step 2: Learn Japanese and pass JFT-Basic or JLPT N4](#step-2-learn-japanese-and-pass-jft-basic-or-jlpt-n4)) <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>.
2. Pass your field's skills test, Tk 800 to 2,700 in Dhaka ([Step 3: Pass the skills test for your field](#step-3-pass-the-skills-test-for-your-field)) <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>.
3. Register on OEP, BMET's online system, and get an e-passport, Tk 4,025 for 5 years ([Step 4: Register on OEP and get your passport and police clearance](#step-4-register-on-oep-and-get-your-passport-and-police-clearance)) <sup>[5](#o5), [6](#o6)</sup>.
4. Find an employer, and have BMET check any demand letter free before you pay anyone ([Step 5: Find the employer](#step-5-find-the-employer)) <sup>[7](#o7), [8](#o8)</sup>.
5. Get the visa at VFS Global, the embassy's visa centre in Dhaka (free; VFS charges BDT 2,090), then do the 3-day orientation and get BMET clearance ([Step 8: The visa at the Japanese Embassy, then BMET clearance](#step-8-the-visa-at-the-japanese-embassy-then-bmet-clearance)) <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9), [N2](#n2), [8](#o8)</sup>.

<a id="what-it-really-costs"></a>

### 1.3 What it really costs

The tests, VFS charge and BMET fees come to about Tk 14,000 to 17,000; a BMET training-centre principal said in June 2026 that the government-approved total is Tk 148,500 <sup>[4](#o4), [N2](#n2), [10](#o10), [11](#o11), [8](#o8), [N3](#n3)</sup>. In 2019 RAB (the Rapid Action Battalion) said an unauthorised agency took Tk 8 lakh a person <sup>[N4 (2019)](#n4)</sup>. Pay agencies only by bank, into their own account, and keep receipts <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.

<a id="three-scams-to-watch-for"></a>

### 1.4 Three scams to watch for

- *"Japan will take 100,000 Bangladeshis":* Japan's foreign ministry said on 29 October 2026 that no such agreement exists <sup>[N5](#n5)</sup>.
- *A Japan visa sold for lakhs:* in 2026 families in Mymensingh said a ring took about Tk 5 lakh each for fake "agricultural visas" (see [Visit visa scams](#visit-visa-scams)) <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>.
- *"Study and earn":* students may work only 28 hours a week, with permission, and schools report breaches (see [Part-time work](#part-time-work)) <sup>[12](#o12), [13](#o13)</sup>.

<a id="who-to-call"></a>

### 1.5 Who to call

- In Bangladesh: Probash-Bondhu, the government's migrant call centre, 16135, free, or +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[14](#o14)</sup>.
- Interns in Japan: OTIT (Japan's intern regulator), English line, 0120-250-147, free; press 1 for emergencies <sup>[15](#o15), [16](#o16), [17](#o17)</sup>.
- The Bangladesh Embassy's labour wing, Tokyo: 03-3234-5801 extension 201 or 080-4352-4795 <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.

<a id="background"></a>

## 2 Background

<a id="in-one-minute"></a>

### 2.1 In one minute

Japan is short of workers and is opening up, but Bangladesh sends very few: BMET (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training, which clears every worker leaving Bangladesh) cleared 1,945 workers for Japan from 1 January to 18 November 2026, out of 652,482 for all countries <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>. Japan counted 44,938 Bangladeshis living there at the end of 2025, a third of them students, against 300,992 Nepalis <sup>[19](#o19), [20](#o20)</sup>. There are three ways in for work. Technical intern training (TITP, officially a scheme for learning skills, run through Japanese "supervising organisations") ties you to one employer, and from 1 April 2027 it is replaced for newcomers by Employment for Skill Development (ESD) <sup>[21](#o21), [22](#o22)</sup>. Specified Skilled Worker (SSW) (i) is a work visa for people who pass a Japanese test and a skills test in one of Japan's labour-short fields, and lets you change employer within your field <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. Graduates go on the Engineer/Specialist in Humanities/International Services visa, which 8,719 Bangladeshis held at the end of 2025 <sup>[20](#o20)</sup>. The gate is Japanese, not money: you need JLPT N4 or the JFT-Basic test, and only a few fields' skills tests are held in Bangladesh <sup>[3](#o3), [4](#o4), [23](#o23)</sup>. Since 2019 the government has said Japan-bound workers should pay little or nothing, and in June 2026 the principal of a BMET training centre told a recruiting agency's job fair that the government-approved cost is Tk 148,500; in 2019 a mobile court fined an agency that, the ministry and the Rapid Action Battalion said, was taking Tk 4 to 8 lakh per candidate <sup>[N7 (2019)](#n7), [N8 (2019)](#n8), [N3](#n3), [N9](#n9), [N4 (2019)](#n4)</sup>. Japan's minimum wages set in 2026 run from 1,085 to 1,280 yen an hour depending on the prefecture, all in force by 2 December 2026, and tax, insurance and agreed rent come off the wage <sup>[24](#o24), [17](#o17)</sup>. The traps are debt to brokers, being stuck with one employer as an intern, students working beyond 28 hours a week, and refugee claims: Japan recognised 187 refugees in 2025, and since June 2024 a third or later claim no longer stops deportation unless it brings material showing substantial reasons for recognition <sup>[25 (2022)](#o25), [21](#o21), [12](#o12), [26](#o26), [27](#o27)</sup>.

<a id="is-it-open-now-and-who-can-go"></a>

### 2.2 Is it open now, and who can go?

Japan is open to Bangladeshi workers on 19 November 2026, but the flow is small and depends on passing Japanese: 1,945 workers were cleared for Japan from 1 January to 18 November 2026, 23rd among destinations <sup>[2](#o2), [3](#o3)</sup>.

<a id="status"></a>

#### 2.2.1 Status

- *Open, and growing from a small base.* BMET's Overseas Employment Platform (OEP, its online system for registering and clearing workers) cleared 965 workers for Japan in 2023, 1,088 in 2024, 1,564 in 2025 and 1,945 from 1 January to 18 November 2026 <sup>[28](#o28), [29](#o29), [30](#o30), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- *TITP closes to newcomers after March 2027.* The law promulgated on 21 June 2024 abolishes technical intern training and creates Employment for Skill Development (ESD, ikusei shūrō), which starts on 1 April 2027 <sup>[22](#o22)</sup>. Interns already in Japan on that date carry on under the old rules and cannot switch into ESD <sup>[22](#o22)</sup>.
- *A new ceiling.* A Cabinet decision of 23 January 2026 set Japan's intake ceiling to the end of March 2029 at 805,700 SSW (i) workers and 426,200 ESD workers, 1,231,900 in all <sup>[31](#o31)</sup>. It also added three SSW fields: linen supply, logistics warehousing and resource recycling <sup>[31](#o31)</sup>.
- *Students face closer checks.* From April 2026 Japanese language schools must check every three months where and how long each student works, and from July 2026 new entrants must prove A1 Japanese by a test certificate or an interview rather than 150 hours of study <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many of the 1,945 workers cleared for Japan in 2026 went as technical interns, how many on SSW, and how many on other visas?

<a id="the-channels"></a>

#### 2.2.2 The channels

- *Technical intern training (TITP).* An intern works for one Japanese company, placed through a supervising organisation (kanri dantai, a non-profit cooperative licensed to oversee the companies that take interns), for up to 1 year in stage (i) and up to 2 years each in stages (ii) and (iii) <sup>[32](#o32), [7](#o7), [33](#o33)</sup>. At the end of 2025, 2,198 Bangladeshis were interns: 816 in stage (i), 1,335 in stage (ii) and 47 in stage (iii); all but 6 came through supervising organisations <sup>[20](#o20)</sup>. Bangladesh and Japan signed a Memorandum of Cooperation (MOC) on TITP on 29 January 2018 <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>. In October 2025 Japan was taking most Bangladeshi interns in care, industrial packaging, welding and car mechanics <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.
- *IM Japan, the government intern route.* In 2023 IM Japan (International Manpower Development Organization, Japan) took people who finished BMET's six-month Japanese course at 39 Technical Training Centres (TTCs) and 5 marine institutes, tested their language and fitness, and trained those it selected for five more months <sup>[N11 (2023)](#n11)</sup>. By August 2023, 461 had gone this way without paying migration costs, earning 150,000 to 190,000 yen a month, and those who complete 3 to 5 years get a one-off 600,000 to 1,000,000 yen business fund from IM Japan on return <sup>[N11 (2023)](#n11)</sup>. In 2019 candidates needed HSC, had to be aged 20 to 28, and men had to be at least 5 ft 4 in tall and women 5 ft <sup>[N12 (2019)](#n12)</sup>. In April 2025 the expatriates' welfare adviser said 695 technical interns had gone in seven years, and the ministry signed a second zero-cost training agreement, with the Japanese company Onodera User Run <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>.
- *Employment for Skill Development (ESD), from April 2027.* Three years of work meant to bring the worker up to SSW (i) level, in 17 fields <sup>[22](#o22)</sup>. Supervising organisations must get a new, stricter licence as "supervision and support organisations" <sup>[22](#o22)</sup>. ESD allows a change of employer at the worker's own wish under conditions (see [What ties you to the employer](#what-ties-you-to-the-employer)) <sup>[22](#o22)</sup>.
- *Specified Skilled Worker (SSW, tokutei ginō) (i) and (ii).* SSW (i) allows up to 5 years in total in one of 19 fields; SSW (ii), for workers at a higher skill level, has no limit on stay and is open in 11 fields <sup>[34](#o34), [22](#o22)</sup>. Bangladesh signed its SSW MOC with Japan in Tokyo on 27 August 2019 <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>. On 30 June 2026, 1,189 Bangladeshis held SSW (i): 334 in agriculture, 326 in care, 168 in construction, 105 in food service, 71 in car repair, 69 in food manufacturing, 65 in industrial products, 17 in hotels, 15 in aviation, 12 in building cleaning and 7 in driving <sup>[36](#o36)</sup>. 13 Bangladeshis held SSW (ii) at the end of 2025 <sup>[20](#o20)</sup>.
- *Engineer/Specialist in Humanities/International Services (gijinkoku).* The visa for graduates in office, IT, engineering and interpreting jobs; 8,719 Bangladeshis held it at the end of 2025, the largest work visa among them <sup>[20](#o20)</sup>.
- *Students.* 14,983 Bangladeshis held student status at the end of 2025 <sup>[20](#o20)</sup>. About 4,000 went in 2025, the government's 2026 target is 10,000, and Probashi Kallyan Bank (PKB, the state bank for migrants) lends up to Tk 10 lakh per student <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. Students may work at most 28 hours a week, and only with permission (see [Beliefs against the facts](#beliefs-against-the-facts)) <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.

<a id="who-qualifies"></a>

#### 2.2.3 Who qualifies

- *Japanese.* SSW (i) needs JLPT N4 or the JFT-Basic (Japan Foundation Test for Basic Japanese) <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. In Bangladesh the JLPT (Japanese-Language Proficiency Test) is run by the Japanese Universities Alumni Association in Bangladesh (JUAAB) at Farmgate, Dhaka, for Tk 3,060 at the December 2025 sitting; JFT-Basic costs Tk 3,800 <sup>[37](#o37), [4](#o4)</sup>. JFT-Basic is scored from 10 to 250: 200 or more is A2.2, the SSW level; 175 to 199 is A2.1; 145 to 174 is A1 <sup>[38](#o38)</sup>. ESD needs A1 (JFT-Basic or JLPT N5) or an equivalent course before work starts <sup>[22](#o22)</sup>.
- *Skills.* SSW (i) also needs the skills test for the field <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. In Bangladesh, Prometric (the company that runs most SSW tests) offers the care test (Tk 1,600), the agriculture test (Tk 2,700) and the construction test (Tk 800), plus JFT-Basic, at two centres in Dhaka, in Dhanmondi and Banani (see [JFT-Basic in Dhaka](#jft-basic-in-dhaka)) <sup>[4](#o4), [39](#o39)</sup>. Building cleaning and automobile transportation tests also had Bangladesh dates in November and December 2026; for other fields the test must be taken in Japan or another country <sup>[23](#o23), [3](#o3)</sup>. A worker who completed technical intern training (ii) is exempt from the SSW tests in the same field <sup>[40](#o40)</sup>.
- *Education and age.* The 2019 ministry guideline for interns sent by private agencies required SSC and age 18 to 35, and BMET keeps a databank of candidates who finished TTC Japanese courses <sup>[N8 (2019)](#n8)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many Bangladeshi women went to Japan in 2025 and 2026, and on which visas?
> - Are SSW tests for food service, food manufacturing or industrial products planned in Bangladesh, or must candidates sit them in Japan or another country?

<a id="the-size-of-the-flow"></a>

#### 2.2.4 The size of the flow

By BMET's count 5,152 workers went to Japan from 2004 to May 2025, 0.04% of all Bangladeshi overseas employment <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>. Workers who went to Japan, by year:
- 2014: 55 <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>
- 2015: 99 <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>
- 2016: 165 <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>
- 2017: 145 <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>
- 2018: 163 <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>
- 2019: 229 <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>
- 2020: 142 <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>
- 2021: 3 <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>
- 2022: 508 <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>
- 2023: 967 (OEP: 965) <sup>[41](#o41), [28](#o28)</sup>
- 2024: 1,088 <sup>[41](#o41), [29](#o29)</sup>
- 2025: 1,564 (OEP) <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>
- 2026, to 18 November: 1,945 (OEP) <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>

The government plans far more: the minister told parliament in April 2026 that Bangladesh intends to send 100,000 technical interns over five years without migration cost, and ministry sources told Prothom Alo in October 2025 that the yearly number could rise from 2,000 workers in 2026 to 50,000 in 2030 <sup>[N15](#n15), [N9](#n9)</sup>. An MoI (memorandum of intent) with Japan's National Business Support Combined Cooperatives (NBCC, a federation of more than 65 Japanese companies) foresees 2,000 workers in 2026, 6,000 in 2027 and 18,000 in 2028 <sup>[N16](#n16), [N17](#n17), [N18](#n18)</sup>. The 100,000 figure first came from Japan's side: at a Tokyo seminar in May 2025 NBCC's chairman, Mikio Kesagayama, said his federation was ready to welcome 100,000 Bangladeshi workers in the next five years, and in October 2025 NBCC told the Chief Adviser it aims to recruit them under TITP and SSW <sup>[N17](#n17), [N18](#n18)</sup>. After returning from Japan in early September 2025, the finance adviser said that 100,000 workers could be sent within three months if they know Japanese <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>. In August 2025 a presentation by the Association of Japanese Language Institutes in Bangladesh (AJLIB) said at least 43,000 students could be sent in three years <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>. In March 2026 a ministry committee was told that more than 100,000 new workers could be sent to Japan by 2030 if Bangladesh prepares properly and Japanese companies confirm demand <sup>[N20](#n20)</sup>. Officials at a June 2026 job fair said Bangladesh aims to send 500,000 workers to Japan within two and a half years <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>. On 29 October 2026 Japan's Ministry of Foreign Affairs said claims that Japan had agreed to take 100,000 Bangladeshi workers were false, and that Japan allocates no jobs by country (see [SSW scams](#ssw-scams)) <sup>[N5](#n5)</sup>.

<a id="who-is-in-japan"></a>

#### 2.2.5 Who is in Japan

Japan counted 44,938 Bangladeshi residents at the end of 2025, up 28.1% in a year and from 8,622 in 2012 <sup>[19](#o19)</sup>. They included 14,983 students, 9,382 family dependants, 8,719 graduates on gijinkoku, 4,538 permanent residents, 2,198 technical interns, 839 SSW workers, 821 on Designated Activities (a status covering many situations, among them graduates looking for work and refugee applicants waiting for a decision), 504 business managers, 441 on the Skilled Labour (ginō) visa and 117 care workers <sup>[20](#o20), [42](#o42), [43](#o43)</sup>. SSW is growing: Bangladeshi SSW (i) holders rose from 826 at the end of 2025 to 1,189 by June 2026 <sup>[44](#o44), [36](#o36)</sup>. Figures quoted in Bangladesh in 2025 were lower: Prothom Alo reported 31,536 Bangladeshis in Japan, about half of them students, in August 2025, and the expatriates' ministry gave 26,000 in October 2025 <sup>[N19](#n19), [N10](#n10)</sup>.

Japan had 300,992 Nepalis, 182,567 Myanmar nationals, 79,128 Sri Lankans and 34,911 Pakistanis at the end of 2025 <sup>[19](#o19)</sup>. At the end of 2025, 116,151 Nepalis were students, 72,978 family dependants, 48,813 on gijinkoku, 12,387 on SSW and 4,477 technical interns <sup>[20](#o20)</sup>. Sri Lanka had 4,003 on SSW and 2,870 technical interns <sup>[20](#o20)</sup>. In June 2026, 15,374 Nepalis and 4,920 Sri Lankans held SSW (i), against 1,189 Bangladeshis <sup>[36](#o36)</sup>. Japan had 4,125,395 foreign residents in all, including 456,618 technical interns and 390,296 SSW workers <sup>[45](#o45)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Designated Activities covers many situations: how many of the 821 Bangladeshis on it are job-seeking graduates, and how many are refused refugee claimants on temporary permission?
> - How many Vietnamese and Indonesians held technical intern and SSW status at the end of 2025, and what do they pay to come, for comparison with Bangladesh?

<a id="what-has-closed-it-before"></a>

#### 2.2.6 What has closed it before

The flow has never been banned, but it has stalled: departures fell from 229 in 2019 to 3 in 2021 <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>. A columnist who has lived in Japan for three decades wrote in November 2024 that Bangladeshis in Japan, who began arriving in the 1980s, passed 50,000 by 2000 and fell to 15,000 by 2020, most having to go home because there was no skilled-worker quota visa; Nepalis in Japan, he wrote, rose from under 5,000 in 2000 to over 200,000 <sup>[N21 (2024)](#n21)</sup>. An academic at North South University's Centre for Migration Studies wrote in October 2026 that a July 2005 Bangladesh-Japan agreement on technical training and work in Japan sent only 41 workers in eight years, 12 through BMET and 29 through private recruiting agencies, although small Japanese firms wanted workers; he blamed weak Japanese, unsuitable selection of workers, long administrative processes and poor coordination <sup>[N22](#n22)</sup>. He wrote that a 2015 understanding with IM Japan to train 500 Bangladeshis and a 2017 initiative for one-year training in manufacturing and construction also did not grow into large, continuous programmes <sup>[N22](#n22)</sup>. The Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo said in January 2025 that after the 2018 and 2019 MOCs the number of Bangladeshis in Japan rose from 18,000 to about 32,000 in two and a half years <sup>[N23](#n23)</sup>. The Financial Express reported in March 2019 that an earlier zero-cost intern route through private agencies had collapsed after recruiters allegedly charged jobseekers large sums <sup>[N8 (2019)](#n8)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Did Japan or Bangladesh suspend departures to Japan in 2020-21, and when did they resume?

<a id="where-it-may-grow"></a>

#### 2.2.7 Where it may grow

- *The overall gap.* Bangladesh's ambassador in Tokyo said in May 2025 that Japan's labour shortage could reach 11 million by 2040, and the expatriates' ministry's senior secretary said in November 2025 that Japan will need about 11 million foreign workers by then <sup>[N17](#n17), [N24](#n24)</sup>.
- *Care.* Ministry sources put Japanese demand for Bangladeshi care workers at 60,000, and care is already the second-largest Bangladeshi SSW field <sup>[N9](#n9), [36](#o36)</sup>. A North South University teacher wrote in September 2026 that Japan's Ministry of Health, Labour and Welfare (MHLW) counted about 2.15 million care workers in 2022 and expects to need about 2.4 million in 2026 and 2.72 million in 2040, about 570,000 more <sup>[N25](#n25)</sup>.
- *ESD from 2027.* ESD's ceiling is 426,200 workers to March 2029 <sup>[31](#o31)</sup>. Bangladesh signed its ESD Memorandum of Cooperation with Japan on 17 October 2026 (see [What changes from April 2027: Employment for Skill Development](#what-changes-from-april-2027-employment-for-skill-development)) <sup>[N26](#n26)</sup>.
- *Construction, factories and farms.* The same ministry sources put the possible numbers by 2030 at 40,000 construction, 20,000 factory, 20,000 care and 20,000 farm and car-service workers <sup>[N9](#n9)</sup>. NBCC said in October 2025 that its members need the most workers in construction, services, aviation, garments and agriculture <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>.
- *Nurses.* NBCC said in October 2025 that Japan will need more than 400,000 skilled nurses in the next few years and that it would consider recruiting more from Bangladesh <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>. The Philippines has long used its Economic Partnership Agreement (EPA) with Japan to train and send nurses and caregivers, with Japanese lessons and preparation for Japan's professional exams, a columnist wrote in September 2026; Bangladesh signed its own EPA with Japan in February 2026 <sup>[N25](#n25), [N27](#n27)</sup>.
- *Drivers.* NBCC expects demand for drivers, automobile and recycling workers to grow <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>. In October 2025 Miki Watanabe, founder of Japan's Watami Group, told the Chief Adviser he plans a driving school in Bangladesh to train drivers for jobs in Japan and was looking for a 12,000 square-metre site <sup>[N28](#n28)</sup>. A Prothom Alo column in January 2026 quoted the expatriates' ministry policy adviser, Zia Hasan, as writing that three Japanese companies had money, technology and an MoU ready to build a 30-acre driving track and would take 10,000 drivers a year, and that with the land they would have trained 30,000 people, Japanese included, within six months; he wrote that after at least five meetings there was no positive response, and the investors were told to look for land in Kaliakair <sup>[N29](#n29)</sup>.
- *Engineers in Hokkaido.* In November 2025 the mayor of Sapporo announced, among measures to draw green-energy investment to Hokkaido, a special visa for foreign engineers <sup>[N30](#n30)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - When will Japan publish the final list of Bangladesh's ESD sending organisations?
> - Does the Bangladesh-Japan EPA signed in February 2026 open a nurse or caregiver route like the Philippines' EPA?
> - Has Sapporo's special visa for foreign engineers been introduced, under what name, and what does it require?
> - Has Watami Group opened its driving school in Bangladesh, where, and does it lead to the SSW automobile transportation test?

<a id="how-a-job-reaches-you-and-who-takes-a-cut"></a>

### 2.3 How a job reaches you, and who takes a cut

A Japanese job becomes a visa only when a Japanese employer picks a candidate, through a supervising organisation for interns or directly for SSW, and an SSW candidate must already have passed the Japanese and skills tests himself <sup>[32](#o32), [46](#o46), [3](#o3)</sup>. The SSW MOC commits both governments to share information to keep out malicious brokers, and deposits and penalty contracts are banned for interns <sup>[35](#o35), [47](#o47)</sup>.

<a id="the-chain-by-rule"></a>

#### 2.3.1 The chain, by rule

**Technical interns:**
1. A Japanese company takes interns through a supervising organisation; Japan accepts interns only from sending organisations approved by Bangladesh and published by OTIT (the Organization for Technical Intern Training, Japan's TITP regulator) <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>.
2. OTIT's list for Bangladesh had 103 entries in October 2026, starting with BMET (No. 1) and BOESL (Bangladesh Overseas Employment and Services Limited, the state recruiting company, No. 2), both approved on 19 March 2018; the rest are private recruiting agencies, listed with their RL (recruiting licence) numbers <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>.
3. OTIT's list of past approvals, dated 30 July 2026, shows 21 Bangladeshi sending organisations removed since September 2022, one of them later re-approved; nine were removed on 30 July 2026: Unique Eastern (RL-021), Orbitals Enterprise (RL-113), Irving Enterprise (RL-215), Binimoy International (RL-351), Akash Bhraman (RL-384), Sadia International (RL-492), Catharsis International (RL-549), Al Khamis International (RL-0680) and Amin Tours and Travels (RL-981) <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>.
4. Under the 2019 ministry guideline the sending agency may not handle any money with interns or use sub-agents, and must have a branch in Japan <sup>[N8 (2019)](#n8)</sup>. The fee the ministry sets is deposited in the intern's own PKB account, from a PKB loan or cash, before BMET issues the smart card (the emigration clearance card) <sup>[N8 (2019)](#n8)</sup>.

**Specified Skilled Workers:**
1. The employer may sign directly with a Bangladeshi worker, or recruit through a sending organisation approved by MEWOE (the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and Overseas Employment) or BOESL; using an agency is optional <sup>[46](#o46), [35](#o35)</sup>. Under the 2022 exchange of notes MEWOE checks and approves these sending organisations, publishes their names and investigates any Japan reports for improper activity <sup>[50 (2022)](#o50)</sup>.
2. The employer first has a Demand Letter attested by the Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo, free, in about 3 working days <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>.
3. Through an agency, the agency gives BMET the job conditions, BMET pulls matching candidates from its database, and MEWOE issues an approval letter for the attested Demand Letter and candidate list <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>.
4. After the contract, the employer applies to a regional immigration office for a Certificate of Eligibility (CoE, the document a Japanese work visa is issued on) and posts the original to the worker, who applies for the visa at the Japanese Embassy in Dhaka <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>.
5. The worker or agency then gets BMET's emigration clearance card, about 2 working days after applying; it is not needed again after home leave <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>.
6. In Japan the worker is supported by the employer or a registered support organisation; Japan shares its list of these, and its improvement orders against employers, with MEWOE <sup>[50 (2022)](#o50)</sup>.

<a id="language-centres-and-training"></a>

#### 2.3.2 Language centres and training

The language course is where the time and money go. BMET teaches Japanese in 53 TTCs (online in 15), and more than 200 private language institutes operate across the country <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. BMET's TTC course runs four months, 40 trainees a batch, up to N4, for anyone with SSC; in 2019 the TTC course fee was Tk 1,000 <sup>[N9](#n9), [N12 (2019)](#n12)</sup>. In November 2025 the ministry's senior secretary said 33 TTCs had been designated to prepare workers for Japan <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>. Ministry officials admit the TTCs lack skilled Japanese teachers, and in 2025 the chairman of JITCO (Japan International Trainee and Skilled Worker Cooperation Organization, the body that promotes Japan's intern and SSW schemes) said Bangladesh still lacks Japanese teachers, and NBCC's chairman said trainers' Japanese could be better <sup>[N9](#n9), [N17](#n17), [N18](#n18)</sup>. The expatriates' welfare adviser said in 2025 that he had asked Japan to give language training itself and take the people it trains <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>. In March 2026 a ministry committee heard a proposal to start Japanese courses at all TTCs in phases, hiring enough local Japanese-language instructors and adding Japanese instructors through a virtual teaching platform <sup>[N20](#n20)</sup>. An academic at North South University's Centre for Migration Studies wrote in October 2026 that Japanese courses are concentrated in Dhaka, cost a lot and lack good teachers, so rural young people often struggle to pay for language study, tests and papers <sup>[N22](#n22)</sup>. Japanese companies are building their own pipelines <sup>[N31](#n31)</sup>:
- *Kawai Group Japan:* a residential training centre on 22 bighas in Birulia, Dhaka, which began training with Japanese teachers on 1 October 2025 <sup>[N9](#n9), [N10](#n10)</sup>.
- *Watami Group:* a language academy at Monohardi, Narsingdi, which its founder said in October 2025 trains 40 people a batch, plans to train and place at least 3,000, and had sent 52 workers to Japan for construction and farm work <sup>[N28](#n28)</sup>; in May 2025 he said a Watami school in Bangladesh trained 1,500 students a year and was planning to raise this to 3,000 <sup>[N17](#n17)</sup>.
- *NBCC:* training-and-recruitment agreements with Bangladesh, with candidates trained at centres in Khulna and at Kapasia, Gazipur <sup>[N31](#n31), [N18](#n18)</sup>.
- *Japan-Bangla Bridge Recruiting Agency (JBBRA) and Kaicom Dream Street BD (KDS):* agreements with BMET signed in May 2025 <sup>[N31](#n31), [N32](#n32)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What do private Japanese language centres in Dhaka charge for an N4 or JFT-Basic course in 2026, and what do they promise about jobs?

<a id="where-brokers-get-in"></a>

#### 2.3.3 Where brokers get in

- *Unauthorised agencies.* In October 2019 a ministry mobile court, with RAB (the Rapid Action Battalion), fined TMSS ICT, a concern of the NGO Thengamara Mohila Sabuj Shangha (TMSS), Tk 10.5 lakh for advertising Japan training and recruitment without permission <sup>[N7 (2019)](#n7)</sup>. The ministry's magistrate said TMSS's recruiting agency was not authorised for Japan and was about to collect Tk 4 lakh per candidate; RAB said the agency, Northern Recruiting Agency in Mirpur, had taken Tk 8 lakh each on promises of Japan jobs <sup>[N7 (2019)](#n7), [N4 (2019)](#n4)</sup>. TMSS Northern Recruiting Agency has been an OTIT-approved sending organisation since 22 September 2022, and it organised the June 2026 job fair where the Tk 148,500 government-approved cost was quoted <sup>[48](#o48), [N3](#n3)</sup>.
- *Visa "consultants".* In October 2020 a mobile court jailed the chairman and managing director of Dhaka's Visa Guide Center for a year and fined each Tk 50,000; one victim said the firm had asked him for Tk 7 lakh to send him to Japan <sup>[N33 (2020)](#n33)</sup>.
- *Overcharging by permitted agencies.* Prothom Alo reported in October 2025 that 27 agencies were permitted to train and place workers for Japan and that one had lost its licence for overcharging workers <sup>[N9](#n9)</sup>.
- *What the Japanese watch.* Bangladesh's finance adviser said in 2025 that Japanese officials had told him Nepal, which had sent 250,000, does not suffer from the dominance of recruiting agencies <sup>[N9](#n9)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which Bangladeshi agencies actually sent SSW and TITP workers in 2025-26, and how many each?

<a id="what-you-pay-and-what-you-earn-and-keep"></a>

### 2.4 What you pay, and what you earn and keep

On paper the Japan route is cheap: the principal of a BMET training centre put the government-approved cost at Tk 148,500 in June 2026, and IM Japan interns had gone without paying migration costs by 2023 <sup>[N3](#n3), [N9](#n9), [N11 (2023)](#n11)</sup>. A minimum-wage job pays 1,085 to 1,280 yen an hour before deductions at the rates set in 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.

<a id="the-official-cost"></a>

#### 2.4.1 The official cost

- *TITP.* The 2019 guideline set a zero migration cost policy for interns, with any ministry-set fee paid into the intern's PKB account, not to the agency <sup>[N8 (2019)](#n8)</sup>.
- *All routes.* The principal of BMET's Bangladesh-Korea Technical Training Centre (BKTTC) in Dhaka told a job fair run by TMSS Northern Recruiting Agency on 18 June 2026 that the government-approved cost for a worker to go to Japan is Tk 148,500, according to BSS (Bangladesh Sangbad Sangstha, the state news agency) <sup>[N3](#n3), [N9](#n9)</sup>. BSS reported in April 2026 that the Tk 15 lakh deposit required of agencies sending to Japan has been waived <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>.
- *ESD, from April 2027.* Everything a worker pays a sending organisation must not exceed two months' basic wage at the start of work, not counting overtime, and Japan says the worker's share should be as close to zero as possible <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>. Sending organisations must publish online how they calculate their fees <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which government circular sets the Tk 148,500 Japan cost, what does it cover, and does it apply to SSW, TITP or both?

<a id="what-people-really-pay"></a>

#### 2.4.2 What people really pay

Japan's 2022 survey of interns covered Vietnam, China, Cambodia, Myanmar, Indonesia and the Philippines, not Bangladesh, and found that 85.3% had paid a sending organisation, that they paid agencies and brokers 542,311 yen on average before arriving, and that 54.7% had borrowed to come <sup>[25 (2022)](#o25)</sup>. Some had paid deposits or penalty fees, which are banned <sup>[25 (2022)](#o25), [47](#o47)</sup>. In Bangladesh, the ministry and RAB said in 2019 that an unauthorised agency was taking Tk 4 to 8 lakh per candidate (see [Where brokers get in](#where-brokers-get-in)) <sup>[N7 (2019)](#n7), [N4 (2019)](#n4)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does a Bangladeshi SSW or TITP worker typically pay in all in 2026 (course, tests, agency, medical, ticket), and how much of it is borrowed?

<a id="what-the-job-pays"></a>

#### 2.4.3 What the job pays

- *Minimum wage.* From October 2026 the national weighted average is 1,177 yen an hour <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>. Tokyo pays 1,280, Kanagawa 1,279, Osaka 1,231 and Aichi 1,195; the lowest is Miyazaki at 1,085 (see [Minimum wage](#minimum-wage)) <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>. Some prefectures' new rates start late: Iwate and Kumamoto from 1 December 2026 and Okinawa from 2 December 2026, and until then their old rates (as low as 1,023 yen in Okinawa) apply <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- *Hours and overtime.* The normal limit is 40 hours a week and 8 a day, and overtime pays at least 25% extra; farm and fisheries work is exempt from the rules on hours, breaks and holidays <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- *What workers are paid.* IM Japan interns earned 150,000 to 190,000 yen a month in 2023, and press reports put SSW pay at 200,000 to 300,000 yen <sup>[N11 (2023)](#n11), [N34](#n34)</sup>. Prothom Alo put pay in taka at Tk 1,24,500 to 1,57,700 a month for IM Japan interns in April 2025, and at Tk 1,35,000 to 1,57,000 for workers generally in October 2025, paid into a bank account <sup>[N13](#n13), [N10](#n10)</sup>.

<a id="what-comes-off"></a>

#### 2.4.4 What comes off

Health insurance, pension and employment insurance premiums, income tax and inhabitant tax come off the wage, and so do rent and utilities if agreed in advance, which must match the real cost (see [How you are paid](#how-you-are-paid-and-what-may-be-deducted), and what may be deducted) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. Health insurance and pension premiums are split half and half with the employer <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. Inhabitant tax (local tax) is taken from June of the year after you earn, so a worker leaving Japan must settle it or name someone to pay it <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="remittances-from-japan"></a>

#### 2.4.5 Remittances from Japan

Bangladesh Bank recorded US$32.73 million of remittances from Japan in July to September 2026, 23rd among source countries (see [Sending money home](#sending-money-home)) <sup>[51](#o51)</sup>. Prothom Alo reported in August 2025 that Bangladeshi students in Japan send about Tk 900 crore a year <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>.

<a id="what-ties-you-to-the-employer"></a>

### 2.5 What ties you to the employer

How tied you are depends on the visa: a technical intern cannot change employer by choice, an SSW worker can within the same field, and an ESD worker will be able to after one to two years <sup>[21](#o21), [3](#o3), [47](#o47)</sup>.

- **Entering.** An intern comes for one company through a supervising organisation; an SSW worker comes on a CoE the employer applies for <sup>[32](#o32), [46](#o46)</sup>.
- **Renewing.** An intern's stay runs stage by stage: up to 1 year, then up to 2, then up to 2 <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>. SSW (i) is granted 4 months, 6 months or 1 year at a time, up to 5 years in total <sup>[3](#o3), [34](#o34)</sup>.
- **Quitting or changing employer as an intern.** Allowed only when "unavoidable circumstances" make training impossible, by telling the supervising organisation or company on OTIT's form 1-44 (see [Technical intern trainees: changing training site](#technical-intern-trainees-changing-training-site)) <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>. The employer cannot dismiss an intern mid-contract without an unavoidable reason <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Changing employer on SSW.** Allowed within the same field; moving to another field means passing that field's skills test (see [SSW: changing employer](#ssw-changing-employer)) <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. A Bangladeshi must also report any change of employer or status to the Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>.
- **Changing employer on ESD, from April 2027.** A move at your own wish is allowed after a restriction period of 1 to 2 years set for each field, once you pass the basic skills test and a Japanese test at A2.1 or above <sup>[47](#o47), [22](#o22)</sup>. No private job broker may arrange the move, and the new employer pays the old one a share of its recruitment and training costs (see [The new rules from April 2027](#the-new-rules-from-april-2027)) <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>.
- **Leaving Japan.** Inhabitant tax must be settled or left to a named agent <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Running away.** Japan counts interns who leave their workplace as missing (shissō); 3,950 went missing in 2025 (see [Overstay and unauthorised work](#overstay-and-unauthorised-work)) <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.

<a id="what-goes-wrong-most-and-what-people-wrongly-believe"></a>

### 2.6 What goes wrong most, and what people wrongly believe

The problems Bangladeshis meet are paying brokers for jobs that do not exist, interns going missing, students breaking the 28-hour rule, and refugee claims that end in refusal <sup>[N7 (2019)](#n7), [52](#o52), [13](#o13), [53](#o53), [26](#o26)</sup>.

<a id="what-goes-wrong"></a>

#### 2.6.1 What goes wrong

- *Debt and broker fees.* In Japan's 2022 survey of interns from six other countries, 54.7% had borrowed to come, and the average paid before arrival was 542,311 yen <sup>[25 (2022)](#o25)</sup>.
- *Runaway interns (shissō).* 3,950 interns went missing in 2025, 0.6% of all interns, down from 9,753 in 2023; of 36,386 who went missing in 2021-2025, 6,609 were still unlocated on 31 May 2026 <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. Few Bangladeshis are interns, but in 2024 and 2025 they went missing at a higher rate than the average: 20 in 2023 (1.6% of Bangladeshi interns, against 1.9% for all), 35 in 2024 (1.9%, against 1.2%) and 32 in 2025 (1.3%, against 0.6%) <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. Japan has stopped new intake from Vietnamese and Cambodian sending agencies with many runaways <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.
- *Students working too much.* Immigration matches employers' reports to find students working at several places, language schools must report suspected illegal work, and immigration uses what it finds when the student next applies to stay <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>.
- *Schools that abuse students.* In July 2023 Japan barred a Sendai language school, Mirai no Morigakuen, from taking new students for five years for making students sign pledges with penalties of about 1 to 3 million yen; the school called the action unjustified <sup>[N35 (2023)](#n35)</sup>.
- *Refugee claims.* Bangladeshis lodged 777 refugee claims in Japan in 2025, the fifth-largest group, up from 80 in 2021 <sup>[53](#o53)</sup>. Japan recognised 187 people of all nationalities as refugees in 2025, and turned down 9,214 claims at first instance <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.
- *Bank accounts and phones.* Selling or handing over your bank book, cash card or phone is a crime (see [Selling or lending your bank account or phone](#selling-or-lending-your-bank-account-or-phone)) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="beliefs-against-the-facts"></a>

#### 2.6.2 Beliefs against the facts

*"You must pay an agency or dalal to go to Japan."* On the government routes workers are not supposed to pay agencies, and IM Japan interns have gone free; what gets people in is Japanese and the skills test <sup>[N7 (2019)](#n7), [N11 (2023)](#n11), [3](#o3)</sup>.

*"Students can work as much as they like."* Students may work at most 28 hours a week in term (8 hours a day in long holidays), and only with permission; a 2026 newspaper report wrongly said they may work more <sup>[12](#o12), [N36](#n36)</sup>.

*"Japan pays Tk 2 to 3 lakh a month."* The minimum wages set in 2026 are 1,085 to 1,280 yen an hour; 200,000 to 300,000 yen a month is a press figure <sup>[24](#o24), [N34](#n34)</sup>.

*"An intern can move to a better-paying job."* Only for "unavoidable circumstances"; free moves come only with SSW, or with ESD from 2027 after 1 to 2 years <sup>[21](#o21), [3](#o3), [47](#o47)</sup>.

*"Doing hard jobs, or claiming asylum, gets your visa extended."* Two Bangladeshi asylum seekers said in 2017 that brokers and construction companies had told them their visas would be extended if they did Fukushima radiation clean-up, which they did in 2015; a justice ministry official said no residence permits were on offer for such work <sup>[N37 (2017)](#n37)</sup>.

<a id="how-the-irregular-routes-end"></a>

#### 2.6.3 How the irregular routes end

- *Repeat refugee claims.* Since 10 June 2024 a third or later claim no longer stops deportation unless the person brings material showing substantial reasons to recognise them (see [Asylum](#asylum)) <sup>[27](#o27)</sup>. Since 2018 repeat applicants are in principle not allowed to stay, and since May 2025 immigration sorts out claims it considers abusive under its "Zero Illegal Stayers Plan" <sup>[53](#o53)</sup>.
- *Runaway interns.* Of 36,386 interns who went missing in 2021-2025, 6,609 were still unlocated in May 2026 (see [Overstay and unauthorised work](#overstay-and-unauthorised-work)) <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.
- *Students who drop out to work.* Schools now check students' work every three months and report illegal work to immigration, which takes it into account when the student applies to extend <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>.

<a id="visas-not-open-to-bangladeshis"></a>

## 3 Visas not open to Bangladeshis

These routes exist but a Bangladesh passport holder cannot use them <sup>[54](#o54), [55](#o55), [56](#o56)</sup>.

- **Visa-free entry:** Japan has visa exemption arrangements with 74 countries and regions, and Bangladesh is not one of them <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>. A visa cannot be obtained on arrival in Japan or while staying there; a Bangladeshi must get one from a Japanese mission before travelling (see ["Visit visa (temporary visitor)"](#visit-visa-temporary-visitor)) <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.
- **Working holiday visa:** the programmes run only under bilateral arrangements, with 33 partner countries and regions as of 1 November 2026, and Bangladesh is not among them <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>.
- **JAPAN eVISA (the online tourist visa):** only people who live in Australia, Brazil, Cambodia, Canada, Saudi Arabia, South Africa, Taiwan, the United Kingdom or the United States can apply online, so a Bangladeshi living in Bangladesh applies on paper through VFS Global <sup>[56](#o56), [9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.
- **Transit visa:** Japan stopped issuing transit visas on 1 July 2026 (see [Transit](#transit)) <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a Bangladeshi who lives and works in Saudi Arabia use the JAPAN eVISA for a tourist trip, as the residence rule suggests? <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>
> - Has the Ministry of Foreign Affairs of Japan or the Embassy of Japan in Dhaka confirmed that transit visas ended on 1 July 2026? <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>

<a id="work-visa-specified-skilled-worker-ssw"></a>

## 4 Work visa: Specified Skilled Worker (SSW)

Specified Skilled Worker (SSW, "tokutei ginou" in Japanese) is Japan's work status for people who pass a skills test and a Japanese test in a field with labour shortages; it started on 1 April 2019 <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. There are two levels. SSW (i) lasts up to 3 years per grant and 5 years in total (6 if there are reasonable grounds), does not normally allow family, and comes with compulsory support from the employer or a registered support organisation <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. SSW (ii) is for proficient workers: it can be renewed without limit and allows a spouse and children to join <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. Japan and Bangladesh signed the SSW Memorandum of Cooperation (MOC, the inter-government agreement that lets a country send SSW workers) on 27 August 2019 <sup>[7](#o7), [57](#o57)</sup>. Under it the two governments share information on brokers who take deposits, impose penalties, use false documents or make unlawful charges <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. Japan plans to accept at most 805,700 SSW workers across all fields until the end of March 2029; 425,961 people held SSW (i) and 18,295 held SSW (ii) at the end of June 2026 <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. On 29 October 2026 Japan's Ministry of Foreign Affairs said that claims of Japan agreeing to take 100,000 Bangladeshi workers were false <sup>[N5](#n5)</sup>. There is no allocation per country: recruitment runs through language tests, skills certification and employer demand <sup>[N5](#n5)</sup>.

The order is: pass the Japanese test and the skills test, find an employer and sign a contract, the employer gets your Certificate of Eligibility (COE) from Japan's Immigration Services Agency (ISA), you get the visa from the Japanese Embassy in Dhaka through VFS Global (the company that runs the embassy's visa application centre), then BMET (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) clears you to leave <sup>[7](#o7), [9 (2024)](#o9), [8](#o8)</sup>.

<a id="who-pays-what-on-ssw"></a>

### 4.1 Who pays what on SSW

<a id="what-the-employer-must-pay"></a>

#### 4.1.1 What the employer must pay

- **Your pay:** at least what a Japanese worker doing the same job gets <sup>[7](#o7), [3](#o3)</sup>.
- **Support:** the 10 forms of support in the support plan (see [What your SSW support organisation must do](#what-your-ssw-support-organisation-must-do)), either itself or by paying a registered support organisation <sup>[7](#o7), [58](#o58)</sup>.
- **The COE:** ISA charges no fee for a Certificate of Eligibility <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.

<a id="what-you-pay"></a>

#### 4.1.2 What you pay

- **Japanese test:** JFT-Basic BDT 3,800 at Prometric in Dhaka, or the JLPT at Tk 4,356 (December 2026) <sup>[4](#o4), [60](#o60)</sup>.
- **Skills test:** at Prometric in Dhaka, BDT 1,600 for the long-term care evaluation test (skills/Japanese), BDT 2,700 for agriculture and BDT 800 for construction; an e-wallet adds about 2% <sup>[4](#o4), [61](#o61)</sup>.
- **Visa:** the Japanese visa is free for Bangladeshis, but VFS Global charges a service charge of BDT 2,090 in cash <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9), [N2](#n2)</sup>.
- **Bangladesh side:** the passport ([Step 4, item 2: Get a passport](#step-4-register-on-oep-and-get-your-passport-and-police-clearance-item-2)), police clearance ([Step 4, item 3: Police clearance, if asked](#step-4-register-on-oep-and-get-your-passport-and-police-clearance-item-3)), the pre-departure orientation fee ([Step 8, item 2: Do the pre-departure orientation (PDO)](#step-8-the-visa-at-the-japanese-embassy-then-bmet-clearance-item-2)) and BMET clearance fees ([Step 8, item 3: Get BMET clearance (the smart card)](#step-8-the-visa-at-the-japanese-embassy-then-bmet-clearance-item-3)) <sup>[6](#o6), [62](#o62), [8](#o8), [10](#o10)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Who pays the air ticket to Japan for an SSW worker: is it set by any Japanese rule or the Bangladesh Japan guideline, or left to the contract?

<a id="the-bangladesh-maximum"></a>

#### 4.1.3 The Bangladesh maximum

When the SSW agreement was signed in 2019, workers going to Japan were to pay nothing, because Japanese employers pay the service charge of the recruiting agencies the government enlists <sup>[N7 (2019)](#n7)</sup>. In 2025 the adviser to the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare said the cost of going to Japan "is not very high" <sup>[N9](#n9)</sup>. By October 2025 one agency approved for Japan had had its licence cancelled for overcharging workers <sup>[N9](#n9)</sup>. By April 2026 the Tk 15 lakh deposit agencies had to make had been waived, and the Ministry said it had moved fee payments to the banking channel and revised its Japan guidelines <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does the Ministry's current Japan SSW guideline ("Guidline SSW", probashi.portal.gov.bd) set as the most a Bangladeshi agency may take from an SSW worker, and is it still the zero-cost rule of 2019?
> - What do SSW workers really pay Bangladeshi agencies and language schools in 2026, in taka?

<a id="how-to-pay"></a>

#### 4.1.4 How to pay

Pay any agency only through the bank, into the agency's own account, and keep every receipt <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. Test fees are paid to Prometric online when you book, by card or e-wallet (see [JFT-Basic in Dhaka](#jft-basic-in-dhaka)) <sup>[61](#o61), [63](#o63)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-check-you-qualify-and-choose-your-field"></a>

### 4.2 Step 1: Check you qualify and choose your field

1. <a id="step-1-check-you-qualify-and-choose-your-field-item-1"></a>**Age:** you must be 18 or older <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. The Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo says 18 to 35 is mostly preferred and that education is generally not required, though some employers want up to HSC <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-1-check-you-qualify-and-choose-your-field-item-2"></a>**Fields:** SSW (i) has 19 designated fields <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>:
   - long-term care (nursing care);
   - building cleaning management;
   - industrial product manufacturing;
   - construction;
   - shipbuilding and ship machinery;
   - automobile repair and maintenance;
   - aviation;
   - accommodation;
   - automobile transportation (truck, bus and taxi drivers);
   - railway;
   - agriculture;
   - fishery;
   - food and beverage manufacturing;
   - food service;
   - forestry;
   - wood industry;
   - linen supply (added in January 2026) <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>;
   - warehousing and resource circulation (added in January 2026; they start only once preparations are complete) <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-1-check-you-qualify-and-choose-your-field-item-3"></a>**Extra rules in some fields:** nursing care needs the Nursing Care Japanese Language Evaluation Test as well as JLPT N4 or JFT-Basic; taxi and bus drivers and train operators need JLPT N3 or higher <sup>[7](#o7), [64](#o64)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-1-check-you-qualify-and-choose-your-field-item-4"></a>**Which tests are held in Bangladesh:** Bangladesh had November 2026 dates for JFT-Basic and the SSW (i) tests in long-term care, building cleaning, construction, automobile transportation and agriculture, and December dates for all of these tests except agriculture <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>. JFT-Basic runs again in Bangladesh from 1 December 2026 to 15 January 2027 <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>. For other fields, ISA says you can enter Japan on a Temporary Visitor (short-stay) visa and take the test there, or take it in another country that holds it <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-1-check-you-qualify-and-choose-your-field-item-5"></a>**What SSW (i) does not allow:** no side jobs, no family on SSW (i), and no move to a different field without passing that field's skills test <sup>[3](#o3), [7](#o7), [64](#o64), [23](#o23)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the food-service SSW test still held in Bangladesh, and when is the next session? It had no Bangladesh dates in November and December 2026 <sup>[66](#o66), [23](#o23)</sup>.

<a id="step-2-learn-japanese-and-pass-jft-basic-or-jlpt-n4"></a>

### 4.3 Step 2: Learn Japanese and pass JFT-Basic or JLPT N4

SSW (i) needs Japanese at level A2.2, shown by passing either the Japan Foundation Test for Basic Japanese (JFT-Basic) at A2.2 or the Japanese-Language Proficiency Test (JLPT) at N4 or higher <sup>[7](#o7), [67](#o67)</sup>. People who completed Technical Intern Training (ii) are exempt from both tests in the same field <sup>[7](#o7), [23](#o23)</sup>. Language is the main barrier: in December 2024, 3,091 people sat JLPT N4 in Bangladesh and 511 passed <sup>[N40](#n40)</sup>. BMET's Director General said it takes about six months to learn, and many drop out because they cannot pay their costs that long <sup>[N40](#n40)</sup>.

<a id="where-to-learn"></a>

#### 4.3.1 Where to learn

- **BMET Technical Training Centres (TTCs):** Japanese is taught at 53 TTCs, 15 of them in a hybrid online model <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>. TTCs teaching up to N4 include Bangladesh-Korea TTC Mirpur, Women's TTC Mirpur, Bangladesh-Korea TTC Chattogram, and the Narsingdi, Chapainawabganj, Tangail and Khulna TTCs; the course runs four months with 40 trainees a batch, and SSC holders can enrol <sup>[N9](#n9)</sup>. Centres teaching in the hybrid model include those three Mirpur and Chattogram centres and the Chapainawabganj, Khulna, Moulvibazar, Brahmanbaria, Kishoreganj, Kapasia, Barishal, Rajbari and Chandpur Sadar TTCs <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>. A ministry official said in 2025 that it does not matter whether you learn Japanese at a government or a private centre <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.
- **Example of a BMET course:** in 2025 Bangladesh-Korea TTC ran a 4-month course for JLPT N4, JFT-Basic and the SSW skills test, for SSC holders who had passed or completed a course at N5, with an assessment fee of Tk 1,000 and free application forms from the BMET website or the TTC <sup>[N41](#n41)</sup>.
  - *Who:* aged 17 to 35 and physically fit; 50 seats in Dhaka (Mirpur) and 30 in Chattogram, for September to December 2025 <sup>[N42](#n42), [N43](#n43)</sup>.
  - *Who gets in first:* JLPT N5 passers, then NAT N5 passers (the NAT-TEST is a separate Japanese test), then people who finished a Japanese course at a TTC or a BMET Institute of Marine Technology (IMT) <sup>[N42](#n42)</sup>.
  - *Timeline:* in Dhaka, applications closed at 4 pm on 24 August 2025, with the selection test and results on 25 August and results posted at www.bkttcdhaka.gov.bd; in Chattogram, applications closed on 26 August and the admission test was at 10 am on 27 August <sup>[N42](#n42), [N43](#n43)</sup>.
  - *Classes:* from 1 September 2025, Sunday to Thursday, 8 am to 2 pm <sup>[N42](#n42), [N43](#n43)</sup>.
- **Private institutes and universities:** more than 200 private language institutes operate, among them Ekush in Mirpur; Dhaka University (its Institute of Modern Languages), Green University and North South University also teach Japanese <sup>[N39](#n39), [N10](#n10), [N44 (2024)](#n44)</sup>.
- **Barendra University, Rajshahi:** under an agreement signed in September 2026 with SICIP (Skills for Industry Competitiveness and Innovation Program, an Asian Development Bank-funded government skills programme), the university runs ICT training (cloud computing, data engineering, AI and machine learning, network administration) with Japanese taught for jobs in Japan, from October 2026 to July 2028, for 1,200 trainees: 450 at its Rajshahi centre and 750 at its Dhaka centre <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>. The programme says it is working with employers and recruiting agencies to place trainees <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>.
- **Study materials:** the Japan Foundation's "Irodori: Japanese for Life in Japan" coursebook and the free IRODORI online course match JFT-Basic <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - When and how does a TTC announce its next Japanese intake, and where is the current list of TTCs teaching N4?

<a id="jft-basic-in-dhaka"></a>

#### 4.3.2 JFT-Basic in Dhaka

JFT-Basic is a computer-based test of about 50 questions in 60 minutes, held every month as a rule <sup>[38](#o38), [67](#o67)</sup>. Scores run from 10 to 250; 200 or more is A2.2, the SSW level <sup>[38](#o38), [67](#o67)</sup>. Since August 2026 it also reports A1 and A2.1 <sup>[38](#o38)</sup>.

**Test centres** (both run for Prometric, the test administrator) <sup>[39](#o39)</sup>:
- **BDJ01, Dhaka Dhanmondi:** New Horizons Computer Learning Center, Momtaz Plaza 3rd Floor, House 7, Road No 4, Dhanmondi, Dhaka 1205.
- **BDJ02, Dhaka Banani:** American Alumni Association (AAA), Delta Dahlia Tower, 3rd Floor, 36 Kemal Ataturk Avenue, Dhaka 1213.

**Fee:** BDT 3,800, paid when booking by 3D-Secure Visa or Mastercard, or by e-wallet with about 2% extra <sup>[4](#o4), [61](#o61)</sup>.

You'll need:
- your passport, the only ID Prometric accepts in Bangladesh <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>;
- a Prometric ID, with your name, passport number and date of birth entered exactly as in the passport <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.

1. Create a Prometric ID on Prometric's registration website; one person may hold only one <sup>[63](#o63), [65](#o65)</sup>.
2. Choose the date, venue and start time, and pay <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>. Book by three business days before the test, or four if the test is on a Saturday, Sunday or public holiday <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>. Bookings for tests from 1 December 2026 to 15 January 2027 open on 25 November 2026 and close on 12 January 2027 <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>. Seats are limited and the site may put you in a virtual waiting room <sup>[N46](#n46)</sup>.
3. On the day arrive 30 minutes early with your original passport and the Confirmation Letter; late arrivals cannot sit and there is no refund <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.
4. The score shows on screen at the end; the official result notification is on your Prometric "My Page" within 5 business days, and does not expire <sup>[65](#o65), [67](#o67)</sup>.
5. If you fail, you cannot sit again for 45 days; once you score 200 or more you cannot retake <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.

**If it fails:** the Japan Foundation does not handle bookings; contact Prometric's customer service through its Bangladesh inquiry page <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>. Prometric closes the centres for national events, such as on 12 February 2026 for the election, and asks candidates to rebook themselves <sup>[N47](#n47)</sup>.

<a id="jlpt-in-dhaka"></a>

#### 4.3.3 JLPT in Dhaka

The JLPT is held in Dhaka twice a year, in July and December by the Japanese Universities Alumni Association in Bangladesh (JUAAB), the local host <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>. The Chief Adviser said in October 2025 that two sittings a year are not enough for the demand <sup>[N28](#n28)</sup>. The December 2026 test is on 6 December 2026, for levels N1 to N5, at a fee of Tk 4,356; registration confirmation opened on 1 September 2026 at 11:00 am <sup>[60](#o60)</sup>. Online results come about two months after the test and the pass/fail notice about three months after <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>.

1. Create an account on JUAAB's JLPT portal and complete your profile <sup>[60](#o60)</sup>.
2. When registration opens, confirm your level and pay online <sup>[60](#o60)</sup>.
3. On the day bring the admit card and valid ID and arrive at least 30 minutes early <sup>[60](#o60)</sup>.

**Contact:** JUAAB, 28 Indira Road (1st Floor), Farmgate, Dhaka 1215; 01749762539, 01734054572; juaabjlpt@gmail.com <sup>[37](#o37)</sup>.

<a id="step-3-pass-the-skills-test-for-your-field"></a>

### 4.4 Step 3: Pass the skills test for your field

Each field has its own skills test, run by the body the field's ministry appoints <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. Prometric runs the SSW (i) tests for long-term care, food service, agriculture, food and beverage manufacturing, automobile repair, fishery, construction and accommodation <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>. In Bangladesh, the long-term care, construction and agriculture tests are taken at the same Prometric centres and booked the same way as JFT-Basic (see [JFT-Basic in Dhaka](#jft-basic-in-dhaka)) <sup>[23](#o23), [39](#o39)</sup>. Building cleaning and automobile transportation tests, which are not on Prometric's list, had dates in Bangladesh in November and December 2026 <sup>[23](#o23), [66](#o66)</sup>. ISA's SSW portal lists every test with its organiser's website <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>. SSW (ii) needs a separate SSW (ii) test, which may require work experience <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Who runs the building cleaning and automobile transportation SSW tests in Dhaka, where, at what fee, and how does a worker book?

<a id="step-4-register-on-oep-and-get-your-passport-and-police-clearance"></a>

### 4.5 Step 4: Register on OEP and get your passport and police clearance

1. <a id="step-4-register-on-oep-and-get-your-passport-and-police-clearance-item-1"></a>**Register on OEP.** OEP (the Overseas Employment Platform, BMET's online system) is where you register, book the pre-departure orientation and apply for emigration clearance <sup>[11](#o11), [70](#o70)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>:
   - your NID (national identity card);
   - a mobile phone that can receive codes;
   - a bKash, Nagad or other mobile-banking account for the fee.

   1. In Google Chrome open employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/login-with-otp and press "অনুগ্রহ করে এখানে ক্লিক করুন" (Please click here) <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.
   2. Type every detail exactly as on your NID, with no dots in the name, and press "যাচাই করুন" (Verify) <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.
   3. Enter the OTP (one-time code) sent to your phone, fill in the full form, pay by mobile banking and download your registration card <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

   **If it fails:** the OEP hotline, 09614445271 <sup>[71](#o71)</sup>; Probash-Bondhu, the Wage Earners' Welfare Board's 24-hour call centre, 16135 free in Bangladesh or +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[14](#o14)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-4-register-on-oep-and-get-your-passport-and-police-clearance-item-2"></a>**Get a passport.** Fill in the e-passport form exactly as on your NID; applicants over 20 must use the NID <sup>[72](#o72)</sup>. A 48-page, 5-year e-passport costs Tk 4,025 (regular, within 15 working days), Tk 6,325 (express) or Tk 8,625 (super express); the 10-year one costs Tk 5,750, Tk 8,050 or Tk 10,350 <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>. Pay online through A-Challan, DGePay or ShurjoPay (cards, bKash, Nagad, Rocket and others), or at any bank through A-Challan <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>. Apply at the divisional or regional passport office for your present address, and bring the original NID <sup>[72](#o72)</sup>. The passport is the only ID for the Prometric tests, so get it before you book <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-4-register-on-oep-and-get-your-passport-and-police-clearance-item-3"></a>**Police clearance, if asked.** Apply at pcc.police.gov.bd with a passport valid at least 3 months and an attested scan of its data page; the police verify you at your present address, and you track it by texting "PCC S \<reference number\>" to 26969 <sup>[62](#o62)</sup>. The portal lists a Tk 1,500 fee and Bangladesh Police's guidance Tk 500 <sup>[62](#o62), [73](#o73)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does ISA or the Japanese Embassy require a police clearance certificate for an SSW COE or visa, or do only some employers ask for it?

<a id="step-5-find-the-employer"></a>

### 4.6 Step 5: Find the employer

ISA's process is that people overseas apply to employers directly or through private employment agencies, and the employer may recruit overseas directly or through an intermediary <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. ISA advises checking whether a sending organisation is licensed and its fees reasonable, where your country requires one <sup>[64](#o64), [3](#o3)</sup>.

<a id="through-bmet-and-licensed-agencies"></a>

#### 4.6.1 Through BMET and licensed agencies

- **Who is approved:** news reports in 2025 and 2026 give 27, 95 and 96 Bangladeshi recruiting agencies approved for Japan; only about 30 send workers in a year, and from 2004 to April 2025 they sent 5,014 workers <sup>[N9](#n9), [N39](#n39), [N40](#n40)</sup>.
- **How a job order reaches Bangladesh:** a Japanese company or supervising organisation issues a demand letter to an enlisted Bangladeshi sending organisation and sends it to the Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo, which verifies and attests it, usually within 3 to 7 days, for JPY 1,000 a page, paid by bank <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>. The demand letter must state the number of workers, employer, job, contract period, age, basic salary, hours, overtime, accommodation, transport, food, medical benefits and air ticket <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.
- **Government channels:** the Ministry set up a "Japan Cell" for Japan recruitment, with a Facebook page and an email channel for queries <sup>[N39](#n39), [N31](#n31)</sup>; its focal person in August 2025 was Md. Hedayetul Islam Mondal <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>. In May 2025 BMET signed agreements with Kaicom Dream Street (a training cell at Monohardi TTC) and with NBCC, a federation of Japanese companies, and Japan-Bangla Bridge Recruiting Agency, to train and place TITP and SSW workers <sup>[N32](#n32)</sup>.
- **Matching seminars in Japan:** the Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo and JITCO bring Japanese employers and Bangladeshi sending agencies together: about 40 companies and agencies in Osaka in January 2025, and about 250 organisations at a seminar and matching event in Nagoya in November 2025, after which four MoUs and agreements were signed between Bangladeshi sending agencies and Japanese recruiters <sup>[N23](#n23), [N24](#n24)</sup>. The ambassador said in August 2025 that 22 organisations and more than 100 companies had recruited Bangladeshi workers in the previous two months <sup>[N19](#n19)</sup>.

**Check it is real:** BMET checks a job visa or demand letter free within 3 working days; bring the visa or letter and a copy of the passport page <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Where is the official list of agencies the Ministry has approved for Japan SSW, and does BMET's OEP show which agency holds an attested Japan demand letter?
> - What are the Japan Cell's phone number, email and office address, and does it take enquiries from individual workers?

<a id="on-your-own"></a>

#### 4.6.2 On your own

ISA says that without a sending organisation you can search corporate hiring websites and job fairs for foreign nationals <sup>[64](#o64)</sup>. Its SSW portal lists job events by Japanese ministries, local governments and foreign governments; on 19 November 2026 none was taking applications <sup>[74](#o74)</sup>. The interview can be held with the company directly or through an employment placement company <sup>[40](#o40)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which Japanese job portals or matching services do Bangladeshi SSW workers actually find employers through, and are any run or approved by a Japanese ministry?

<a id="step-6-sign-the-employment-contract"></a>

### 4.7 Step 6: Sign the employment contract

1. <a id="step-6-sign-the-employment-contract-item-1"></a>**Read it before you sign.** The company must give you a written notice of employment conditions; ISA tells workers to check the job, work location and salary <sup>[64](#o64)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-6-sign-the-employment-contract-item-2"></a>**Check the pay.** It must be at least what a Japanese worker doing the same job gets <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-6-sign-the-employment-contract-item-3"></a>**Check there is no deposit or penalty.** The worker must not have paid any security deposit or signed any contract setting penalties; this is a condition of the status itself <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-6-sign-the-employment-contract-item-4"></a>**Get the advance guidance.** Before the COE application, the employer or its support organisation must explain in person or by video call the working conditions, the job, the immigration procedures and whether any deposit has been collected <sup>[7](#o7), [58](#o58)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-6-sign-the-employment-contract-item-5"></a>**The medical check.** After the contract the employer arranges a medical check; the medical examination report is one of the COE documents <sup>[7](#o7), [75](#o75)</sup>.

<a id="step-7-the-employer-applies-for-your-coe"></a>

### 4.8 Step 7: The employer applies for your COE

The Certificate of Eligibility (COE) is ISA's certificate that you meet the conditions for SSW; you show it at the embassy and on landing <sup>[7](#o7), [75](#o75)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-7-the-employer-applies-for-your-coe-item-1"></a>**Who applies:** in principle you, through the staff of your Japanese employer acting as proxy, at the Regional Immigration Services Bureau <sup>[7](#o7), [59](#o59)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-7-the-employer-applies-for-your-coe-item-2"></a>**What goes in:** the application form (you and the company fill it in), the medical report, your test pass certificates, the support plan and company documents <sup>[75](#o75), [7](#o7)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-7-the-employer-applies-for-your-coe-item-3"></a>**Cost and time:** free; the standard time is 1 to 3 months <sup>[59](#o59), [64](#o64)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-7-the-employer-applies-for-your-coe-item-4"></a>**What you get back:** the COE goes to the employer, which sends it to you for the visa; since 17 March 2023 it can be issued by email <sup>[7](#o7), [59](#o59)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-7-the-employer-applies-for-your-coe-item-5"></a>**If it is refused:** there is no appeal <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long is a COE valid for an SSW visa application, and what happens if it expires before the visa is issued?

<a id="step-8-the-visa-at-the-japanese-embassy-then-bmet-clearance"></a>

### 4.9 Step 8: The visa at the Japanese Embassy, then BMET clearance

1. <a id="step-8-the-visa-at-the-japanese-embassy-then-bmet-clearance-item-1"></a>**Apply for the visa at VFS Global.** Since 3 November 2024 the Embassy of Japan takes visa applications only through VFS Global, Delta Life Tower, 4th Floor, Road 90, Plot 37, Gulshan-2, Dhaka-1212 (09606 777 910, info.japanbd@vfshelpline.com) <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>. Book online; slots for the month after next open on the 15th of each month <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>:
   - the visa application form;
   - your valid passport and a copy of its ID page;
   - one photograph;
   - a photocopy of the COE;
   - an airline booking slip.

   The visa is free; VFS charges BDT 2,090 in cash <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9), [N2](#n2)</sup>. Processing takes at least 7 working days from the application at VFS to the return of your passport, and VFS may not accept an application whose travel date is more than three months away <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9), [N2](#n2)</sup>. A single-entry visa is basically valid for three months: enter Japan within three months of its issue <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>. If the visa is refused, the embassy gives no specific reason and does not accept a new application for the same purpose within six months <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-8-the-visa-at-the-japanese-embassy-then-bmet-clearance-item-2"></a>**Do the pre-departure orientation (PDO).** The 3-day PDO is compulsory, at one of 70 institutes; apply on OEP and pay the institute's fee against a money receipt <sup>[8](#o8), [11](#o11)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-8-the-visa-at-the-japanese-embassy-then-bmet-clearance-item-3"></a>**Get BMET clearance (the smart card).** With it you are insured and need no separate Welfare Board membership; your family gets a Tk 3 lakh grant from the Board if you die abroad <sup>[77](#o77), [78](#o78), [11](#o11)</sup>.
   1. **Through an agency:** the agency applies after the visa with its application, the visa copy, the attested contract, your attested registration card, its undertaking on Tk 300 stamp paper and pay orders for the fees; BMET clears within 3 working days <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
   2. **If you found the job yourself:** apply at BMET's emigration branch in Dhaka or the Chattogram district employment office with your application, the visa copy, the contract, the attested registration card, a Tk 300 stamp declaration and pay orders; 3 working days <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
   3. **Fees:** welfare fee Tk 5,500 from 1 October 2026, insurance Tk 1,000 once, smart card fee Tk 250, and source income tax of Tk 250 (attested visa) or Tk 400 (unattested) for a single visa, or Tk 1,200 (skilled) or Tk 800 (unskilled) for a group visa <sup>[10](#o10), [11](#o11), [8](#o8)</sup>.

   **Check it is real** at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check; airport welfare desks check smart cards before you leave <sup>[77](#o77), [8](#o8)</sup>. **If it goes wrong:** BMET's Director (Emigration), +8802222222415, 01711-135901, diremi@bmet.gov.bd <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

4. <a id="step-8-the-visa-at-the-japanese-embassy-then-bmet-clearance-item-4"></a>**Know your insurance.** Jiban Bima Corporation (JBC) insures workers who leave with BMET clearance for Tk 10 lakh, for workers aged 18 to 55, for 5 years, for the one-time Tk 1,000 <sup>[79 (2024)](#o79), [11](#o11)</sup>. It pays on death or permanent disability, and Tk 50,000 if you lose the job and return within 6 months, unless you went entirely at the employer's cost <sup>[11](#o11), [79 (2024)](#o79)</sup>. The Welfare Board checks claims and JBC pays; you can download the certificate from JBC's e-service <sup>[79 (2024)](#o79)</sup>.

5. <a id="step-8-the-visa-at-the-japanese-embassy-then-bmet-clearance-item-5"></a>**If you need a loan.** PKB (Probashi Kallyan Bank, the government bank for migrants) lends up to Tk 3 lakh at 9% simple interest over up to 3 years, deciding within 7 working days <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>. You must be 18 to 60 and not in default, with two guarantors (one solvent person and one family member) <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>. Bring the application form, 3 photos, copies of NID, passport, visa and BMET card, and 3 signed MICR cheques from your own account; apply at the branch for your permanent address or on mygov.bd <sup>[80](#o80), [81 (2019)](#o81)</sup>. Fees are 0.5% processing and 0.5% documentation plus VAT, and Tk 900 per lakh for risk cover <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>. Women have a separate loan on the same terms <sup>[82](#o82)</sup>. **If it goes wrong:** PKB loans department, 01716-175560, migration@pkb.gov.bd; grievance officer, +88-02-48321195, gm2@pkb.gov.bd <sup>[83](#o83)</sup>.

6. <a id="step-8-the-visa-at-the-japanese-embassy-then-bmet-clearance-item-6"></a>**Before you fly,** check you have your passport, visa, COE, contract, the agency's details and the BMET card, keep copies at home, and never carry a packet for anyone <sup>[11](#o11), [64](#o64)</sup>.

<a id="step-9-arrival-residence-card-and-your-support-organisation"></a>

### 4.10 Step 9: Arrival, residence card and your support organisation

1. <a id="step-9-arrival-residence-card-and-your-support-organisation-item-1"></a>**Pick-up.** Someone from the company or the registered support organisation meets you at the airport and takes you to the workplace or home <sup>[7](#o7), [64](#o64)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-9-arrival-residence-card-and-your-support-organisation-item-2"></a>**Residence card.** You get a residence card on entry, though it may be issued later <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-9-arrival-residence-card-and-your-support-organisation-item-3"></a>**First weeks.** You attend a life orientation, register as a resident at the city office, open a salary bank account and secure housing <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. The employer or support organisation helps with the housing contract (as guarantor or with company housing), the bank account, phone, electricity and gas, and goes with you to the city office for residence, social security and tax procedures <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-9-arrival-residence-card-and-your-support-organisation-item-4"></a>**Your support for the whole of SSW (i).** The employer must give you 10 forms of support, itself or by paying a registered support organisation (RSO, a body registered with ISA to give this support) (see [What your SSW support organisation must do](#what-your-ssw-support-organisation-must-do)) <sup>[7](#o7), [58](#o58)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-9-arrival-residence-card-and-your-support-organisation-item-5"></a>**Changing job.** You may move to another employer in the same job category (see [SSW: changing employer](#ssw-changing-employer)) <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.

<a id="ssw-ii-and-family"></a>

### 4.11 SSW (ii) and family

SSW (ii) needs an SSW (ii) skills test, which may require work experience <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>. From 1 April 2027 it will also need Japanese at B1 level or higher, under a ministerial ordinance <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. It covers 11 fields; in industrial product manufacturing only machining and metal processing, electric and electronic assembly, and metal surface treatment <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. The stay is renewed for 3 years, 2 years, 1 year or 6 months at a time without limit, a spouse and children can join if requirements are met, and the employer has no duty to provide support <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. A nursing-care SSW worker who passes Japan's certified care worker exam can change to the "Nursing Care" status, also renewable without limit <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>.

<a id="ssw-scams"></a>

### 4.12 SSW scams

<a id="done-to-workers"></a>

#### 4.12.1 Done to workers

**"Japan has agreed to take 100,000 Bangladeshis"**

The figure goes back to May 2025, when the chairman of NBCC, a Japanese business federation, told a Tokyo seminar his federation was ready to welcome 100,000 Bangladeshi workers over five years (see [The size of the flow](#the-size-of-the-flow)) <sup>[N17](#n17)</sup>. In October 2026 several Bangladeshi TV channels reported, and social-media posts spread, that Japan would take 100,000 Bangladeshi workers a year; one such post on X had 1.8 million views <sup>[N5](#n5), [N49](#n49)</sup>. On 29 October 2026 Japan's Ministry of Foreign Affairs said no such agreement existed, and its press secretary, Kitamura Toshihiro, said the 27 October summit made no decision or agreement on it <sup>[N5](#n5), [N49](#n49)</sup>. The summit's joint statement instead expects up to 1 trillion yen of Japanese private investment to create 200,000 jobs inside Bangladesh, and the ESD MOC signed on 17 October 2026 sets no yearly number of workers <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>. **Spot it:** there is no quota per country; a job needs your own test passes and an employer <sup>[N5](#n5)</sup>.

**Training firms that charge for a Japan job**

In 2019 a mobile court fined TMSS ICT Tk 10.5 lakh for advertising Japan training and recruitment without authorisation; it was about to take Tk 4 lakh from each candidate <sup>[N7 (2019)](#n7)</sup>.
