By October 2025 one agency approved for Japan had had its licence cancelled for overcharging <sup>[N9](#n9)</sup>. In August 2025 the Chief Adviser's special envoy on international affairs said manpower syndicates charge extra money to send workers to Japan <sup>[N50](#n50)</sup>. **Spot it:** publishing a job-abroad advertisement without the government's or BMET's prior approval is itself an offence, punishable by up to 1 year in jail and a fine of at least Tk 50,000 <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>; BMET checks a demand letter free (see [Through BMET and licensed agencies](#through-bmet-and-licensed-agencies)) <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

**"Pass our course and the job is guaranteed"**

Passing JFT-Basic or a skills test does not guarantee a COE or a visa <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>. **Spot it:** the 2025 BMET course at Bangladesh-Korea TTC charged a Tk 1,000 assessment fee, and the tests themselves are booked online with Prometric in your own name (see [JFT-Basic in Dhaka](#jft-basic-in-dhaka)) <sup>[N41](#n41), [63](#o63)</sup>.

**A contract with a deposit or penalty**

Taking a deposit or setting a penalty is grounds to refuse the status, and the two governments share information on brokers who do it <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. **Spot it:** the employer must tell you before the COE application whether any deposit has been collected ([Step 6, item 4: Get the advance guidance](#step-6-sign-the-employment-contract-item-4)) <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What do Japan-bound SSW workers report being charged by language schools and agencies in 2026, and for what?

<a id="tried-by-workers"></a>

#### 4.12.2 Tried by workers

- **Fake certificates and bank statements.** The Chief Adviser's special envoy said in 2025 that fake certificates and forged bank statements are a major reason Bangladeshis struggle to get Japan visa appointments <sup>[N40](#n40)</sup>. Falsified JLPT results are sold; the Japan Foundation confirms whether a JLPT certificate taken outside Japan is genuine <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>. JFT-Basic results are checked against the Prometric record in the candidate's own passport details <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.
- **Cheating in the test.** Taking or sharing test content is cheating and can invalidate the score <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>. Holding more than one Prometric ID is prohibited <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.
- **Buying a visa.** Buying or selling a visa or work permit inside Bangladesh, or getting one by illegal means, is a crime under the Overseas Employment and Migrants Act 2013: 2 to 7 years' rigorous imprisonment and a fine of at least Tk 3 lakh <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>.

<a id="where-to-get-help-by-stage"></a>

#### 4.12.3 Where to get help, by stage

- **Tests:** Prometric's customer service through its Bangladesh inquiry page for JFT-Basic and skills-test bookings <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>; JUAAB for the JLPT, 01749762539, 01734054572, juaabjlpt@gmail.com <sup>[37](#o37)</sup>.
- **Before you pay or fly:** complain about an agency to BMET's Director General with an application and proof, free, decided within 30 working days <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>; or to the Ministry's enforcement branch with documentary proof, your address and the agency's RL number <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>. Taking money on a false promise of a job abroad, or keeping a worker's passport, carries up to 5 years in jail and a fine of at least Tk 1 lakh, and the offence is cognisable and non-bailable <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>.
- **COE and status questions in Japan:** ISA's SSW call centre, 0570-013904 (03-5796-7112 from abroad), Monday to Friday 8:30 am to 5:15 pm; it has no Bengali line <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>. ISA's Immigration Information Center at each regional office answers in several languages by phone, in person or email <sup>[75](#o75)</sup>.
- **No support or a broken promise from the employer:** the nearest immigration office <sup>[58](#o58)</sup>.
- **Unpaid wages or abuse:** the Labour Welfare Wing of the Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo works on unpaid wages through labour courts with legal aid, and visits workplaces, hospitals and detention centres; its contact is the Counsellor (Labour), 3-29 Kioicho, Chiyoda-ku, Tokyo 102-0094, +81 3 3234 5801, fstokyo@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[18](#o18), [48](#o48)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has the Labour Welfare Wing in Tokyo recovered unpaid wages for any SSW worker, and how long did it take?
> - Which email does the labour wing answer now: fstokyo@probashi.gov.bd, given on OTIT's list, or fslabor@mofa.gov.bd, given on the embassy's labour wing page <sup>[48](#o48), [18](#o18)</sup>?

<a id="work-visa-technical-intern-training-titp-and-the-new-employment-for-skill-development-system"></a>

## 5 Work visa: Technical Intern Training (TITP) and the new Employment for Skill Development system

The Technical Intern Training Program (TITP, "ginou jisshu") brings people to Japan as "technical intern trainees" for up to 5 years: 1 year in TITP (i), 2 in TITP (ii) and 2 in TITP (iii) <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. From 1 April 2027 it is replaced by Employment for Skill Development (ESD, "ikusei shuro"), a 3-year route designed to bring workers to SSW (i) level <sup>[22](#o22)</sup>. Japan and Bangladesh signed the TITP MOC on 29 January 2018, and Japan takes Bangladeshi trainees only from sending organisations the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare has accredited <sup>[57](#o57), [88](#o88)</sup>. About 2,500 Bangladeshi technical interns are in Japan, according to the Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo <sup>[57](#o57)</sup>. People who complete TITP (ii) can move to SSW (i) in the same field without the tests <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.

<a id="how-a-titp-job-reaches-you"></a>

### 5.1 How a TITP job reaches you

Three bodies stand between you and the job:
- **The sending organisation (Bangladesh):** an agency, BMET or BOESL accredited by the Ministry and listed by Japan's Organization for Technical Intern Training (OTIT) <sup>[48](#o48), [88](#o88)</sup>. BMET (BGD000001) and BOESL (BGD000002, the government-owned Bangladesh Overseas Employment and Services Limited) were approved on 19 March 2018 <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>.
- **The supervising organisation (Japan):** a non-profit body, usually a business cooperative, licensed by the Japanese ministries, that matches trainees with companies and audits them <sup>[7](#o7), [17](#o17)</sup>.
- **The implementing organisation:** the Japanese company where you train and work, under an employment contract <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

The supervising organisation sends a demand letter to the Bangladeshi sending organisation through the Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo, which attests it (see [Through BMET and licensed agencies](#through-bmet-and-licensed-agencies)) <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.

<a id="the-zero-cost-government-route-im-japan"></a>

#### 5.1.1 The zero-cost government route (IM Japan)

Under the Ministry's agreement with IM Japan (the International Manpower Development Organization, Japan), trainees go with no migration cost after BMET's Japanese course at a TTC or Institute of Marine Technology; IM Japan then tests their language and fitness <sup>[N11 (2023)](#n11)</sup>. As reported in April 2025, the course lasts four months and those who pass get four more months of training managed by IM Japan before going as interns; in 2023 it was six months, then five <sup>[N13](#n13), [N11 (2023)](#n11)</sup>. By August 2023, 461 had gone this way; they were paid JPY 150,000 to 190,000 a month and get a one-off JPY 600,000 to 1,000,000 on return after 3 to 5 years <sup>[N11 (2023)](#n11)</sup>. From 2017 to 2024, 702 workers went through IM Japan <sup>[N40](#n40)</sup>. Prothom Alo described the route in 2025 as a four-month Japanese course at a TTC, then a test, then four more months of training run by IM Japan before going as interns, with pay of Tk 1,24,500 to 1,57,700 a month in Japan <sup>[N13](#n13), [N10](#n10)</sup>.

**Onodera User Run.** On 10 April 2025 the Ministry signed an MoU with Onodera User Run Inc., a private Japanese company, under which Bangladeshi workers get free Japanese and technical training and those trained go to jobs in Japan without migration cost <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>. The training covers caregiving, industrial packaging, plastic moulding, rod binding, scaffolding, car painting, welding and car mechanics <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the IM Japan programme still recruiting in 2026, and how does a TTC graduate apply?
> - Has anyone gone to Japan under the Onodera User Run MoU, where is its training held, and how does a worker apply?

<a id="who-pays-what-on-titp"></a>

### 5.2 Who pays what on TITP

- **The supervision fee:** paid by the supervising and implementing organisations, never by the trainee or from wages <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **What you pay the sending organisation:** listed in the sending organisation's Statement of Expenses (Reference Form 2-10); before entry you sign Form 1-21, confirming you were charged nothing else <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Banned:** the sending organisation may not take guarantee money (a deposit) from you or your relatives, or make you sign a contract with penalties <sup>[17](#o17), [47](#o47)</sup>. Keeping your passport or residence card is a human-rights violation under the Technical Intern Training Act <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Keep receipts** for everything you pay, to prove overcharging later <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Bangladesh's 2019 rule:** the Ministry's 2019 guideline for technical interns set a zero migration cost policy: the sending organisation may not handle any money with interns or use sub-agents, and the commission and fees the Ministry sets are deposited in the intern's own bank account, from a PKB loan or cash, before BMET issues the smart card <sup>[N8 (2019)](#n8)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the Ministry's 2019 zero-cost TITP guideline still in force, what fee in taka does it set, and what do trainees really pay?

<a id="step-1-learn-japanese-and-choose-a-sending-organisation"></a>

### 5.3 Step 1: Learn Japanese and choose a sending organisation

1. <a id="step-1-learn-japanese-and-choose-a-sending-organisation-item-1"></a>**Japanese:** the Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo says trainees need at least 160 hours of Japanese or JLPT N5 (N4 for caregivers), are 18 or older (18 to 35 preferred), and usually need no formal education <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-1-learn-japanese-and-choose-a-sending-organisation-item-2"></a>**Check the sending organisation** on OTIT's list of approved sending organisations for Bangladesh, which gives each one's OTIT number, RL number, address and contact person <sup>[48](#o48), [89](#o89)</sup>. OTIT also lists organisations whose approval ended <sup>[89](#o89)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-1-learn-japanese-and-choose-a-sending-organisation-item-3"></a>**Watch for the state recruiter's calls.** BOESL, the state recruiting company, advertises its own intern recruitment, such as its September 2026 call for skilled women technical interns as nursing caregivers in Japan; its office and hotline are under Contacts (see [In Bangladesh](#in-bangladesh)) <sup>[90](#o90), [91 (2023)](#o91)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What did BOESL's September 2026 caregiver intern call require, what does BOESL charge, and how does a candidate apply?

<a id="step-2-selection-contract-and-training-plan"></a>

### 5.4 Step 2: Selection, contract and training plan

1. The supervising organisation and company select you and you sign an employment contract <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
2. The company's training plan is certified by OTIT; since 1 September 2025 Bangladeshi trainees no longer need a recommendation letter for it <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>.
3. Before leaving, check and sign Form 1-21 on the expenses you paid <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="step-3-coe-visa-and-leaving-bangladesh"></a>

### 5.5 Step 3: COE, visa and leaving Bangladesh

The COE, visa and Bangladesh-side steps are the same as for SSW: the company's staff apply for the COE (see [Step 7: The employer applies for your COE](#step-7-the-employer-applies-for-your-coe)), you apply for the visa at VFS ([Step 8, item 1: Apply for the visa at VFS Global](#step-8-the-visa-at-the-japanese-embassy-then-bmet-clearance-item-1)), and you do the PDO and BMET clearance ([Step 8: The visa at the Japanese Embassy, then BMET clearance](#step-8-the-visa-at-the-japanese-embassy-then-bmet-clearance), items 2 and 3) <sup>[59](#o59), [N48](#n48), [8](#o8)</sup>.

<a id="step-4-in-japan"></a>

### 5.6 Step 4: In Japan

1. <a id="step-4-in-japan-item-1"></a>**Lectures first.** TITP (i) starts with classes on Japanese, daily life, the relevant laws and skills, then training under the employment contract <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-4-in-japan-item-2"></a>**Tests to stay on.** To move to TITP (ii) you take the basic-level trade skills test <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-4-in-japan-item-3"></a>**Changing employer.** Only in unavoidable situations, or on moving from (ii) to (iii) (see [Technical intern trainees: changing training site](#technical-intern-trainees-changing-training-site)) <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.

<a id="what-changes-from-april-2027-employment-for-skill-development"></a>

### 5.7 What changes from April 2027: Employment for Skill Development

- **Start date:** ESD starts on 1 April 2027 and lasts up to 3 years, with an individual plan certified by the Organization for Employment for Skill Development of Foreign Nationals, which OTIT becomes in April 2027 <sup>[22](#o22), [92](#o92)</sup>.
- **Only through the MOC:** Japan in principle accepts ESD workers only from countries that have signed an ESD MOC, through their listed sending organisations <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>. Bangladesh's ESD MOC is dated 17 October 2026, and OTIT published a tentative list of Bangladeshi sending organisations (BMET and BOESL first) on 6 October 2026 <sup>[93](#o93), [94](#o94)</sup>. An organisation on the tentative list but not on the final list cannot be used <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>.
- **The Bangladesh MOC:** the Memorandum of Cooperation on Employment Skills Development (ESDP-MOC) was signed on 17 October 2026, for Bangladesh by the expatriates' ministry secretary and for Japan by representatives of its Ministry of Justice, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, MHLW and National Police Agency; it runs five years and renews automatically for five more, and the ministry said Japan will start recruiting Bangladeshi workers under it from 1 April 2027 <sup>[N26](#n26)</sup>. The expatriates' minister said it gives Bangladeshi workers the same legal protection as Japanese workers, that women cannot be dismissed or treated unfairly because of pregnancy or childbirth, and that it therefore has no mandatory forced-return provision <sup>[N26](#n26)</sup>. It sets no yearly number of workers <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>.
- **Fee cap:** everything you pay a sending organisation must not exceed 2 months' salary, meaning the scheduled monthly wage of the first year without overtime; Japan's aim is as close to zero as possible <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>. The sending organisation must publish how it calculates its fees online, and Japan checks the amount you declare against your salary and reports bad sending organisations to Bangladesh <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>.
- **Who may send:** the sending organisation must be recommended by the Bangladesh government, confirm your good conduct, and give no kickbacks or lavish entertainment to Japanese companies or supervising organisations <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>. The worker must be recommended by a public body in Bangladesh <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>.
- **Japanese:** pass a test at A1 (JFT-Basic at A1 or JLPT N5) or take an A1 course before starting work, then pass A1 after one year and A2.2 by the end <sup>[47](#o47), [67](#o67), [65](#o65)</sup>.
- **Skills:** pass the basic-grade trade skills test after one year, and trade skills grade 3 or the SSW (i) test by the end; anyone who fails at the end can stay up to 1 more year to retake it <sup>[47](#o47), [22](#o22)</sup>.
- **Changing employer by choice:** allowed after a period set for each field of 1 to 2 years, if you meet the skill and Japanese levels (see [The new rules from April 2027](#the-new-rules-from-april-2027)) <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>.
- **Your terms:** pay at least equal to a Japanese worker's, paid leave to visit home, proper housing, and no supervising fee passed on to you <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>.
- **Fields:** ESD covers the SSW fields except automobile transportation and aviation <sup>[22](#o22)</sup>.
- **Going straight to SSW:** you can still pass the tests in Bangladesh and go directly on SSW (i) without ESD <sup>[22](#o22)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has Bangladesh's ESD MOC been published in English, and does it set a lower fee cap than 2 months' salary?

<a id="titp-and-esd-scams"></a>

### 5.8 TITP and ESD scams

- **A sending organisation charging above the cap or taking a deposit.** Deposits and penalty contracts are banned under TITP, and from April 2027 fees above 2 months' salary disqualify the sending organisation <sup>[17](#o17), [47](#o47)</sup>. **Spot it:** compare what you are asked to pay with Form 1-21 and the fee scale it publishes, and pay only against a receipt <sup>[17](#o17), [47](#o47)</sup>.
- **A "sending organisation" not on OTIT's list.** Japan takes trainees only from accredited organisations <sup>[88](#o88)</sup>. **Spot it:** look up its OTIT number on OTIT's Bangladesh list <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>.
- **Kickbacks.** A supervising organisation that takes a kickback from a sending organisation loses its licence; report it to OTIT, and you will not be treated badly for doing so <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **A job-change broker in Japan.** Under ESD a private recruiter may not arrange your move <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>.

<a id="where-to-get-help-with-a-titp-or-esd-job"></a>

#### 5.8.1 Where to get help with a TITP or ESD job

- **Before you go:** complain about a sending organisation to BMET's Director General with an application and proof, free, within 30 working days, or to the Ministry's enforcement branch with proof and the agency's RL number <sup>[8](#o8), [86](#o86)</sup>.
- **In Japan:** OTIT's native-language line in English, 0120-250-147 (toll free), Tuesdays and Thursdays 11:00 am to 7:00 pm and Saturdays 9:00 am to 5:00 pm; press 1 for the emergency desk; there is no Bengali line <sup>[17](#o17), [15](#o15)</sup>. Report to OTIT if the work differs from the contract, someone takes your passport, you are forced to go home or you cannot leave your accommodation <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>. OTIT's general call centre: 03-3453-8000, weekdays <sup>[89](#o89)</sup>. Interns who came through IM Japan can use its Bangladesh mental-health programme, Vai-bon, which gives consultation in the interns' own language through a toll-free number <sup>[N51](#n51)</sup>. From April 2027, ESD questions go to OTIT's ESD call centre, 0570-011-300, weekdays 9:00 to 17:00 <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>.
- **The Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo:** the Counsellor (Labour), +81 3 3234 5801, fstokyo@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>.

<a id="living-and-working-in-japan-ssw-workers-and-technical-interns"></a>

## 6 Living and working in Japan (SSW workers and technical interns)

<a id="your-rights-at-work"></a>

### 6.1 Your rights at work

<a id="which-laws-protect-you"></a>

#### 6.1.1 Which laws protect you

The Labour Standards Act, Japan's main labour law, covers you as it covers a Japanese worker, whether you are a technical intern trainee or a Specified Skilled Worker (SSW, the work visa called "tokutei ginou") <sup>[17](#o17), [95](#o95)</sup>. A technical intern trainee ("ginou jisshusei", under the Technical Intern Training Programme, TITP) is also protected by the Technical Intern Training Act, which bans the employer and the supervising organisation (the "kanri dantai", the cooperative that brings trainees in and is meant to audit the employer) from specific abuses (see [Extra protections for technical intern trainees](#extra-protections-for-technical-intern-trainees)) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. An SSW worker is also protected by the Ministry of Justice order that sets what an SSW employment contract and support plan must contain, on top of the Labour Standards Act (see [What your SSW support organisation must do](#what-your-ssw-support-organisation-must-do)) <sup>[95](#o95)</sup>.

Your pay must be at least what a Japanese worker would get for the same work, under both systems <sup>[17](#o17), [7](#o7)</sup>. An SSW employer may not treat you worse than Japanese staff in pay, training or use of company facilities because you are a foreigner, and your scheduled hours must be the same as its regular workers' <sup>[95](#o95)</sup>.

<a id="your-contract"></a>

#### 6.1.2 Your contract

When you sign, the employer must give you your working conditions in writing: how long the contract runs, where you work, what work, start and finish times, overtime, breaks, holidays, leave, pay and how you can resign <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. Get your contract and the written conditions from the employer and keep them somewhere safe <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. If what you actually get differs from the contract, a trainee can report it (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid)), and an unfixed difference is grounds for a TITP transfer (see [Technical intern trainees: changing training site](#technical-intern-trainees-changing-training-site)) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="minimum-wage"></a>

#### 6.1.3 Minimum wage

Each prefecture (Japan's 47 regional governments) sets its own hourly minimum wage, and the employer must also pay any higher "specified" minimum wage set for an industry in that area <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. To compare a monthly wage, divide it by your average scheduled hours per month and check the result against the hourly minimum <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. The FY2026 rates, effective from the date shown, are:

- **Tokyo:** 1,280 yen from 1 October 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Kanagawa:** 1,279 yen from 1 October 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Osaka:** 1,231 yen from 1 October 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Saitama and Chiba:** 1,196 yen and 1,195 yen from 1 October 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Aichi:** 1,195 yen from 1 October 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Shizuoka:** 1,154 yen from 15 October 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Hiroshima:** 1,141 yen from 11 October 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Ibaraki:** 1,136 yen from 18 October 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Hokkaido:** 1,131 yen from 1 October 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Gunma:** 1,120 yen from 3 October 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Fukuoka:** 1,114 yen from 4 October 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Iwate and Kumamoto:** 1,031 yen and 1,034 yen until 30 November 2026, then 1,090 yen and 1,092 yen from 1 December 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Okinawa:** 1,023 yen until 1 December 2026, then 1,086 yen from 2 December 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Miyazaki (the lowest new rate):** 1,085 yen from 24 October 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.
- **National weighted average:** 1,177 yen, up from 1,121 yen <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.

You can check any prefecture at saiteichingin.mhlw.go.jp or at the nearest Labour Standards Inspection Office <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="hours-overtime-and-rest-days"></a>

#### 6.1.4 Hours, overtime and rest days

- **Normal hours:** no more than 8 hours a day and 40 a week <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Breaks:** at least 45 minutes if you work more than 6 hours, and 60 minutes if more than 8 hours <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Days off:** at least one a week, or 4 in every 4 weeks <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Overtime pay:** at least 25% extra beyond the legal hours, and at least 50% extra for overtime beyond 60 hours in a month <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Late-night work (10pm to 5am):** at least 25% extra; overtime that is also late at night is at least 50% extra <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Work on your legal day off:** at least 35% extra <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Agriculture and fisheries:** these jobs are exempt from the hours, breaks and days-off rules, but technical intern trainees in them must still be given the Labour Standards Act standards <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

Overtime is lawful only if the employer has an overtime agreement with the workers' representative or union and has filed it with the Labour Standards Inspection Office <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. Being told to punch your time card as if you did no overtime is a violation you can report <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="paid-leave-maternity-and-dismissal"></a>

#### 6.1.5 Paid leave, maternity and dismissal

- **Annual paid leave:** 10 days after 6 months if you worked at least 80% of your work days, then 11, 12, 14, 16, 18 and 20 days in each following year <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. The employer may not buy your leave from you instead of letting you take it <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Going home on leave (SSW):** the employer must let an SSW worker take the paid leave needed for a temporary trip home <sup>[95](#o95)</sup>.
- **Pregnancy:** dismissal or other bad treatment because of marriage, pregnancy or childbirth is banned, maternity leave can start 6 weeks before the due date, and you may not work for 8 weeks after the birth, except that after 6 weeks you may go back if you want to and a doctor agrees <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Dismissal:** the employer must give at least 30 days' notice or pay 30 days' average wages instead, and must give the reason in writing if you ask <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. It may not dismiss you while you are off work injured or ill, or for 30 days after, or because you reported it to the Labour Standards Inspection Office <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. A technical intern trainee may be dismissed mid-contract only for an unavoidable reason <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Days off caused by the employer:** if the employer stops your work for its own reasons, it must pay a leave allowance of at least 60% of your average wage <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="how-you-are-paid-and-what-may-be-deducted"></a>

#### 6.1.6 How you are paid, and what may be deducted

Wages must be paid in full, at least once a month, on a fixed day, directly to you; payment into your bank account needs your written agreement and a wage statement (payslip) every time <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. An SSW employer must pay into a bank account you choose, or in a way that proves how much you actually received <sup>[95](#o95)</sup>.

Deducted by law from your gross pay <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>:
- health insurance and employees' pension premiums (half each; the employer pays the other half) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
- employment insurance, if you are scheduled for 20 hours or more a week and expected to be employed for 31 days or more: 0.55% of wages in most work, 0.65% in agriculture, forestry, fisheries and construction (rates from 1 April 2025) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
- income tax and inhabitant (local) tax <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

Deducted only by agreement:
- housing and utilities, and only if there is a labour-management agreement made in advance, at actual cost or another reasonable amount <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

Never deducted:
- workers' accident insurance (rōsai), which the employer pays in full <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
- for trainees, the supervision fee the supervising organisation charges the employer <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

Banned outright: setting a loan from the employer against your wages, compulsory savings, and the employer or supervising organisation keeping your bankbook <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does company housing typically cost a Bangladeshi trainee or SSW worker per month in 2026 (rent and utilities), and how often is it above actual cost?

<a id="housing"></a>

#### 6.1.7 Housing

An SSW employer, or its support organisation, must help you find housing, for example by acting as guarantor on your lease or giving you company housing <sup>[7](#o7), [95](#o95)</sup>. Whatever you pay for housing and utilities must be agreed in advance, with full understanding of what you are getting, and must be actual cost or another appropriate amount <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. A trainee's accommodation rules may not stop you going out or ban your mobile phone: both are unlawful restrictions on your private life <sup>[17](#o17), [96 (2020)](#o96)</sup>.

<a id="social-insurance-pension-and-tax"></a>

#### 6.1.8 Social insurance, pension and tax

Joining social insurance is compulsory <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. At work you are enrolled in Health Insurance and Employees' Pension, and the employer handles the enrolment; trainees in the post-entry training period (the classes after arrival) instead enrol themselves in National Health Insurance and National Pension at their city or town office <sup>[17](#o17), [3](#o3)</sup>. Health insurance pays 70% of medical bills and you pay 30%, but treatment for an injury at work or on the way to work is paid in full by workers' accident insurance <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. The employees' pension rate is 18.3% of your standard monthly pay, split half and half with the employer, and the National Pension premium was 17,510 yen a month in 2025 <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. Pension contributions can be partly refunded when you leave (see [The lump-sum pension refund](#the-lump-sum-pension-refund)) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

Inhabitant tax is charged by the city where you lived on 1 January, on last year's income, and is taken from your pay from June to the next May <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. Unpaid tax or insurance can lead to your visa extension or change of status being refused <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. You may be able to cut your income tax by claiming dependants who live in Bangladesh <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. Questions about filing pension enrolment online (through MynaPortal, the government's online portal) can go to the Nenkin Kanyusha Dial on 0570-003-004 (03-6630-2525 from a phone number starting 050), which also takes calls in other languages <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="your-passport-and-residence-card"></a>

#### 6.1.9 Your passport and residence card

No one may keep your passport or residence card. For trainees, the Technical Intern Training Act bans the employer and supervising organisation from holding either, and keeping them against your wishes is punishable by up to 6 months in prison or a fine of up to 300,000 yen <sup>[96 (2020)](#o96)</sup>. For SSW workers, an employer that has taken a foreign worker's passport or residence card in the last 5 years is barred from hiring SSW workers <sup>[95](#o95)</sup>. You must carry your residence card yourself and never leave it with the employer or supervising organisation <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. If you are told to hand over your passport, call OTIT (the Organization for Technical Intern Training, which inspects employers and helps trainees; see [Help organisations](#help-organisations)) <sup>[15](#o15), [17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="extra-protections-for-technical-intern-trainees"></a>

#### 6.1.10 Extra protections for technical intern trainees

Under the Technical Intern Training Act, the employer and the supervising organisation may not <sup>[96 (2020)](#o96)</sup>:
- force you to work by violence, threats or confinement, punishable by 1 to 10 years in prison or a fine of 200,000 to 3,000,000 yen <sup>[96 (2020)](#o96)</sup>;
- make you or your family sign a penalty contract, for example a fine if you leave early <sup>[96 (2020)](#o96)</sup>;
- make you sign a savings contract or take control of your savings <sup>[96 (2020)](#o96)</sup>;
- keep your passport or residence card <sup>[96 (2020)](#o96)</sup>;
- stop you going out or otherwise restrict your private life <sup>[96 (2020)](#o96)</sup>.

The Bangladeshi sending agency also may not take "guarantee money" from you or your relatives to stop you running away, and you should keep receipts for everything you paid before you left: you sign a Statement of Expenses (Reference Form No. 1-21) listing them, and the receipts prove any overcharge later <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. A supervising organisation that takes a kickback from a sending agency loses its licence <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. The supervising organisation must in principle pay your full fare home when the training ends <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. You may report any violation of the Act to the Commissioner of the Immigration Services Agency (ISA) and the Minister of Health, Labour and Welfare, including through OTIT, and it is illegal to punish you for reporting <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="what-your-ssw-support-organisation-must-do"></a>

#### 6.1.11 What your SSW support organisation must do

An employer of an SSW (i) worker (the first SSW level) must have a support plan for you, and may carry it out itself or contract all or part of it to another party, such as a registered support organisation (a "touroku shien kikan", registered with ISA and listed on ISA's website) <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. There is no support duty for SSW (ii) <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. The plan must cover 10 things <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>:
1. Guidance before you come, in person or by video call, on your conditions, work and immigration steps, and on whether any deposit was taken from you <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
2. Pick-up at the airport when you arrive and drop-off when you leave <sup>[7](#o7), [95](#o95)</sup>.
3. Help with housing (acting as guarantor or providing company housing) and with opening a bank account and getting a phone and utilities <sup>[7](#o7), [95](#o95)</sup>.
4. A life orientation on Japanese rules, public services and disasters <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
5. Going with you to the city office for residence, social security and tax registration, if needed <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
6. Information on Japanese classes <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
7. Handling your questions and complaints about work and life, without delay, in a language you fully understand <sup>[7](#o7), [95](#o95)</sup>.
8. Help to take part in local events and meet Japanese people <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.
9. If the employer ends your contract for its own reasons, help to find a new job: an introduction to Hello Work (the public job centre), a recommendation letter and paid leave for job hunting <sup>[7](#o7), [95](#o95)</sup>.
10. Interviews with you and your supervisor at least once every three months, and reporting any labour law violation to the Labour Standards Inspection Office <sup>[7](#o7), [95](#o95)</sup>.

A registered support organisation may not hand the work on to anyone else <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. Registered support organisations are listed in a public registry on ISA's website; there were 11,456 at the end of June 2026 <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. If you are not getting the support, contact the nearest immigration office <sup>[58](#o58)</sup>. An employer may not hire SSW workers if it took a deposit from you or made you sign a contract with a penalty for leaving <sup>[7](#o7), [95](#o95)</sup>. An employer that fails to give support can be refused permission to take foreign workers and be given guidance and improvement orders by ISA, and missing or false reports are punishable; a registered support organisation that fails to give support can lose its registration <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What monthly support fee do employers pay registered support organisations for a Bangladeshi SSW worker, and is any of it passed on to the worker?
> - Which support organisations have Bengali-speaking staff?

<a id="your-residence-card-and-stay"></a>

### 6.2 Your residence card and stay

<a id="how-long-you-can-stay"></a>

#### 6.2.1 How long you can stay

- **SSW (i):** each grant is for up to 3 years, set individually, and your total time in SSW (i) is limited to 5 years (6 if there are reasonable grounds), not counting time off for pregnancy, childbirth or childcare <sup>[7](#o7), [34](#o34)</sup>. SSW (ii) has no total limit <sup>[22](#o22)</sup>.
- **Technical intern trainee:** 3 years for TITP (i) and (ii), and up to 5 years away from home if you go on to TITP (iii) in the 4th and 5th years <sup>[97](#o97), [17](#o17)</sup>; the new Employment for Skill Development (ESD) programme that replaces it from April 2027 is also 3 years <sup>[22](#o22), [92](#o92)</sup>.

You can move from TITP to SSW without going home <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>.

<a id="renewing"></a>

#### 6.2.2 Renewing

Apply for an extension at your regional immigration bureau, or online, from 3 months before your current period ends if you were given 6 months or more <sup>[98](#o98)</sup>. ISA usually decides in 2 weeks to 1 month <sup>[98](#o98)</sup>. At the counter, the fee depends on the period granted: 25,000 yen for over 6 months and under 1 year, 33,000 yen for 1 year, 48,000 yen for over 1 year and under 3 years, and 64,000 yen for 3 years or more and under 5 years; some people qualify for a reduced 10,000 yen <sup>[98](#o98)</sup>. Applications received by 30 September 2026 pay the old 6,000 yen, even if granted later <sup>[98](#o98)</sup>. Unpaid taxes or social insurance can lead to refusal <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Who pays the new 33,000 to 64,000 yen renewal fee for SSW workers in practice: the employer or the worker?

<a id="going-home-on-leave-re-entry"></a>

#### 6.2.3 Going home on leave: re-entry

If you hold a residence card and will be back within 1 year, you do not need a re-entry permit: you leave on the "special re-entry permit" (minashi sainyūkoku kyoka) <sup>[99](#o99)</sup>. For a longer absence, get a re-entry permit at the regional immigration bureau for where you live before you leave: 4,000 yen for one trip or 7,000 yen for multiple trips, issued the same day, and valid no longer than your period of stay <sup>[99](#o99)</sup>. Ask your SSW employer for paid leave for the trip (see ["Paid leave, maternity and dismissal"](#paid-leave-maternity-and-dismissal)) <sup>[95](#o95)</sup>.

**Flights.** Biman Bangladesh Airlines restarted direct Dhaka-Tokyo flights in September 2023 after 17 years, at three a week and later two; a Japan-based Bangladeshi journalist wrote in July 2025 that the route was being suspended indefinitely in the first week of July 2025, the third time Biman had stopped it <sup>[N52](#n52)</sup>. He wrote that the direct flight took six to seven hours with no transit and that Biman allowed more baggage than other airlines <sup>[N52](#n52)</sup>. Prothom Alo reported in May 2026 that Biman was moving to restart direct Dhaka-Tokyo flights in June 2026, and in October 2026 the Prime Minister flew to Japan on a regular Biman flight that landed at Narita International Airport <sup>[N53](#n53), [N27](#n27)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many direct Biman Dhaka-Narita flights a week run in late 2026, and which connecting routes do Bangladeshi workers in Japan mostly use?

<a id="moving-house"></a>

#### 6.2.4 Moving house

Report your new address within 14 days of moving <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>. Taking your residence card to your new city or town office and filing the move-in notice there counts as the immigration report, and it is free <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>. If you leave your registered address and don't report a new one within 90 days, your status can be revoked <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>.

<a id="if-your-job-ends-the-14-day-report-and-the-3-month-rule"></a>

#### 6.2.5 If your job ends: the 14-day report and the 3-month rule

An SSW worker must report to ISA within 14 days when the contract with the employer ends and again within 14 days of signing a new one <sup>[102](#o102), [3](#o3)</sup>. File it online through ISA's e-notification system at any hour (no documents needed), in person at a regional immigration office showing your residence card, or by tracked post with a copy of the card, marked "NOTIFICATION ENCLOSED", to the Tokyo Regional Immigration Services Bureau, Yotsuya Tower 14F, 1-6-1 Yotsuya, Shinjuku-ku, Tokyo 160-0004 <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. ISA does not confirm postal reports, so keep the tracking record <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>.

ISA can revoke your status if you have not done the work your visa is for for 3 months or more without good reason <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>. Before revoking, an immigration officer hears you, and you can give your side and evidence; you may then be given up to 30 days to leave <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>. Of 1,184 revocations in 2024, 710 were technical intern trainees, and 761 were for the 3-month rule <sup>[103](#o103)</sup>.

<a id="bank-account-and-phone"></a>

#### 6.2.6 Bank account and phone

Your wages go into a bank account, and your employer or support organisation helps you open it and get a phone contract ([Step 9, item 3: First weeks](#step-9-arrival-residence-card-and-your-support-organisation-item-3)) <sup>[7](#o7)</sup>. When you open the account the bank checks your name, address, period of stay, status of residence and employment, without exception, and it may refuse or later block the account if it cannot verify them <sup>[104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>. Tell the bank if your address, period or status of residence changes or you quit your job <sup>[104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>. Never sell or lend the account or the phone (see [Selling or lending your bank account or phone](#selling-or-lending-your-bank-account-or-phone)), and close both before you go home <sup>[17](#o17), [104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which banks open accounts for workers in their first months in Japan, and does any refuse workers with less than 6 months of stay?
> - Which mobile carriers give a newly arrived worker a SIM or phone contract on a residence card alone, and what does a basic plan cost a month in 2026?

<a id="the-residence-card-itself"></a>

#### 6.2.7 The residence card itself

A new card design started in June 2026, and you can now get a single card combining it with your My Number card (Japan's ID number card) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. If you lose it, go to the nearest regional immigration bureau quickly <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported"></a>

### 6.3 What can get you jailed or deported

<a id="overstay-and-unauthorised-work"></a>

#### 6.3.1 Overstay and unauthorised work

- **Overstaying:** up to 3 years in prison, a fine of up to 3 million yen, or both <sup>[17](#o17), [105](#o105)</sup>.
- **Working outside your status:** up to 1 year, 2 million yen or both, or up to 3 years, 3 million yen or both if that work is clearly all you do <sup>[17](#o17), [105](#o105)</sup>.
- **Side jobs:** a technical intern trainee may not do any other work and cannot get permission for it; an SSW worker may not work part-time either <sup>[17](#o17), [3](#o3)</sup>.
- **Anyone who gets a foreigner into illegal work:** up to 3 years, 3 million yen or both <sup>[17](#o17), [105](#o105)</sup>.

Surrendering does not make an overstay legal or let you work <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>. An overstayer who comes forward and meets the conditions can leave under a "departure order" without detention and with a shorter ban (see [Step 2: Surrender to immigration](#step-2-surrender-to-immigration)) <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.

<a id="selling-or-lending-your-bank-account-or-phone"></a>

#### 6.3.2 Selling or lending your bank account or phone

Crime groups recruit foreign workers through social-media job ads and friends, offering "part-time work" that is easy and well paid <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. The jobs are <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>:
- selling or giving away your bankbook, cash card, or account ID and password;
- giving your mobile phone to someone else;
- collecting cash or cards from scam victims;
- withdrawing cash from someone else's account with their card;
- receiving parcels addressed to someone else.

Some technical intern trainees have sold accounts or withdrawn money for crime groups <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>. An amendment to the Act on Prevention of Transfer of Criminal Proceeds, promulgated on 10 June 2026, raised the penalties from 10 July 2026: buying, selling, giving away or recruiting for bank accounts is now punishable by up to 3 years in prison, a fine of up to 5 million yen, or both, up from 1 year and 1 million yen <sup>[108](#o108), [109](#o109), [110](#o110)</sup>. From the same date, moving money through your own account for someone else for a reward (a "remittance crime", such as "I'll pay you 10,000 yen to send this through your account") is a separate offence: up to 2 years, 3 million yen or both, and asking or recruiting someone to do it is also a crime <sup>[109](#o109), [111](#o111), [104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>. Anyone who sells an account may be unable to open or use an account at any bank <sup>[110](#o110)</sup>. You can be punished and deported even if you were only used by the gang, and handing over your account can stop you staying in or re-entering Japan <sup>[17](#o17), [104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>. Close your bank account and phone contract before you go home <sup>[17](#o17), [104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>. Send money only through banks or transfer services licensed by the FSA (Financial Services Agency); unlicensed ones are illegal <sup>[104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>.

<a id="drugs-gambling-and-other-crimes"></a>

#### 6.3.3 Drugs, gambling and other crimes

- **Drugs:** anyone convicted of a drug offence (narcotics, cannabis, opium or stimulants) is barred from Japan for life <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.
- **Any sentence of 1 year or more:** also a lifetime ban <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.
- **Gambling:** illegal in Japan, including online, even if it is legal at home <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="runaway-trainees"></a>

#### 6.3.4 "Runaway" trainees

Leaving your training site and working somewhere else is work outside your status: it can mean deportation or a criminal charge <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. OTIT warns trainees not to be tempted by friends or online contacts promising more money <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. If your employer abuses you, the lawful way out is a transfer (see [Technical intern trainees: changing training site](#technical-intern-trainees-changing-training-site)) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="bans-on-coming-back"></a>

#### 6.3.5 Bans on coming back

A first deportation bars you from Japan for 5 years and a second for 10; leaving under a departure order bars you for 1 year in most cases; a drug conviction or a prison sentence of 1 year or more bars you for life <sup>[106](#o106), [112](#o112)</sup>. For the full rules, including how a ban can be shortened, see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers).

> **Need to know**
> - How many Bangladeshi technical intern trainees and SSW workers were reported missing ("shissō") in 2024 and 2025?

<a id="if-something-goes-wrong"></a>

### 6.4 If something goes wrong

<a id="if-you-arent-paid"></a>

#### 6.4.1 If you aren't paid

You'll need:
- your contract and written working conditions <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
- payslips and bank statements <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
- your own record of the hours you worked, especially if you were told to falsify your time card <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

1. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-1"></a>**Report to the Labour Standards Inspection Office (LSIO, "rōdō kijun kantokusho").** It takes reports of unpaid wages, unpaid overtime, falsified time cards, missing breaks and refused leave, and the employer may not dismiss or punish you for reporting <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. Some LSIOs and prefectural labour bureaus have a foreign-worker desk; at others, bring someone who can interpret <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-2"></a>**If you need an interpreter, call first.** The MHLW (Ministry of Health, Labour and Welfare) Telephone Consultation Service for Foreign Workers has an English line, 0570-001701, open weekdays 10am to 3pm (closed noon to 1pm), costing 8.5 yen per 3 minutes from a landline and 10 yen per 20 seconds from a mobile <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. The Multilingual Contact Center, 0570-00-8609, weekdays 8.30am to 5.15pm, connects you through an interpreter to one of the 379 General Labour Consultation Corners <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. Neither offers Bengali; the languages include English <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-3"></a>**If you are a trainee, also report to OTIT.** Unpaid overtime is one of OTIT's listed problems, and non-payment of wages is grounds to transfer to a new employer (see [Technical intern trainees: changing training site](#technical-intern-trainees-changing-training-site)) <sup>[17](#o17), [15](#o15)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-4"></a>**If the company has gone bankrupt,** the government pays part of the unpaid wages through JOHAS (the Japan Organization of Occupational Health and Safety) under the Act on Ensuring Wage Payment <sup>[17](#o17), [113](#o113)</sup>. Bankruptcy proceedings must be filed with a court, or an application for the LSIO's certification of insolvency made, within 6 months of your leaving, or you cannot be paid, so go to the LSIO at once <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>. JOHAS's advance-payment desk is on 044-431-8663, weekdays 9.15am to 5pm, by phone or in person only <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>. In FY2024 it paid about 11 billion yen to 30,591 people <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does an LSIO actually do for a foreign worker's unpaid-wage report in practice (inspection, order to pay), and how long does it take?
> - How long after leaving can a worker still claim unpaid wages (the 3-year limit), and can he claim from Bangladesh?

<a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work"></a>

#### 6.4.2 If you are injured at work

Workers' accident compensation insurance ("rōsai hoken") covers injury, illness, disability or death from work or from commuting, and every workplace with even one worker must have it <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. Some small farms, forests and fisheries are exempt, but then the employer must buy private insurance for you <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

1. <a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work-item-1"></a>**Get treated at a rōsai-designated hospital or clinic.** Hand in the rōsai claim form on the day of treatment and you pay nothing; find designated clinics at rousai-kensaku.mhlw.go.jp <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. Do not use your health insurance card for a work injury <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work-item-2"></a>**At a non-designated clinic,** you pay the full cost and claim it back from the LSIO, which pays into your bank account <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work-item-3"></a>**For days off work,** the employer gives paid leave for days 1 to 3, and rōsai pays 60% of your basic daily benefit amount from the fourth day; claim it from the LSIO <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work-item-4"></a>**Wait for the decision,** which usually takes about a month <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
5. <a id="if-you-are-injured-at-work-item-5"></a>**If the injury is not cured 1 year and 6 months after treatment began,** rōsai pays a pension; if it leaves a disability, a pension or lump sum by grade; if you die, your family receives a pension or lump sum and funeral costs <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

The employer may not dismiss you while you are off with the injury or for 30 days after <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. POSSE, a labour NGO, helps foreign workers file rōsai claims (see [Help organisations](#help-organisations)) <sup>[N54](#n54)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do employers in practice pressure Bangladeshi trainees not to file rōsai claims, and does OTIT or the LSIO intervene?

<a id="if-you-are-harassed-hit-or-your-passport-is-taken"></a>

#### 6.4.3 If you are harassed, hit or your passport is taken

- **Violence, threats, sexual harassment, being told you'll be sent home, passport taken, not allowed out (trainees):** call OTIT's English line, 0120-250-147, press 1 after the announcement for the emergency (SOS) operator; it is free <sup>[17](#o17), [15](#o15), [16](#o16)</sup>. Violence and harassment are grounds to change training site <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Power harassment (bullying by a boss) and sexual harassment:** employers have had to take measures against power harassment since 1 June 2020, under an MHLW guideline; if the company does not deal with it, go to the prefectural labour bureau's Employment Environment and Equal Employment Department or a General Labour Consultation Corner, free and anonymously if you wish <sup>[114](#o114)</sup>. Being forced to buy the company's products can count as power harassment <sup>[114](#o114)</sup>. Since 1 October 2026 every employer must also protect staff from harassment by customers <sup>[114](#o114)</sup>.
- **Discrimination, bullying or harassment as a human-rights issue:** the Ministry of Justice's Foreign-language Human Rights Hotline, 0570-090911, weekdays 9am to 5pm <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Pregnancy-related bad treatment:** the prefectural labour bureau, or OTIT for trainees <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-are-being-forced-to-go-home"></a>

#### 6.4.4 If you are being forced to go home

Neither a supervising organisation nor an employer may send a trainee home against his will <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. Call OTIT <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. If you are taken to the airport, tell the immigration inspector at departure that you are being forced home; inspectors ask trainees about this <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="help-organisations"></a>

#### 6.4.5 Help organisations

- **OTIT (Organization for Technical Intern Training):** the public body under the Ministry of Justice and MHLW, set up under the Technical Intern Training Act, that inspects employers and supervising organisations and helps trainees <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. It has no Bengali line; Bangladeshi trainees use the English line, 0120-250-147, Tuesday and Thursday 11am to 7pm and Saturday 9am to 5pm, free, with an answering machine outside hours, closed on public holidays and 29 December to 3 January <sup>[15](#o15), [17](#o17), [16](#o16)</sup>. Its web form (support.otit.go.jp/soudan/en) takes messages at any hour, answered on the next line day; letters go to the Assistance Division, OTIT, 3F LOOP-X Building, 3-9-15 Kaigan, Minato-ku, Tokyo 108-0022 <sup>[15](#o15)</sup>. Regional offices see people on weekdays 9am to 5pm and can arrange an interpreter <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. From April 2027 OTIT becomes the Employment for Skill Development Organization (ESDO) <sup>[92](#o92)</sup>.
- **FRESC (Foreign Residents Support Center):** eight government bodies in one building, including the Tokyo immigration bureau, the Tokyo Labour Bureau's foreign-worker desk and Hou Terrasu (legal aid) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>, at Yotsuya Tower 13F, 1-6-1 Yotsuya, Shinjuku-ku, Tokyo; 0570-011000, weekdays 9am to 5pm <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Immigration Services Agency (ISA):** Foreign Residents Information Center, 0570-013904 (03-5796-7112 from IP phones or abroad), weekdays 8.30am to 5.15pm, in Japanese, English and other languages <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. SSW workers can also call ISA's call centre for SSW, on the same number, for help with SSW procedures and paperwork <sup>[3](#o3), [87](#o87)</sup>.
- **Hou Terrasu (Japan Legal Support Center):** multilingual legal information, 0570-078377 <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Prefectural multilingual centres,** such as the Ibaraki Foreigner Consultation Center, Gunma's one-stop centre for foreigners and Tokyo Multilingual Consultation Navi <sup>[N55](#n55)</sup>.
- **POSSE (a non-profit for young and foreign workers):** free advice by email in any language (web form, or soudan@npoposse.jp), and by phone (042-227-5495; 022-302-3349 for the Tohoku region) on Fridays 6pm to 9pm and Sundays and holidays 1pm to 5pm, with an interpreter not always available; it helps with visa, rōsai and unemployment claims <sup>[N54](#n54)</sup>. Don't contact it from a work phone or computer <sup>[N54](#n54)</sup>.
- **Bangladesh Embassy, Tokyo, Labour Welfare Wing:** 3-29 Kioicho, Chiyoda-ku, Tokyo 102-0094; main line 03-3234-5801; consular hours Monday to Friday except holidays <sup>[115](#o115), [116 (2024)](#o116)</sup>. The labour wing's First Secretary (Labour) is at fslabor@mofa.gov.bd <sup>[117](#o117)</sup>. The embassy's 24-hour emergency numbers are under Contacts (see [In Japan](#in-japan)) <sup>[118](#o118), [115](#o115)</sup>. The wing says it gives aid to stranded workers to go home, visits workplaces, hospitals and detention centres, and gives legal aid for unpaid wages <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>. The embassy serves about 40,045 Bangladeshis in Japan (June 2025) <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the labour wing still on extension 201 of 03-3234-5801 <sup>[115](#o115)</sup>?
> - What has the labour wing actually done in recent cases of unpaid or abused Bangladeshi workers in Japan?
> - Is there a Bengali-speaking union or NGO in Japan that takes migrant workers' cases?

<a id="changing-employer-or-leaving"></a>

### 6.5 Changing employer or leaving

<a id="ssw-changing-employer"></a>

#### 6.5.1 SSW: changing employer

An SSW worker may quit and join another company in the same field without going home, in the same job category or one ISA has confirmed shares the same skills; to move to a different field you must first pass that field's skills test <sup>[3](#o3), [7](#o7)</sup>. Your SSW status covers work under a contract with an employer designated by the Minister of Justice <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>.

1. <a id="ssw-changing-employer-item-1"></a>**Find the new job** through SSW job fairs or Hello Work (Japan's public job centre) <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. If your employer is letting you go for its own reasons, its support organisation must help, including a recommendation letter and paid time off to job-hunt <sup>[7](#o7), [95](#o95)</sup>.
2. <a id="ssw-changing-employer-item-2"></a>**Report the end of your old contract to ISA within 14 days** (see [If your job ends: the 14-day report and the 3-month rule](#if-your-job-ends-the-14-day-report-and-the-3-month-rule)) <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>.
3. <a id="ssw-changing-employer-item-3"></a>**Apply for a change of status of residence for the new employer, and wait for it before you start:** working for a different SSW employer needs this permission, not just a report, and starting at the new company without it is illegal work <sup>[34](#o34), [119](#o119), [64](#o64)</sup>. Ask the regional immigration bureau or the Foreign Residents Information Center on 0570-013904 what to file <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
4. <a id="ssw-changing-employer-item-4"></a>**Report the new contract to ISA within 14 days** <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>.
5. <a id="ssw-changing-employer-item-5"></a>**Don't stay jobless for 3 months**, or your status can be revoked <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>.

You may not work part-time while you look <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - When an SSW worker changes employer, who files the change-of-status application (the worker or the new employer), and how long does it take?
> - What do brokers charge Bangladeshi SSW workers in Japan to find a transfer job?

<a id="technical-intern-trainees-changing-training-site"></a>

#### 6.5.2 Technical intern trainees: changing training site

A trainee normally cannot change employer, but can for "unavoidable circumstances", or when moving from TITP (ii) to (iii) <sup>[17](#o17), [7](#o7)</sup>. If your rights are violated, OTIT can help you change training site <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. The unavoidable circumstances include:
- the employer goes bankrupt, closes or cuts staff <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
- serious violations such as unpaid wages <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
- violence or harassment <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>;
- conditions far worse than the contract, not fixed after you asked <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

1. <a id="technical-intern-trainees-changing-training-site-item-1"></a>**Ask the supervising organisation** using the Written Application for Change in the Training Site (Reference Form No. 1-44) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
2. <a id="technical-intern-trainees-changing-training-site-item-2"></a>**If it refuses or ignores you, go to OTIT,** which will help directly <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
3. <a id="technical-intern-trainees-changing-training-site-item-3"></a>**While you wait,** if you need money to live on, you may work up to 28 hours a week after completing the procedure at the regional immigration bureau, and if you cannot stay in your accommodation, OTIT can give temporary accommodation <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

A trainee who went home because of illness, injury, family reasons or pregnancy can ask to resume the training <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="the-new-rules-from-april-2027"></a>

#### 6.5.3 The new rules from April 2027

On 1 April 2027 the Technical Intern Training Programme ends and the Employment for Skill Development (ESD, "ikusei shūrō") programme replaces it <sup>[92](#o92)</sup>. Under ESD you may change employer by your own choice once you have worked a set period of 1 to 2 years (set for each field) and reached a set level of skill and Japanese; A2.1 on JFT-Basic is the Japanese level mainly used for this <sup>[47](#o47), [22](#o22), [67](#o67)</sup>. Where the period is over a year, the employer must raise your pay or improve your treatment <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>. A worker whose ESD period has been extended beyond 3 years cannot move by choice <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>. The move must be arranged by the supervising and support organisation, ESDO or Hello Work, not a private broker <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>. The new employer must be rated "excellent" at developing skills and Japanese, may not have more than a third of its ESD workers from such transfers (and in big-city areas no more than a sixth from rural areas), and pays your old employer part of a recruiting and training cost set by the ministers: 5/6 if you move within 18 months, 2/3 at 18 months to 2 years, 1/2 at 2 to 2.5 years and 1/4 after <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>. ESD runs 3 years and leads to SSW (i); if you fail the SSW (i) test you may stay up to 1 more year to retake it <sup>[22](#o22)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Under ESD, must a worker's own-choice transfer stay within the same job category ("gyōmu kubun") of the same field?

<a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job"></a>

#### 6.5.4 Leaving at the end of the job

1. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-1"></a>**Get your final pay and any unpaid inhabitant tax settled.** Remaining inhabitant tax is either taken from your last pay or paid by you to the city; otherwise appoint a tax agent (someone living in Japan) and tell the city before you go <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
2. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-2"></a>**File the move-out notice at your city office** before you leave; this also proves for the pension refund that you no longer live in Japan <sup>[17](#o17), [97](#o97)</sup>.
3. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-3"></a>**Close your bank account and phone** <sup>[17](#o17), [104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>.
4. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-4"></a>**Get your fare paid.** For trainees, the supervising organisation pays the full fare home; tell OTIT if you are asked to pay <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. An SSW employer must pay your fare if you cannot, and its support organisation must take you to the airport <sup>[95](#o95)</sup>.
5. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-5"></a>**Claim your pension refund** within 2 years (see [The lump-sum pension refund](#the-lump-sum-pension-refund)) <sup>[120](#o120)</sup>.

<a id="the-lump-sum-pension-refund"></a>

#### 6.5.5 The lump-sum pension refund

A foreign worker who paid into Japan's pension for at least 6 months, and less than 10 years, can claim back part of it after leaving: the "dattai ichijikin" (lump-sum withdrawal payment) <sup>[17](#o17), [97](#o97)</sup>. The rules:
- **Deadline:** claim within 2 years of leaving Japan (of ceasing to have a Japanese address) <sup>[120](#o120)</sup>.
- **Cap:** the refund counts at most 60 months (5 years) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. If you were enrolled for longer, you get 60 months' worth and lose all the months, so someone who did 3 years of TITP then 5 years of SSW without a break loses 3 years <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>. To be paid for both, you must leave Japan and claim after each stay <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>. Taking the refund forfeits all your Japanese pension coverage up to the claim <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **Employees' pension amount:** your average standard monthly pay times a rate: 3.3 for 36 to 41 months and 5.5 for 60 months or more <sup>[120](#o120)</sup>.
- **National pension amount:** if your last premium was paid between April 2026 and March 2027, 322,560 yen for 36 to 41 months and 537,600 yen for 60 months or more; unpaid months don't count <sup>[120](#o120)</sup>.
- **Currency and bank:** payments to Bangladesh are made in US dollars, and Japan Post Bank cannot receive the payment <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **If the worker dies:** close relatives can receive it only if the claim was sent before the death <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **Tax:** 20.42% income tax is withheld from the employees' pension refund, but not the national pension refund <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>. You can get it back by filing a "Tax Return for Refund Due to Taxation on Retirement Income at the Taxpayer's Option" through a tax agent, anyone with a Japanese address, whom you name to the tax office before leaving, or with the return if you did not <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>:
- the Claim for Lump-sum Withdrawal Payment form <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>;
- a copy of your passport's photo page and status page;
- a bank certificate showing an account in your own name;
- your Basic Pension Number Notice or Pension Handbook;
- proof you no longer live in Japan, unless you filed the move-out notice.

1. <a id="the-lump-sum-pension-refund-item-1"></a>**Claim before you leave, or from Bangladesh.** You can hand it in before departure, after filing the move-out notice, as long as it reaches the Japan Pension Service after your residence record ends <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>. Otherwise post it to the Japan Pension Service, 3-5-24 Takaido-nishi, Suginami-ku, Tokyo 168-8505, Japan <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
2. <a id="the-lump-sum-pension-refund-item-2"></a>**Send the original payment notice to your tax agent,** who files the tax refund for you <sup>[97](#o97)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How much do agents or administrative scriveners in Japan or Dhaka charge Bangladeshi workers to claim the pension refund and tax refund, and how long does payment to a Bangladeshi bank take?

<a id="tricks-at-the-end-of-the-job-and-during-it"></a>

#### 6.5.6 Tricks at the end of the job, and during it

- **Being sent home early:** an employer or supervising organisation that wants rid of a trainee may try to put him on a plane; it is banned, and you can tell the immigration inspector at the airport <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Passport "for safe keeping":** banned for trainees and grounds to bar an SSW employer (see [Your passport and residence card](#your-passport-and-residence-card)) <sup>[96 (2020)](#o96), [95](#o95)</sup>.
- **Penalty contracts and deposits:** fines for leaving early, or "guarantee money" taken from you or your family, are illegal for trainees and bar an SSW employer <sup>[96 (2020)](#o96), [17](#o17), [95](#o95)</sup>.
- **Deductions you never agreed:** housing above cost, the supervision fee, or rōsai premiums taken from your pay <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Unpaid overtime hidden by a falsified time card:** report it to the LSIO <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **"Easy part-time jobs" on social media:** selling your account or moving money for a reward is now punished more heavily (see [Selling or lending your bank account or phone](#selling-or-lending-your-bank-account-or-phone)) <sup>[110](#o110), [111](#o111)</sup>.
- **Leaving without a move-out notice or tax agent:** you then have to prove separately that you left Japan for the pension refund, and unpaid inhabitant tax can attract late charges <sup>[97](#o97), [17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="skilled-and-professional-visas"></a>

## 7 Skilled and professional visas

A Bangladeshi graduate goes to Japan as a professional on the status of residence (the legal category printed on the residence card) called Engineer/Specialist in Humanities/International Services, known in Japan as "gijinkoku"; a points-based upgrade, Highly Skilled Professional, gives longer stays and faster permanent residence <sup>[122](#o122), [123](#o123), [124](#o124), [125](#o125)</sup>.

<a id="engineerspecialist-in-humanitiesinternational-services"></a>

### 7.1 Engineer/Specialist in Humanities/International Services

This status covers work under a contract with a Japanese organisation that needs technical or specialist knowledge in the natural sciences (engineering, IT) or the humanities (law, economics, business), or thinking rooted in a foreign culture <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>. Typical jobs are engineers, interpreters, designers, language teachers at private companies and marketing staff <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>. It is granted for 5 years, 3 years, 1 year or 3 months at a time, and can be renewed <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>.

<a id="who-qualifies-for-engineerspecialist"></a>

#### 7.1.1 Who qualifies for Engineer/Specialist

You must meet one of these, for the job you will do <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>:
- **A degree:** a university degree (or equivalent education) in a subject related to the job <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>.
- **A Japanese diploma:** a related course completed at a Japanese senmon gakko (specialised training college) <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>.
- **Experience:** 10 years or more of practical experience in the field, counting years spent studying it <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>.
- **An IT exam (IT jobs only):** a pass in an IT exam or qualification listed in the Minister of Justice's notice, which replaces the degree or experience requirement <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>. Bangladesh's ITEE (Information Technology Engineers Examination), run by the Bangladesh Computer Council (BCC) under a 2014 agreement with Japan's IPA (Information-technology Promotion Agency), is on the list, but only two levels count: the Fundamental Information Technology Engineer exam (FE) and the Applied Information Technology Engineer exam (AP); the third level the 2014 agreement covers, IP, is not on the list (see [Bangladeshi IT engineers: ITEE and B-JET](#bangladeshi-it-engineers-itee-and-b-jet)) <sup>[127](#o127), [128](#o128)</sup>.

For work based on foreign culture (translation, interpreting, language teaching, public relations, advertising, overseas trade, fashion or interior design, product development), you need 3 years of related experience, except that a university graduate can translate, interpret or teach languages without it <sup>[126](#o126), [122](#o122)</sup>.

Two more conditions apply to everyone:
- **Pay:** at least what a Japanese person would earn for the same work <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>.
- **Language (some jobs):** from 15 April 2026, for a category 3 or 4 employer (see [Step 1, item 2: Ask which ISA "category" the employer is in](#step-1-find-a-real-employer-and-check-what-it-must-file-item-2)), a person whose job mainly uses language face to face, such as translation, interpreting or a hotel front desk, must prove CEFR B2 level in that language; JLPT N2 or higher counts as B2 in Japanese <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>.

The status does not cover simple manual work, and a holder who stops doing the job of their status can have it revoked (see [Scams on professional visas, item 6: Wrong job on arrival](#scams-on-professional-visas-item-6)) <sup>[122](#o122), [105](#o105)</sup>.

<a id="engineerspecialist-cost-and-time"></a>

#### 7.1.2 Engineer/Specialist cost and time

- Certificate of Eligibility (COE, the pre-approval issued by Japan's Immigration Services Agency, ISA): free; standard processing 1 to 3 months <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.
- Visa at VFS Global in Dhaka: no visa fee for Bangladeshis, but VFS charges a service charge of BDT 1,900 for work and other COE-based visas, in cash in taka; at least 7 working days <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9), [N2](#n2)</sup>.
- Renewal or change of status in Japan, at the counter, from 1 October 2026: 10,000 yen for 3 months or less, 33,000 yen for 1 year, 48,000 yen for over 1 to under 3 years, 64,000 yen for 3 to under 5 years, 75,000 yen for 5 years <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>. Online applications cost less, for example 27,000 yen plus a 330 yen payment charge for 1 year <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>. Applications received by 30 September 2026 paid the old 6,000 yen fee <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long is a COE valid for visa application and entry after it is issued? <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>

<a id="step-1-find-a-real-employer-and-check-what-it-must-file"></a>

#### 7.1.3 Step 1: Find a real employer and check what it must file

1. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-employer-and-check-what-it-must-file-item-1"></a>**Get a written offer that states the job duties and the salary.** The employer must later give ISA the written working conditions required by Article 15 of Japan's Labour Standards Act, so the same paper is what you should receive <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>. Check the salary is at least what a Japanese employee in that job earns (see [Who qualifies for Engineer/Specialist](#who-qualifies-for-engineerspecialist)) <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-employer-and-check-what-it-must-file-item-2"></a>**Ask which ISA "category" the employer is in.** ISA sorts employers into four categories, and the paperwork depends on it <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>:
   - **Category 1:** companies listed on a Japanese stock exchange, and national or local government bodies <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>.
   - **Category 2:** organisations that withheld 10 million yen or more in income tax on salaries in the previous year <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>.
   - **Category 3 and 4:** smaller employers, and any employer that cannot prove a category falls into category 4 <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>.

   Categories 1 and 2 in principle send no other documents, while categories 3 and 4 must send the company register certificate, a company profile, the latest financial statements (or a business plan if new) and, from 15 April 2026, a declaration about the company's representative <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>. A small or new employer is not a scam in itself, but it means more checking by ISA <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-employer-and-check-what-it-must-file-item-3"></a>**If you will be dispatched (haken) to another company,** ISA's rules updated on 13 July 2026 require a pledge on the decided dispatch destination and the dispatch contract <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>.

<a id="step-2-the-employer-applies-for-your-coe"></a>

#### 7.1.4 Step 2: The employer applies for your COE

1. <a id="step-2-the-employer-applies-for-your-coe-item-1"></a>**Send the employer your papers** <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>:
   - a CV showing where, what and how long you worked in the field <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>;
   - your degree certificate (or proof of equivalent education), or proof of related work experience, or for IT engineers the ITEE pass certificate <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>;
   - a passport copy, so the name on the COE matches the passport <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>;
   - Japanese translations of anything not in Japanese <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-2-the-employer-applies-for-your-coe-item-2"></a>**The employer files the COE at the regional immigration bureau or online.** Staff of the hiring company can file it, or a registered lawyer or gyoseishoshi (administrative scrivener, a licensed paperwork professional) can file it on their behalf <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>. The employer adds the application form, a photo, a return envelope with stamps for simple registered post, and its category documents <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>. There is no ISA fee <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-2-the-employer-applies-for-your-coe-item-3"></a>**Wait 1 to 3 months.** There is no appeal if the COE is refused <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>. Incomplete files are delayed or refused <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-2-the-employer-applies-for-your-coe-item-4"></a>**Receive the COE.** Since 17 March 2023 a COE can be issued by email; the employer can forward the email to you, and you can show it on a phone at VFS and at the airport <sup>[59](#o59), [131](#o131)</sup>. Genuine emails from ISA's online system come from the domain @rasens-immi.moj.go.jp <sup>[131](#o131)</sup>.

<a id="step-3-get-the-visa-at-vfs-global-in-dhaka"></a>

#### 7.1.5 Step 3: Get the visa at VFS Global in Dhaka

1. <a id="step-3-get-the-visa-at-vfs-global-in-dhaka-item-1"></a>**Book an appointment online** with VFS Global, which has handled all Japan visa applications in Bangladesh since 3 November 2024; the embassy does not accept applications directly or give special treatment <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>. VFS Global Japan Visa Application Centre: Delta Life Tower, 4th floor, Road 90, Plot 37, Gulshan-2, Dhaka-1212; 09606 777 910; info.japanbd@vfshelpline.com <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>. From mid-March to early June and mid-September to late October the wait for an appointment can be over a month <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-3-get-the-visa-at-vfs-global-in-dhaka-item-2"></a>**Bring these documents** <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>:
   - the visa application form;
   - your passport and a copy of its ID page;
   - one photo, 2 inch x 1.4 inch, white background <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>;
   - a photocopy of the COE;
   - an airline booking slip.
3. <a id="step-3-get-the-visa-at-vfs-global-in-dhaka-item-3"></a>**Pay the BDT 1,900 service charge** in cash in taka at the centre; there is no visa fee <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-3-get-the-visa-at-vfs-global-in-dhaka-item-4"></a>**Wait for the visa.** VFS's checklist says about one week, and the embassy says at least 7 working days <sup>[N48](#n48), [9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-3-get-the-visa-at-vfs-global-in-dhaka-item-5"></a>**If it is refused,** the embassy gives no specific reason and does not accept a new application for the same purpose within six months <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does a Bangladeshi going to Japan on a gijinkoku visa have to get BMET emigration clearance and a smart card, and if so, through which channel when no recruiting agency is involved? <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>

<a id="step-4-keep-your-status-valid-in-japan"></a>

#### 7.1.6 Step 4: Keep your status valid in Japan

1. <a id="step-4-keep-your-status-valid-in-japan-item-1"></a>**Notify ISA when you change employer,** using the notification on the affiliated organisation <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-4-keep-your-status-valid-in-japan-item-2"></a>**Keep doing the job of your status.** If you stop doing it for 3 months without a good reason, your status can be revoked <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-4-keep-your-status-valid-in-japan-item-3"></a>**Pay resident tax, pension and health insurance on time.** Unpaid tax or large or long unpaid health insurance premiums count against renewals and changes <sup>[132](#o132)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-4-keep-your-status-valid-in-japan-item-4"></a>**Renew before your period of stay ends,** at the counter or online (fees: see [Engineer/Specialist cost and time](#engineerspecialist-cost-and-time)) <sup>[129](#o129), [130](#o130)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-4-keep-your-status-valid-in-japan-item-5"></a>**Ask questions** at ISA's Immigration Services Information Center: 0570-013904 (from IP phones or abroad 03-5796-7112), weekdays 8:30 to 17:15 <sup>[122](#o122), [133](#o133)</sup>. It speaks Japanese, English, Chinese, Korean, Spanish, Portuguese, Vietnamese, Nepali, Thai, Myanmar and Sinhala, not Bengali, and cannot tell you how your own case is going <sup>[133](#o133)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the deadline for the employer-change notification, and can it be filed online? <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>

<a id="highly-skilled-professional-points-system"></a>

### 7.2 Highly Skilled Professional (points system)
