Highly Skilled Professional (i) is granted when your points for education, career, income and other items add up to 70 or more <sup>[123](#o123), [134](#o134)</sup>. It has three types: advanced academic research, advanced specialised or technical work (most engineers), and business management <sup>[135](#o135)</sup>.

<a id="how-the-points-work"></a>

#### 7.2.1 How the points work

Some of the points for the specialised or technical type <sup>[134](#o134), [127](#o127)</sup>:
- **Education:** bachelor's degree 10 points <sup>[134](#o134)</sup>.
- **Age:** up to 29 years 15 points; 30 to 34 years 10; 35 to 39 years 5 <sup>[134](#o134)</sup>.
- **Japanese:** JLPT N1 15 bonus points <sup>[134](#o134)</sup>.
- **Qualification:** a foreign qualification related to the work 5 points; an IT exam on the Minister of Justice's list, such as Bangladesh's ITEE at FE or AP level, 5 points (10 for two or more) <sup>[134](#o134), [127](#o127), [128](#o128)</sup>.
- **Experience:** only experience in the job you will do counts <sup>[134](#o134)</sup>.
- **Salary:** at least 3 million yen a year is required for this type, and bonuses count as income <sup>[134](#o134)</sup>.

Use ISA's English Points Calculation Table to add up your own score before the employer applies <sup>[134](#o134)</sup>.

<a id="what-you-get"></a>

#### 7.2.2 What you get

- **5-year stay:** HSP (i) holders always get 5 years <sup>[124](#o124)</sup>.
- **Fast processing:** ISA aims to decide COE applications in about 10 days and in-country applications in about 5 days <sup>[124](#o124)</sup>.
- **Working spouse:** your spouse can work in Engineer/Specialist-type jobs without meeting the degree or experience rules <sup>[124](#o124)</sup>.
- **Parents:** a parent of you or your spouse can join you to care for a child under 7 or a pregnant spouse, if household income is 8 million yen or more and the parent lives with you <sup>[124](#o124)</sup>.
- **Domestic worker:** you can bring one if household income is 10 million yen or more and you pay at least 200,000 yen a month, and either the worker was employed by you for at least a year before coming to Japan, or you have a child under 13 or a spouse too ill to do housework <sup>[124](#o124)</sup>.
- **Faster permanent residence:** after 3 years at 70 points or 1 year at 80 points (see [Permanent residence](#permanent-residence)) <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
- **HSP (ii):** after a period on HSP (i), an indefinite stay with wider permitted work <sup>[123](#o123)</sup>.

You must still apply for a change of status when you change employer, and a renewal is refused if your points are under 70 at that time <sup>[123](#o123)</sup>.

The J-Skip route (Special Highly Skilled Professional, since April 2023) skips the points: a master's degree or 10 years' experience plus an annual income of 20 million yen or more, with permanent residence possible after 1 year <sup>[135](#o135)</sup>.

<a id="how-to-apply"></a>

#### 7.2.3 How to apply

The steps are the same as Engineer/Specialist ([Step 1: Find a real employer and check what it must file](#step-1-find-a-real-employer-and-check-what-it-must-file) to [Step 3: Get the visa at VFS Global in Dhaka](#step-3-get-the-visa-at-vfs-global-in-dhaka)), with the points calculation sheet and its evidence added to the COE file <sup>[123](#o123), [59](#o59)</sup>. HSP enquiries go to 0570-013904 (03-5796-7112 from abroad), in Japanese or English <sup>[136](#o136)</sup>.

<a id="bangladeshi-it-engineers-itee-and-b-jet"></a>

### 7.3 Bangladeshi IT engineers: ITEE and B-JET

<a id="itee"></a>

#### 7.3.1 ITEE

Japan's IPA signed a mutual recognition agreement with Bangladesh on 1 September 2014, and the Bangladesh Computer Council has run the common Asian IT exam in Bangladesh since October 2014, as a member of ITPEC (Information Technology Professionals Examination Council) <sup>[127](#o127), [137 (2023)](#o137)</sup>. Volunteers of JICA (Japan International Cooperation Agency) brought the exam to Bangladesh so that employers would feel more confident hiring young Bangladeshi engineers <sup>[137 (2023)](#o137)</sup>. A pass at FE or AP level counts in place of a related degree or 10 years' experience for an IT job on the Engineer/Specialist status, and earns HSP points; an IP pass does not <sup>[127](#o127), [128](#o128)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - When is the next ITEE (FE/IP) exam at BCC in Dhaka, and what is the fee? <sup>[127](#o127)</sup>

<a id="b-jet"></a>

#### 7.3.2 B-JET

B-JET (Bangladesh-Japan ICT Engineers' Training Program) began in 2017 as a JICA technical cooperation project to train Bangladeshi IT engineers in Japanese language and business etiquette for jobs in Japan <sup>[137 (2023)](#o137), [N56](#n56)</sup>. When JICA's support ended in 2020, 280 people had finished and 186 were working as engineers for companies in Miyazaki and elsewhere in Japan; in Miyazaki 24 companies hired more than 50 Bangladeshi engineers between 2017 and 2022 <sup>[137 (2023)](#o137)</sup>. It continued without JICA under the University of Miyazaki, North South University (NSU) and companies, and is now run by the publisher Shinko Shuppansha Keirinkan with NSU at the B-JET Centre on the NSU campus in Dhaka <sup>[137 (2023)](#o137), [N57](#n57)</sup>. The programme says it has over 400 graduates, about 250 of them hired by more than 80 Japanese companies <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>.

How the 16th batch worked (October 2026 to March 2027, 30 places) <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>:
1. <a id="b-jet-item-1"></a>**Check you qualify:** a bachelor's degree in CSE, IT or an ICT subject <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>.
2. <a id="b-jet-item-2"></a>**Apply online** by the deadline (30 June 2026 for the 16th batch; selection in July) <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>.
3. <a id="b-jet-item-3"></a>**Pass the selection:** application review, an online coding test, an AI skills interview, a Japanese test and general interview, and an "ideathon" project with a demo video <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>.
4. <a id="b-jet-item-4"></a>**Train about 6 months (100 days),** Monday to Friday 9:00 to 12:30, in person at NSU; Japanese classes aim at JLPT N3 <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>.
5. <a id="b-jet-item-5"></a>**Pay your own transport and accommodation** <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>.
6. <a id="b-jet-item-6"></a>**Meet Japanese companies at the programme's career seminars;** a job depends on your performance and the companies' evaluation, and is not guaranteed <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>.

The 16th batch opened in October 2026 <sup>[N56](#n56)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the B-JET course fee, if any, for the 17th batch, and when do applications open? <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>
> - Do B-JET graduates go on the Engineer/Specialist status, and who pays their COE and flight? <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>

<a id="scams-on-professional-visas"></a>

### 7.4 Scams on professional visas

**Done to the migrant:**
1. <a id="scams-on-professional-visas-item-1"></a>**Fake jobs and loans in JICA's name.** In May 2025 JICA's Bangladesh office warned that scammers use its name on Facebook and elsewhere for fake jobs, savings schemes and loans; JICA charges no fee at any stage of recruitment <sup>[N58](#n58)</sup>. *How to spot it:* any request for money in JICA's or B-JET's name.
2. <a id="scams-on-professional-visas-item-2"></a>**Fake COEs.** A real e-COE comes from ISA's system at @rasens-immi.moj.go.jp and is sent to the employer, who forwards it <sup>[131](#o131)</sup>. *How to spot it:* a COE sent by a broker, from another address, or offered before any employer has interviewed you.
3. <a id="scams-on-professional-visas-item-3"></a>**Paying for a visa.** Japan charges Bangladeshis no visa fee, and the COE is free; only VFS's service charge is paid <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9), [N2](#n2), [59](#o59)</sup>. *How to spot it:* anyone selling a "Japan work visa" or a COE.
4. <a id="scams-on-professional-visas-item-4"></a>**Unlicensed agencies.** In October 2019 RAB (Rapid Action Battalion, Bangladesh's elite police unit) said an agency in Mirpur that was not appointed for Japan had taken Tk 8 lakh each from people through newspaper ads promising Japan jobs (see [Where brokers get in](#where-brokers-get-in)) <sup>[N4 (2019)](#n4)</sup>. *How to spot it:* an ad or agent charging lakhs for a Japan job.
5. <a id="scams-on-professional-visas-item-5"></a>**Fake fee emails.** Online fee notices from 1 October 2026 come from the payment agent ISA designates, so a fee request from any other address is suspect <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.
6. <a id="scams-on-professional-visas-item-6"></a>**Wrong job on arrival.** The status does not cover simple manual work, and a holder who does not do the job of the status for 3 months risks losing it, whatever the employer promised; since 13 July 2026 ISA has required a dispatch (haken) employer to pledge the decided dispatch destination <sup>[122](#o122), [105](#o105)</sup>. *How to spot it:* a contract that names a dispatch agency but no destination company.

**Tried by the migrant:**
- **Fake degrees, experience letters or ITEE certificates.** A status obtained by deceit or false documents can be revoked, and getting landing or permission by deceit carries up to 3 years in prison or a 3 million yen fine <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.
- **Working outside the status.** Working in a job your status does not allow carries up to 1 year in prison or a 2 million yen fine, and employers and brokers who arrange it face up to 3 years or 3 million yen <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has any Bangladeshi been caught with a forged COE or forged ITEE certificate, and how did it surface? <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>

<a id="where-professionals-can-get-help"></a>

### 7.5 Where professionals can get help

- **ISA Immigration Services Information Center:** 0570-013904, from abroad 03-5796-7112, weekdays 8:30 to 17:15; general guidance only, no Bengali <sup>[122](#o122), [133](#o133)</sup>.
- **Bangladesh Embassy labour wing, Tokyo:** First Secretary (Labour), fslabor@mofa.gov.bd; it helps with employment conditions, gives legal aid and aids stranded workers <sup>[117](#o117), [18](#o18)</sup>. Embassy: 3-29 Kioicho, Chiyoda-ku, Tokyo 102-0094, 03-3234-5801 <sup>[116 (2024)](#o116)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many professional (gijinkoku) workers has the labour wing helped in a dispute, and what did it do? <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>

<a id="visit-visa-temporary-visitor"></a>

## 8 Visit visa (temporary visitor)

The temporary visitor visa ("short-term stay", *tanki taizai*) covers sightseeing, rest, sports, visiting relatives, inspection tours, courses, meetings and business contact, for up to 90 days, with no paid work <sup>[138](#o138), [56](#o56)</sup>. Since 3 November 2024 the Embassy of Japan in Dhaka no longer takes applications itself: every application goes through VFS Global, the private company the embassy uses to receive papers <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>. VFS plays no part in the decision, which is the embassy's alone <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. Apply in Bangladesh only if you live there; a Bangladeshi living abroad applies to the Japanese mission in the country of residence <sup>[139](#o139), [76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>.

<a id="visit-visa-cost-and-time"></a>

### 8.1 Visit visa cost and time

- **Visa fee:** free for Bangladeshi nationals <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9), [N2](#n2)</sup>. From 1 July 2026 Japan raised its visa fees to about 15,000 yen for a single-entry visa and 30,000 yen for a multiple-entry visa, but the fee can be waived depending on nationality <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>.
- **VFS service charge:** BDT 2,090 per application for tourism, family visits and multiple-entry visas, charged even though the visa itself is free, and paid in cash in taka only <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. VFS also sells optional extras, such as filling in the form for you for BDT 880 <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>.
- **Time:** the embassy's standard is at least 7 working days from submission at VFS to getting the passport back, not counting days when Japan's Ministry of Foreign Affairs (MOFA) or the Embassy of Japan in Bangladesh is closed for a public holiday <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>. VFS says it usually takes 5 working days <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. It takes longer, from a couple of weeks to several months, when the embassy asks for more papers, checks documents, interviews you or consults MOFA in Tokyo <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9), [76 (2023)](#o76), [N1](#n1)</sup>.
- **Busy seasons:** mid-March to early June and mid-September to late October, when students apply; the wait for a VFS appointment can then pass one month <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-your-guarantor-in-japan-prepares-their-papers"></a>

### 8.2 Step 1: Your guarantor in Japan prepares their papers

A guarantor (*mimoto hoshonin*) is a person living in Japan who promises the Japanese ambassador that you will stay legally; it is a moral promise, not a legal debt <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>. You need one when someone in Japan invites you or pays for your trip; a tourist paying their own way needs no guarantor <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.

The person inviting you prepares these papers and posts them to you, not to the embassy <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>. Papers issued in Japan must be less than three months old <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>. They don't need a company or personal seal <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.

- **Invitation letter ("Letter of Reason for Invitation", a form in the VFS checklist):** addressed to the Japanese ambassador, giving the inviter's name, address and phone number and the specific things you will do; "tourism" or "visiting friends" alone is rejected <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76), [N1](#n1)</sup>.
- **Itinerary in Japan ("Schedule of Stay", a form in the VFS checklist):** arrival and departure dates, where you will stay, and what you plan to do each day <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76), [N1](#n1)</sup>.
- **Letter of guarantee (a form in the VFS checklist)**, if the guarantor pays <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.
- **Proof of the guarantor's money:** the latest income or tax certificate from the city office, a tax return (Form 2) from the tax office, or a bank balance certificate; a withholding tax slip (*gensen choshu-hyo*) is not accepted <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>.
- **Certificate of residence (*juminhyo*)** of the guarantor, showing all family members <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.
- **If the guarantor is a foreigner (for example a Bangladeshi living in Japan):** a copy of both sides of their residence card (*zairyu* card) and of their passport, including all visa pages <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.
- **If the guarantor is Japanese and a relative:** their family register (*koseki tohon*) <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.

<a id="step-2-prepare-your-own-papers"></a>

### 8.3 Step 2: Prepare your own papers

Bring one original and one photocopy of every document except the passport and photos, in the order of the VFS checklist, with a copy of the checklist attached <sup>[141](#o141), [N2](#n2)</sup>. Papers other than the passport are not returned <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>. You'll need:

- **The visa application form** (MOFA's form), with your current passport and any old passports <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.
- **Photos:** VFS asks for photos 2 inch x 1.4 inch, taken within 6 months <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>.
- **Return air booking and hotel booking** <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>. VFS's checklist says you need not buy the ticket or pay for the hotel before the visa <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>.
- **Proof you can pay, if you pay yourself:** your bank statement, and your tax-income certificate if you have one <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.
- **Proof of the relationship, if visiting family or friends:** a birth or marriage certificate for relatives; photos, emails, call history or letters for friends <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>.
- **The guarantor's papers from [Step 1: Your guarantor in Japan prepares their papers](#step-1-your-guarantor-in-japan-prepares-their-papers)** <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.
- **Travel medical insurance:** not on the list, but MOFA strongly recommends it <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.

An application with any required paper missing is not accepted <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>. Errors in the papers, too little passport validity or no blank visa page also get it turned away <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the embassy still want a paid hotel booking and air ticket, as its own list says, or only the itinerary, as the VFS checklist says? <sup>[141](#o141), [N1](#n1)</sup>
> - What bank balance does the embassy expect from a self-funded Bangladeshi tourist?

<a id="step-3-book-a-vfs-appointment-and-submit"></a>

### 8.4 Step 3: Book a VFS appointment and submit

1. <a id="step-3-book-a-vfs-appointment-and-submit-item-1"></a>**Book online** at [visa.vfsglobal.com/bgd/en/jpn](https://visa.vfsglobal.com/bgd/en/jpn); slots for the month after next open on the 15th of each month <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>. Don't apply more than three months before you travel, or VFS may not accept the application <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-3-book-a-vfs-appointment-and-submit-item-2"></a>**If you are in a hurry,** go to VFS early and ask to join the same-day waiting list for cancelled slots; it is first come, first served and not guaranteed <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-3-book-a-vfs-appointment-and-submit-item-3"></a>**Go to the Japan Visa Application Centre:** Delta Life Tower, 4th floor, Road 90, Plot 37, Gulshan-2, Dhaka <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>. Pay the BDT 2,090 service charge in cash <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-3-book-a-vfs-appointment-and-submit-item-4"></a>**Answer any interview:** some applicants are interviewed, which can lengthen processing <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. Going to the embassy in Baridhara instead, or asking someone to get you special treatment there, doesn't work: the embassy refuses such requests <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does VFS Dhaka tell applicants the passport is ready, and what must they bring to collect it?

<a id="step-4-collect-the-passport-and-check-the-visa"></a>

### 8.5 Step 4: Collect the passport and check the visa

A single-entry visa must be used within three months of being issued <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>. The visa is not a guarantee of entry: the immigration officer at the Japanese airport decides whether to let you land <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76), [56](#o56)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How can a Bangladeshi check that a Japanese visa sticker is genuine: does the embassy or VFS confirm it by phone or email?

<a id="how-long-a-visitor-can-stay"></a>

### 8.6 How long a visitor can stay

The immigration officer gives 15, 30 or 90 days on arrival <sup>[138](#o138)</sup>. An extension is allowed only for truly unavoidable humanitarian reasons, such as needing medical treatment <sup>[138](#o138)</sup>. A visitor cannot switch to a student or work status inside Japan except in unavoidable special circumstances; the route to those is a Certificate of Eligibility (COE) applied for from Japan and a new visa from Dhaka (see ["Student visa (Japanese-language schools and universities)"](#student-visa-japanese-language-schools-and-universities)) <sup>[105](#o105), [56](#o56)</sup>.

Overstaying is a crime punished by up to 3 years' imprisonment, a fine of up to 3 million yen, or both <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>. A person deported cannot come back for 5 years, or 10 years if deported or sent out under a departure order before; one who leaves under a departure order is barred for 1 year <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many days do Bangladeshi visitors usually get on arrival: 15, 30 or 90?

<a id="can-you-work-on-a-visit-visa"></a>

### 8.7 Can you work on a visit visa?

No. Any business that earns money or any paid activity is forbidden on this visa <sup>[N1](#n1), [56](#o56)</sup>. Working without permission is punished by up to 1 year's imprisonment or a 2 million yen fine, and working as your main activity by up to 3 years or 3 million yen <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>. The employer and anyone who arranged the job face up to 3 years or 3 million yen <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.

<a id="if-the-visit-visa-is-refused"></a>

### 8.8 If the visit visa is refused

The embassy does not say why <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76), [N1](#n1)</sup>. You cannot apply again for the same purpose within six months of the refusal, because nothing will have changed; the embassy sometimes accepts one sooner when your circumstances have changed significantly and the trip is needed for humanitarian reasons <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76), [139](#o139)</sup>.

<a id="visit-visa-scams"></a>

### 8.9 Visit visa scams

**A visit visa sold as a job.** No job can be done on this visa (see [Can you work on a visit visa?](#can-you-work-on-a-visit-visa)) <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>. In December 2014 the Embassy of Japan warned that offers of full-time jobs in Japan advertised this way are a total fraud <sup>[142 (2014)](#o142)</sup>. On 21 September 2026 people in Haluaghat, Mymensingh, said at a human chain that a ring took about Tk 5 lakh each from at least 20 people for "agricultural visas" to Japan, handed them copies of fake manpower and BMET (Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) papers, and kept their passports <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>. One man was handed a visa and papers, and found they were all fake only when he checked <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>.

**Fake or bought guarantors.** A real guarantor's papers come from Japanese offices: the *juminhyo* and income certificate from the city office or tax office, less than three months old, and for a foreign guarantor a residence card copy <sup>[N1](#n1), [141](#o141)</sup>. Japan checks visit applications closely because of trafficking through fake marriages and illegal work <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>. A status obtained with documents containing false entries can be cancelled <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.

**Paid "visa agents" and fake websites.** MOFA warns of fake websites, social media and emails claiming to be the Japanese government or embassy and asking visa applicants for money <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>. Only agencies the embassy has approved may handle visa applications <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>. VFS is the only channel in Bangladesh, and it charges only its service fee and listed extras <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9), [N2](#n2)</sup>.

**Fake sports teams.** In Pakistan, the Federal Investigation Agency (FIA) arrested a man on 15 September 2025 who, an FIA official said, set up a fake club called "Golden Football Trial", took 40 to 45 lakh Pakistani rupees from each person, and used a football club's invitation to get 15-day Japanese visas for 17 people, who went to Japan on 1 January 2024 and did not return <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>. On 15 June 2025 he tried again with a 22-member "team" in football kit carrying fake no-objection letters; Japanese immigration caught the fraud while questioning them at the airport and sent them all back <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>.

**Tried by migrants: overstaying to work.** Overstaying is a crime and leads to a ban on coming back (see [How long a visitor can stay](#how-long-a-visitor-can-stay)) <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What do Dhaka brokers charge in 2026 for a Japanese "invitation" or guarantor letter, and how does the embassy catch them?
> - Is the 2014 warning on the embassy site still the embassy's current scam notice? <sup>[142 (2014)](#o142)</sup>

<a id="where-to-get-help-with-a-visit-visa"></a>

### 8.10 Where to get help with a visit visa

- **VFS Global Japan Visa Application Centre (appointments, application status):** 09606 777 910, info.japanbd@vfshelpline.com <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.
- **Embassy of Japan, consular section (only for cases VFS can't handle):** +880-2-2222-60010, consular@dc.mofa.go.jp <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.
- **To report a fraudulent applicant, broker or leak:** a letter to the "Special unit for visa service", Embassy of Japan, Plot 5 and 7, Dutabash Road, Baridhara, Dhaka-1212 <sup>[139](#o139)</sup>.
- **MOFA visa information (in Tokyo):** +81-3-5369-6577 from overseas, Monday to Friday 9:00 to 17:00 Japan time <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.
- **In Japan, in distress:** the Embassy of Bangladesh in Tokyo's 24-hour hotline, 080-7004-1971, 070-1260-1984 or 080-4065-6601 (not for routine consular questions) <sup>[116 (2024)](#o116)</sup>.

<a id="business-visit"></a>

## 9 Business visit

A business visit is a temporary visitor visa for business contact, meetings and similar short visits of up to 90 days, with no pay from Japan <sup>[N2](#n2), [138](#o138), [N1](#n1)</sup>. The time, VFS steps, length of stay, refusal rule and help contacts are those of the visit visa (see ["Visit visa (temporary visitor)"](#visit-visa-temporary-visitor)) <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>. The visa is free; VFS charges BDT 2,090 for business affairs, meetings, conferences and training visits, and BDT 1,900 for business communication, business negotiation, after-sales service and marketing visits <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-the-japanese-company-prepares-its-papers"></a>

### 9.1 Step 1: The Japanese company prepares its papers

The inviting side must be a company, an organisation or a government body; a university professor or associate professor may invite for an exchange <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>. It sends you:

- **Invitation letter** stating the specific business, plus any contract or L/C for this visit <sup>[N1](#n1), [141](#o141)</sup>.
- **Letter of guarantee,** if the Japanese side pays <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.
- **Its company registration certificate (*tokibo tohon*),** issued within three months; a company listed on a Japanese stock exchange can send its quarterly report instead <sup>[141](#o141), [N1](#n1)</sup>.

<a id="step-2-prepare-your-papers"></a>

### 9.2 Step 2: Prepare your papers

You'll need, besides the form, passports and photos ([Step 2: Prepare your own papers](#step-2-prepare-your-own-papers)):

- **Employment certificate** from your employer <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.
- **Your tax-income certificate and bank statement** <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.
- **Business documents for this trip,** if any: contract or L/C (letter of credit), list of trade goods, brochures, leaflets, newspaper clippings, and papers about your company <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.

<a id="multiple-entry-visa-for-frequent-visitors"></a>

### 9.3 Multiple-entry visa for frequent visitors

A multiple-entry visa allows stays of up to 90 days each time and is valid for 1, 3 or 5 years, depending on the applicant <sup>[N60](#n60), [139](#o139)</sup>. For business it is open to directors, or full-time employees of more than one year, of a government enterprise, a listed company, a Japanese-affiliated company that belongs to the Japan Chamber of Commerce, a branch or subsidiary of a Japan-listed company, or a company that trades regularly with a Japan-listed company <sup>[N60](#n60)</sup>. Add proof that you qualify and a letter explaining why you need a multiple-entry visa; the embassy may give a single-entry visa instead <sup>[N60](#n60)</sup>.

<a id="how-long-a-business-visitor-can-stay"></a>

### 9.4 How long a business visitor can stay

The same as for a visitor: 15, 30 or 90 days, set on arrival, with no extension except for unavoidable humanitarian reasons <sup>[138](#o138)</sup>.

<a id="can-you-work-on-a-business-visit"></a>

### 9.5 Can you work on a business visit?

No. Earning money or being paid in Japan is not allowed <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>. A Bangladeshi who will be paid by a Japanese employer needs a work visa through a COE <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.

<a id="business-visa-scams"></a>

### 9.6 Business visa scams

**A "business visa" to get into Japan to work.** Working on it is a crime, with the penalties of the visit visa (see [Can you work on a visit visa?](#can-you-work-on-a-visit-visa)) <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do Dhaka brokers sell fake trade-fair or company invitations for Japanese business visas, and at what price?

<a id="if-the-business-visa-is-refused"></a>

### 9.7 If the business visa is refused

No reason is given, and you cannot reapply for the same purpose within six months (see [If the visit visa is refused](#if-the-visit-visa-is-refused)) <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>.

<a id="where-to-get-help-with-a-business-visa"></a>

### 9.8 Where to get help with a business visa

The same offices as for visitors (see [Where to get help with a visit visa](#where-to-get-help-with-a-visit-visa)) <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.

<a id="student-visa-japanese-language-schools-and-universities"></a>

## 10 Student visa (Japanese-language schools and universities)

Many Bangladeshis come to Japan on student visas to study Japanese and then build careers there <sup>[57](#o57)</sup>. The Bangladesh ambassador in Tokyo said in April 2026 that Bangladeshi students in Japan rose from 5,000 to 25,000 in four years <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>. In 2026, 51 Bangladeshi students went to Saga University, for the first time its largest group of foreign students <sup>[N62](#n62)</sup>. The Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo says most students who come through Japanese-language schools mean to work in Japan, working part-time while they study and moving into jobs after gaining language skills <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Japan counted 14,983 Bangladeshis on student status at the end of 2025: what does the ambassador's figure of 25,000 students include <sup>[20](#o20), [N61](#n61)</sup>?

You choose a school in Japan; the school applies to the Immigration Services Agency (ISA, Japan's immigration authority) for a Certificate of Eligibility (COE), a document confirming you meet the conditions for "Student" status <sup>[59](#o59), [56](#o56)</sup>. With the COE, you get the visa through VFS Global (the company that takes Japan visa applications for the embassy) in Dhaka, and at the Japanese airport you receive a residence card <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9), [N48](#n48), [143 (2019)](#o143)</sup>. A student may work part-time only with a separate permission and only up to 28 hours a week <sup>[143 (2019)](#o143), [12](#o12)</sup>. Since April 2026 ISA requires Japanese-language schools to check every student's part-time work every three months and report suspected illegal work to immigration <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>.

<a id="cost"></a>

### 10.1 Cost

- **Japanese-language school, first year:** about 610,000 to 1,900,000 yen for admission and tuition <sup>[144](#o144)</sup>.
- **Vocational school (*senmon gakko*), first year:** about 1,300,000 yen; private university (not medicine, dentistry or pharmacy) about 1,300,000 yen; national university about 820,000 yen <sup>[144](#o144)</sup>.
- **COE:** free <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.
- **Visa:** the embassy's visa page says visas are free for Bangladeshis; the VFS service charge for study visas is listed as BDT 1,900 <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9), [N2](#n2)</sup>. From 1 July 2026 Japan's visa fee is about 15,000 yen for a single entry, but it can be waived depending on nationality <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>.
- **Part-time work permission in Japan:** free <sup>[145 (2024)](#o145)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What do Dhaka agencies charge in 2026 on top of school fees for a Japanese-language school "processing" package, and what is usually included?
> - Which Bangladeshi bank gives the government student loans for Japan that the ambassador mentioned, and on what terms? <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>
> - Is the Japan visa still free for Bangladeshi students after the 1 July 2026 fee change? <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9), [140](#o140)</sup>

<a id="scholarships-and-research-internships"></a>

### 10.2 Scholarships and research internships

- **MEXT scholarship (Japanese government):** Bangladesh's Ministry of Education nominates candidates and publishes the list on its website; for 2024-25 it nominated 84: 16 for research PhD, 20 for research master's, 30 undergraduate, 8 specialised training college and 10 college of technology <sup>[N63 (2024)](#n63)</sup>. Nomination does not guarantee the scholarship: the Japanese Embassy holds written and oral exams, and the Japanese authorities decide on the results <sup>[N63 (2024)](#n63)</sup>. A MEXT scholar's visa is marked "Govt. Scholar" <sup>[N63 (2024)](#n63)</sup>.
- **OIST research internship:** the Okinawa Institute of Science and Technology offers free research internships of two to six months, twice a year, open to Bangladeshi undergraduate and postgraduate students; in 2024 it paid 2,400 yen per working day plus visa support, air tickets, housing and insurance <sup>[N64 (2024)](#n64)</sup>. Applicants need a 400-word statement of purpose, mark sheets in English, passport and NID copies and a recommendation letter <sup>[N64 (2024)](#n64)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - When does the Ministry of Education open MEXT nominations for 2027, and through which portal?

<a id="step-1-choose-a-school-and-check-it"></a>

### 10.3 Step 1: Choose a school and check it

1. <a id="step-1-choose-a-school-and-check-it-item-1"></a>**Check the school is allowed to take students.** Schools on the Ministry of Justice's notified list must also be certified by the Ministry of Education, Culture, Sports, Science and Technology (MEXT) by 31 March 2029 <sup>[146](#o146)</sup>. ISA rates schools, and a school that ISA has not rated as "appropriate" (*tekiseiko*, Class I or II) must send more papers with each COE application <sup>[147](#o147), [148](#o148)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-1-choose-a-school-and-check-it-item-2"></a>**Know what a bad school looks like.** In 2023 ISA barred a Sendai language school from taking new students for five years after it made students sign pledges to pay about 1 to 3 million yen if they switched to a work visa without its permission, and reportedly told students changing status it could not issue their papers <sup>[N35 (2023)](#n35)</sup>.

To sit a university or school entrance exam in Japan before enrolling, apply for a temporary visitor visa with the exam admission ticket <sup>[143 (2019)](#o143)</sup>. The Embassy of Japan has offered free education counselling at the embassy and at the office of JUAAB (Japan Universities Alumni Association in Bangladesh), 26 Indira Road, Farmgate <sup>[142 (2014)](#o142)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Where can a Bangladeshi check online whether a school is on ISA's notified list, its rating, and MEXT certification?
> - Does the embassy or JUAAB still give free education counselling in 2026? <sup>[142 (2014)](#o142)</sup>

<a id="step-2-learn-japanese-and-prove-it"></a>

### 10.4 Step 2: Learn Japanese and prove it

Language-school entrants need Japanese at CEFR (Common European Framework of Reference) A1 level or above <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. From July 2026, 150 hours of study is no longer enough proof: ISA checks your level mainly by a Japanese test certificate, such as the JLPT (Japanese-Language Proficiency Test), or by the school's own interview, which can be online <sup>[13](#o13), [148](#o148)</sup>. Keep the certificate from your Bangladeshi language centre too; the embassy asks for it with the visa <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.

<a id="step-3-gather-the-sponsors-papers"></a>

### 10.5 Step 3: Gather the sponsor's papers

The sponsor (*keihi shibensha*) is the person who pays, usually a parent <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>. For a school ISA has not rated "appropriate", the COE needs <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>:

- **Statement of financial support (*keihi shiben-sho*)**, ISA's form <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>.
- **Proof of the sponsor's relationship to you** <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>. If the sponsor is not a parent, the embassy also wants a family tree with passport, NID (national identity card) or *nikahnama* (marriage certificate) copies <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.
- **Original bank balance certificate** <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>.
- **Records of how the money built up over the past year** <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>.
- **Proof of the sponsor's job and of their income over the past year** <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>.
- **Your final school's graduation certificate** <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>.

Documents in Bengali or English need a Japanese translation attached <sup>[147](#o147)</sup>. The school fills in the "confirmation forms"; you don't <sup>[147](#o147)</sup>. For entry to a Japanese-language school from April 2027, ISA adds a new confirmation of ability to pay <sup>[147](#o147)</sup>. The embassy's own list asks for 6 months of the sponsor's bank statements and 3 years of the sponsor's tax certificates and tax payment receipts <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What sponsor bank balance do schools and ISA expect from Bangladeshi applicants in 2026?
> - How does ISA check a Bangladeshi sponsor's bank certificate and tax papers, and how often are they found false?

<a id="step-4-the-school-applies-for-your-coe"></a>

### 10.6 Step 4: The school applies for your COE

1. <a id="step-4-the-school-applies-for-your-coe-item-1"></a>**Send the papers to the school.** A staff member of the school files the COE application with ISA for you <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-4-the-school-applies-for-your-coe-item-2"></a>**Wait 1 to 3 months** (ISA's standard) <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-4-the-school-applies-for-your-coe-item-3"></a>**Receive the COE by email** or on paper from the school; a printed copy or the email is accepted for the visa <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>.

A COE does not guarantee the visa <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>. If ISA has refused you a COE or residence permission before, the new application must explain and remove the reason <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What share of Bangladeshi COE applications to language schools did ISA refuse in 2025 and 2026, and for what main reasons?
> - How long is a student COE valid for entering Japan?

<a id="step-5-pay-the-school-through-a-bangladeshi-bank"></a>

### 10.7 Step 5: Pay the school through a Bangladeshi bank

Bangladesh Bank's FEPD-1 Circular No. 40 of 30 September 2026 lets banks send money for a language course abroad, even one that is not a step to a bachelor's degree <sup>[149](#o149)</sup>. Bring the bank the Appendix-6 application form, the school's admission letter, its estimate of tuition, housing, insurance and other costs, attested copies of your certificates and your passport <sup>[149](#o149)</sup>. The bank pays tuition directly to the school; housing and living money that need not be paid to the school can go to you on an international card <sup>[149](#o149)</sup>. The bank sends one academic year at a time, and all payments must go through one bank branch, which keeps your student file <sup>[149](#o149)</sup>. If the school's papers say a deposit is needed to process the application or hold a place, the bank may send it in advance <sup>[149](#o149)</sup>.

<a id="step-6-apply-for-the-visa-at-vfs"></a>

### 10.8 Step 6: Apply for the visa at VFS

Book and submit as in [Step 3: Book a VFS appointment and submit](#step-3-book-a-vfs-appointment-and-submit) <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>. VFS asks COE holders only for the application form, passport and a copy of its photo page, one photo, the COE copy and an air booking <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>. The embassy's own list for language-school students is longer: the original COE, the admission certificate, the fee receipt, any JLPT certificate, your Bangladeshi language-centre certificate, SSC and HSC (Secondary and Higher Secondary School Certificate) certificates, mark sheets and registration cards, the sponsor's 6-month bank statement and 3 years of tax papers, information on your Bangladeshi language centre, and your original Japanese textbook <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>. Student applications crowd VFS from mid-March to early June and mid-September to late October <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>. Japan's pre-entry tuberculosis screening applies only to nationals of the Philippines, Viet Nam, Indonesia, Nepal, Myanmar and China, so Bangladeshis do not need it <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the embassy still ask language-school students for the long list (sponsor bank statements, tax papers, textbook) at VFS, or only the COE checklist? <sup>[N48](#n48), [141](#o141)</sup>

<a id="step-7-arrive-and-register"></a>

### 10.9 Step 7: Arrive and register

1. <a id="step-7-arrive-and-register-item-1"></a>**At Narita, Haneda, Kansai, Chubu, New Chitose, Hiroshima or Fukuoka airport,** you get the residence card on landing <sup>[143 (2019)](#o143)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-7-arrive-and-register-item-2"></a>**Apply for part-time work permission at the airport** on the same day, if you were given a student stay of more than 3 months; otherwise apply later at the immigration office for your address <sup>[143 (2019)](#o143), [145 (2024)](#o145)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-7-arrive-and-register-item-3"></a>**Register your address** at the city office within 14 days of finding your home, with the residence card <sup>[143 (2019)](#o143)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-7-arrive-and-register-item-4"></a>**Before any trip home,** check you have re-entry permission <sup>[143 (2019)](#o143)</sup>.

<a id="how-long-a-student-can-stay"></a>

### 10.10 How long a student can stay

ISA sets the period of stay for each student, up to 4 years and 3 months <sup>[147](#o147)</sup>. A student who stops studying for three months without good reason, or who studies only in name while doing something else, can have the status cancelled <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.

<a id="part-time-work"></a>

### 10.11 Part-time work

You may not work on student status unless you hold "permission to engage in activity other than that permitted" (*shikakugai katsudo kyoka*) <sup>[143 (2019)](#o143), [145 (2024)](#o145)</sup>.

- **Hours:** up to 28 hours a week, and up to 8 hours a day during the school's long holidays <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.
- **How to get it:** the application form only, at the airport on arrival or at the immigration office for your address, weekdays 9:00 to 12:00 and 13:00 to 16:00; no fee; 2 weeks to 2 months; no appeal if refused <sup>[12](#o12), [143 (2019)](#o143), [145 (2024)](#o145)</sup>.
- **Never in the adult entertainment trade:** work in bars, clubs and other businesses under the adult entertainment law (*fuzoku*) cannot be permitted <sup>[145 (2024)](#o145)</sup>.
- **Delivery and freelance work:** if your hours can't be checked objectively, you need an individual permission <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.
- **Work must not get in the way of study** <sup>[145 (2024)](#o145)</sup>.

**How you get caught.** Every three months your school checks whether you have permission, where you work, what you do and how many hours; ISA uses employers' reports of foreign staff to find students working in several places and tells the school <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. The school must report a student who keeps breaking the rules, and any student who says an employer forces them to work more than 28 hours a week; ISA investigates and reflects the findings in its residence screening <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. The school also reports any student whose monthly attendance falls below 50%, together with the name of their part-time employer, and any student who drops out <sup>[146](#o146)</sup>.

Working beyond the permission is punished by up to 1 year's imprisonment or 2 million yen; working as your main activity by up to 3 years or 3 million yen <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.

<a id="schools-that-treat-students-as-cheap-labour"></a>

### 10.12 Schools that treat students as cheap labour

ISA's April 2026 rules name the case of an employer forcing a student to work more than 28 hours a week, and tell schools to report it to immigration <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. The Sendai case shows the other side: a school that tried to tie students to it with penalty pledges lost its right to take students <sup>[N35 (2023)](#n35)</sup>. ISA investigates such reports and reflects the findings in residence screening (see [Part-time work](#part-time-work)) <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which Japanese-language schools or employers have been reported for overworking Bangladeshi students, and what happened to the students?

<a id="agents-in-dhaka-who-promise-study-and-earn"></a>

### 10.13 Agents in Dhaka who promise "study and earn"

The law caps work at 28 hours a week, and the school reports breaches to immigration (see [Part-time work](#part-time-work)) <sup>[12](#o12), [13](#o13)</sup>. ISA asks for the sponsor's bank balance, how it was built up over a year, and their income, not the student's expected earnings ([Step 3: Gather the sponsor's papers](#step-3-gather-the-sponsors-papers)) <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What do Bangladeshi language students in Japan actually earn per month from 28 hours a week, and how long does it take to repay an agent's fee?
> - Which Dhaka agencies have been prosecuted for Japan student-visa fraud?

<a id="after-graduation-job-hunting-visa"></a>

### 10.14 After graduation: job-hunting visa

A student who has started job hunting before graduating can switch to "Designated Activities" (*tokutei katsudo*) for continued job hunting if they graduated from a Japanese university, junior college or graduate school, or from a vocational school with the *senmonshi* title <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>. A Japanese-language school graduate qualifies only if they already hold a bachelor's degree from a university abroad, and the school meets ISA's conditions <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>. You'll need the graduation certificate, the school's recommendation letter for continued job hunting, proof that you are job hunting, and proof you can pay your costs <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>. Language-school graduates also need an attendance certificate, the foreign degree, and a signed confirmation that they will meet the school regularly for job-hunting advice <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>. Part-time work of up to 28 hours a week is possible with permission <sup>[150](#o150)</sup>. A person not doing the activity of their status can have it cancelled <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long is the job-hunting Designated Activities stay, and how many times can it be renewed in 2026?
> - What share of Bangladeshi language-school graduates go on to a vocational school, a university or a job?

<a id="if-the-coe-or-visa-is-refused"></a>

### 10.15 If the COE or visa is refused

Neither ISA nor the embassy gives the specific reason <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>. The embassy does not accept a new visa application for the same purpose within six months <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>. A new COE application must explain and remove the reason for the earlier refusal <sup>[148](#o148)</sup>.

<a id="student-visa-scams"></a>

### 10.16 Student visa scams

**Fake papers sold as Japan jobs.** In September 2026 families in Mymensingh said a ring had sold them fake Japan visas and kept their passports (see [Visit visa scams](#visit-visa-scams)) <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>.

**Penalty pledges and withheld papers.** A school or agent that makes you sign a promise to pay money if you change school or status is doing what got the Sendai school barred <sup>[N35 (2023)](#n35)</sup>.

**Tried by students: false sponsor papers.** A status obtained with documents containing false entries can be cancelled <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.

**Tried by students: enrolling only to work.** A student who studies only in name can lose the status (see [How long a student can stay](#how-long-a-student-can-stay)) <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.

<a id="where-students-can-get-help"></a>

### 10.17 Where students can get help

- **In Bangladesh, about the visa:** VFS Global, 09606 777 910, info.japanbd@vfshelpline.com; the Embassy of Japan's consular section, +880-2-2222-60010, consular@dc.mofa.go.jp <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.
- **In Japan, immigration questions:** ISA's Immigration Information Center, 0570-013904 (03-5796-7112 from IP phones or abroad) <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>.
- **In Japan, from your embassy:** the Labour Welfare Wing of the Embassy of Bangladesh, Tokyo, fslabor@mofa.gov.bd, +81-3-3234-5801 extension 201 or +81-80-4352-4795; consular section consular.bdembjp@mofa.gov.bd; 24-hour emergency hotline 080-7004-1971 <sup>[18](#o18), [116 (2024)](#o116)</sup>.
- **Bangladeshi student community:** the Bangladesh Students Support Association in Japan (BSSAJ) works with the embassy on student matters <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the Tokyo embassy's Labour Welfare Wing take complaints from students about employers or schools, and what does it do with them?

<a id="transit"></a>

## 11 Transit

If you change planes in Japan without passing immigration, you need no visa <sup>[151](#o151)</sup>. You do need a visa if you must pass immigration: for an overnight connection (Narita closes its airside area from midnight to 6:00), a change between Narita and Haneda, a change of airline with your bags checked in again, or any other reason <sup>[152](#o152), [N38](#n38)</sup>. Japan stopped issuing transit visas on 1 July 2026; a person who must enter Japan applies for a temporary visitor visa instead (see ["Visit visa (temporary visitor)"](#visit-visa-temporary-visitor)) <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>.

The airline can ask immigration for a "shore pass" (port-of-call landing permission) for a passenger to land near the airport for up to 72 hours, or for transit landing for a passenger leaving within 3 days from another nearby airport <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>. It is at the officer's discretion and cannot be booked <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>.

**How long and can you work?** A shore pass or transit landing lasts only the hours or days above <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>. A temporary visitor visa does not allow paid work <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>. Staying beyond the period on a landing permission is a crime with up to 3 years' imprisonment or a 3 million yen fine <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.

**If refused:** after a temporary visitor visa refusal, the embassy does not accept a new application for the same purpose within six months, and gives no specific reason <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>.

**Scams and help:** a "transit visa" sold in 2026 is not a real product, since Japan no longer issues them <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>. For help, contact VFS Global, 09606 777 910, info.japanbd@vfshelpline.com <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has the Dhaka embassy removed the transit visa from its lists, as other Japanese missions did on 1 July 2026? Its old list asked for the next country's visa and refused "impractical" routes such as Dhaka to Seoul via Tokyo <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.

<a id="medical-stay-visa"></a>

## 12 Medical stay visa

The medical stay visa is for patients going to Japan for treatment, check-ups, dental care or rest cures, and for family or other people who go with them to look after them <sup>[153 (2019)](#o153)</sup>. Companions may not work <sup>[153 (2019)](#o153)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-find-a-registered-guarantor"></a>

### 12.1 Step 1: Find a registered guarantor

Contact a guarantor on the list of registered guarantors kept by Japan's Ministry of Foreign Affairs (MOFA), either a medical coordinator or a travel agency, and ask it to arrange your treatment <sup>[153 (2019)](#o153)</sup>. The hospital is chosen with the guarantor <sup>[153 (2019)](#o153)</sup>. One registered guarantor is Emergency Assistance Japan in Tokyo, +81-3-3811-8600, mj-info@emergency.co.jp <sup>[154](#o154)</sup>.

<a id="step-2-get-the-hospitals-certificate"></a>

### 12.2 Step 2: Get the hospital's certificate

Through the guarantor, get the "Certificate from the medical institution about the planned medical services and references from the guarantor"; for repeated visits, also a Medical Treatment Plan from a doctor in Japan <sup>[153 (2019)](#o153)</sup>.

<a id="step-3-apply"></a>

### 12.3 Step 3: Apply

You'll need your passport, the application form, a photo, the certificate from [Step 2: Get the hospital's certificate](#step-2-get-the-hospitals-certificate), proof of your finances such as a bank statement, and an identity document <sup>[153 (2019)](#o153)</sup>. A companion needs only the passport, form, photo and identity document <sup>[153 (2019)](#o153)</sup>. For stays over 90 days you must be admitted to hospital, and hospital staff or a family member in Japan must get a COE for you first <sup>[153 (2019)](#o153)</sup>. Apply through VFS as in [Step 3: Book a VFS appointment and submit](#step-3-book-a-vfs-appointment-and-submit) <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.

<a id="how-long-a-patient-can-stay"></a>

### 12.4 How long a patient can stay

The period of stay is 90 days, 6 months or 1 year, depending on your condition <sup>[153 (2019)](#o153)</sup>. A multiple-entry visa, valid up to 3 years, is possible if each visit is 90 days or less <sup>[153 (2019)](#o153)</sup>.

<a id="can-you-work-on-a-medical-stay-visa"></a>

### 12.5 Can you work on a medical stay visa?

No; companions also may not do paid work <sup>[153 (2019)](#o153)</sup>.

<a id="if-the-medical-visa-is-refused"></a>

### 12.6 If the medical visa is refused

The embassy does not accept a new application for the same purpose within six months, and gives no specific reason <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>.

<a id="medical-visa-scams"></a>

### 12.7 Medical visa scams

> **Need to know**
> - Are there brokers in Bangladesh selling "medical visas" to Japan as a way to work?

<a id="where-to-get-help-with-a-medical-visa"></a>

### 12.8 Where to get help with a medical visa

The registered guarantor handles the hospital side <sup>[153 (2019)](#o153)</sup>. For the visa, contact VFS Global, 09606 777 910, info.japanbd@vfshelpline.com <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which registered medical guarantors serve Bangladeshi patients or speak Bengali?

<a id="family-visas"></a>

## 13 Family visas

Family of a professional or a student come on the Dependent status; family of technical interns and Specified Skilled Worker (i) holders cannot come <sup>[155](#o155), [156](#o156)</sup>.

<a id="who-can-bring-family"></a>

### 13.1 Who can bring family

The Dependent status covers the spouse or child supported by a holder of a work status such as Engineer/Specialist, Highly Skilled Professional (HSP), Skilled Labor or Specified Skilled Worker (ii), or by a Student <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. Technical Intern Training (TITP) and Specified Skilled Worker (i) are not on that list, and ISA's SSW portal states that SSW (i) holders cannot bring family while SSW (ii) holders can <sup>[155](#o155), [156](#o156)</sup>. Only a spouse and children are covered, not parents or siblings (for an HSP's parents, see [Family of a Highly Skilled Professional](#family-of-a-highly-skilled-professional)) <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>.

<a id="dependent-spouse-and-children-of-a-worker-or-student"></a>

### 13.2 Dependent: spouse and children of a worker or student

The Dependent status is granted for a period ISA sets, up to 5 years <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>.

<a id="what-a-dependant-needs"></a>

#### 13.2.1 What a dependant needs

- **Relationship proof:** the nikah nama (marriage certificate) or the child's birth certificate, with Japanese translations <sup>[155](#o155), [122](#o122)</sup>.
- **The supporter's residence card or passport copy** <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>.
- **The supporter's income proof:** an employment certificate, plus the resident tax certificate and tax payment certificate showing a year's income and tax, issued by the city or ward office where the supporter lived on 1 January <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>.
- **If the supporter is a student:** a bank balance certificate in the supporter's name, or a scholarship certificate <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>.

<a id="how-a-dependant-applies"></a>

#### 13.2.2 How a dependant applies

1. <a id="how-a-dependant-applies-item-1"></a>**The supporter in Japan applies for each family member's COE** at the regional immigration bureau with the papers above; it is free and takes 1 to 3 months <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.
2. <a id="how-a-dependant-applies-item-2"></a>**The family member applies for the visa at VFS in Dhaka** with the form, passport, photo, COE copy and flight booking (as in [Step 3: Get the visa at VFS Global in Dhaka](#step-3-get-the-visa-at-vfs-global-in-dhaka)) <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>.
3. <a id="how-a-dependant-applies-item-3"></a>**In Japan, keep the status in order.** A dependant must notify ISA on divorce or the spouse's death <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. A dependant can work only up to 28 hours a week with permission to engage in activities outside the status, which is free <sup>[157 (2024)](#o157), [158](#o158), [145 (2024)](#o145)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is there a minimum salary a Bangladeshi gijinkoku holder must earn before ISA grants a dependent COE? <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>
> - Do brokers in Dhaka sell forged nikah namas, birth certificates or sponsor tax papers for Dependent COEs, and how have such cases been caught?

<a id="spouse-of-a-japanese-national"></a>

### 13.3 Spouse of a Japanese national

The Spouse or Child of Japanese National status covers a Japanese national's spouse and is granted for 5 years, 3 years, 1 year or 6 months <sup>[159](#o159)</sup>.

<a id="what-a-japanese-nationals-spouse-needs"></a>

#### 13.3.1 What a Japanese national's spouse needs

- the Japanese spouse's koseki tohon (family register) showing the marriage <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>;
- a marriage certificate from Bangladesh <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>;
- proof of who pays living costs: the supporter's resident tax and tax payment certificates <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>;
- a letter of guarantee from the Japanese spouse living in Japan <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>;
- the Japanese spouse's certificate of residence listing the household <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>;
- ISA's questionnaire (shitsumonsho), available in English <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>;
- 2 to 3 snapshots of the couple together, clearly showing both faces and not edited with apps, plus social media or call records <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>.

<a id="how-a-japanese-nationals-spouse-applies"></a>

#### 13.3.2 How a Japanese national's spouse applies

1. <a id="how-a-japanese-nationals-spouse-applies-item-1"></a>**Make sure the marriage is on record in both countries:** ISA needs the Japanese spouse's koseki showing the marriage and a marriage certificate from Bangladesh <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>.
2. <a id="how-a-japanese-nationals-spouse-applies-item-2"></a>**The Japanese spouse applies for the COE** with the papers above; it is free and takes 1 to 3 months <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.
3. <a id="how-a-japanese-nationals-spouse-applies-item-3"></a>**Apply for the visa at VFS in Dhaka** (see [Step 3: Get the visa at VFS Global in Dhaka](#step-3-get-the-visa-at-vfs-global-in-dhaka)) <sup>[N48](#n48)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which Bangladeshi marriage documents does the Japanese city office accept to register a marriage with a Bangladeshi, and does it need MOFA attestation? <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>

<a id="marriage-scams"></a>

#### 13.3.3 Marriage scams

- **Paper marriages (gisō kekkon).** A spouse who does not live as a spouse for 6 months without good reason can lose the status, and a status obtained by deceit is revoked; getting permission by deceit carries up to 3 years in prison or a 3 million yen fine <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>. ISA asks for photos, call records and a long questionnaire precisely to test the relationship <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>.
- **If a real marriage breaks down,** ISA must consider giving the person a chance to change status or apply for permanent residence before revoking the spouse status <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What do brokers charge in Dhaka or Tokyo to arrange a paper marriage with a Japanese national, and how have such cases been caught? <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>

<a id="family-of-a-highly-skilled-professional"></a>

### 13.4 Family of a Highly Skilled Professional

An HSP can also bring a parent (in limited cases) and a domestic worker, and the spouse can work without meeting the degree rules (see [What you get](#what-you-get)) <sup>[124](#o124)</sup>.

<a id="where-to-get-help-with-a-family-visa"></a>

### 13.5 Where to get help with a family visa

ISA's Immigration Information Center answers status questions on 0570-013904 (03-5796-7112 from IP phones or abroad) <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>. The Labour Welfare Wing of the Embassy of Bangladesh, Tokyo, is on fslabor@mofa.gov.bd and +81-3-3234-5801 extension 201 <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.

<a id="permanent-residence-and-citizenship"></a>

## 14 Permanent residence and citizenship

Permanent residence (PR) normally needs 10 years' continuous stay in Japan, including 5 years on a work or residence status, and TITP and SSW (i) years do not count as work years <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.

<a id="permanent-residence"></a>

### 14.1 Permanent residence

<a id="requirements"></a>

#### 14.1.1 Requirements

ISA's guidelines (revised 24 February 2026) require <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>:
1. <a id="requirements-item-1"></a>**Good conduct:** a law-abiding life <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
2. <a id="requirements-item-2"></a>**Independent living:** assets or skills so you are not a burden on the public <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
3. <a id="requirements-item-3"></a>**Ten years:** in principle 10 years' continuous stay, including 5 years on a work or residence status other than TITP and SSW (i) <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
4. <a id="requirements-item-4"></a>**No fines or prison sentences, and public duties paid on time:** tax, pension and health insurance, and immigration notifications; paying late counts against you even if paid by the time you apply <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
5. <a id="requirements-item-5"></a>**The longest period of stay** for your current status; until 31 March 2027 a 3-year stay counts as the longest <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.

The spouse or child of a Japanese national or permanent resident does not need items 1 and 2 <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.

<a id="shorter-routes"></a>

#### 14.1.2 Shorter routes

- **Spouse of a Japanese or PR:** 3 years of real married life and 1 year living in Japan <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
- **Long-Term Resident:** 5 years <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
- **Recognised refugee:** 5 years after recognition <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
- **HSP with 70+ points:** 3 years <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
- **HSP with 80+ points or J-Skip (Special Highly Skilled Professional, since April 2023):** 1 year <sup>[125](#o125), [135](#o135)</sup>.

<a id="cost-time-and-documents"></a>

#### 14.1.3 Cost, time and documents

- **Fee:** 200,000 yen, paid in revenue stamps only when granted; applications received by 30 September 2026 pay the old 10,000 yen <sup>[161](#o161), [129](#o129)</sup>. A reduced 20,000 yen can apply only to spouses and children of Japanese nationals or PRs (and similar) who live in hardship close to welfare level <sup>[161](#o161), [162](#o162)</sup>.
- **Time:** 4 to 6 months; there is no appeal <sup>[161](#o161)</sup>.
- **Tax and insurance proof:** 5 years of resident tax certificates and proof of paying on time, and 2 years of pension and health insurance records <sup>[163](#o163)</sup>.
- **Guarantor:** normally a Japanese national or permanent resident living in Japan <sup>[161](#o161)</sup>.
- **Your stay:** if it will expire while PR is pending, renew it separately <sup>[161](#o161)</sup>.

Check ISA's PR checklist first: one "No" makes a refusal likely <sup>[161](#o161)</sup>.

A draft revision of the PR guidelines, open for public comment until 3 September 2026, adds a higher income standard and a concrete "national interest" test, and would not count time spent job-hunting or in refugee procedures toward the 10 years <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has the September 2026 draft of the PR guidelines been adopted, and what income level does it set? <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>
> - Do agents or administrative scriveners charge Bangladeshis for PR applications, how much, and do any sell a guarantor?

For help with an application, ISA's Immigration Information Center is on 0570-013904 (03-5796-7112 from IP phones or abroad) <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>.

<a id="realistic-timelines"></a>

#### 14.1.4 Realistic timelines

- **Engineer/Specialist from day one:** 10 years, and you must hold the longest period of stay for your status (until 31 March 2027, a 3-year stay counts) <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
- **Student, then Engineer/Specialist:** student years count toward the 10 years but you also need 5 years on the work status <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
- **TITP or SSW (i), then another status:** those years do not count as work years, so the 5 work years start only on SSW (ii), Engineer/Specialist or another work status <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
- **HSP:** 3 years at 70 points, 1 year at 80 points <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
- **Spouse of a Japanese national:** 3 years married and 1 year in Japan <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.

<a id="naturalisation-japanese-citizenship"></a>

### 14.2 Naturalisation (Japanese citizenship)

Naturalisation needs <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>:
- 5 years' continuous residence, on a valid status of residence <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>;
- age 18 or over and adult under Bangladeshi law <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>;
- good conduct, judged on criminal record, tax payment and nuisance to others <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>;
- enough household income to live without hardship; a spouse's income counts <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>;
- giving up Bangladeshi nationality in principle <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>;
- Japanese good enough for daily life, in speaking, reading and writing <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>.

From 1 April 2026 the Ministry of Justice requires, in principle, 10 years' stay to show integration and checks tax and social insurance payments over a longer period <sup>[165](#o165)</sup>.

1. <a id="naturalisation-japanese-citizenship-item-1"></a>**Go in person to the Legal Affairs Bureau (homukyoku) for your address** and ask which documents your case needs <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>.
2. <a id="naturalisation-japanese-citizenship-item-2"></a>**Prepare the documents:** the application with a photo, a statement of motivation, a CV, and proof of income and tax payment <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>. Nationality and family documents must in principle come from Bangladeshi authorities <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>.

A child born in Japan to two Bangladeshi parents does not become Japanese (see [Having a child in Japan](#having-a-child-in-japan)) <sup>[164](#o164), [166](#o166)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long does naturalisation take at the Tokyo Legal Affairs Bureau, and does Bangladesh's Home Ministry issue a certificate of loss of nationality? <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>

<a id="changing-visa-type"></a>

## 15 Changing visa type

A person already in Japan can apply to ISA to switch to another status without leaving Japan, from the time the reason arises until the current stay expires <sup>[167](#o167), [129](#o129)</sup>.

<a id="how-a-change-works"></a>

### 15.1 How a change works

1. <a id="how-a-change-works-item-1"></a>**Apply at the regional immigration bureau for your address, or online.** You must be in Japan, even if your employer or a gyoseishoshi (administrative scrivener, a licensed paperwork professional) files for you; being busy at work is not a reason for a relative to file instead <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>.
2. <a id="how-a-change-works-item-2"></a>**Show the new activity is real and fits a status,** with the documents for the new status <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>.
3. <a id="how-a-change-works-item-3"></a>**Wait 1 to 2 months.** There is no appeal <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>.
4. <a id="how-a-change-works-item-4"></a>**Pay the fee only when approved,** in revenue stamps: 10,000 to 75,000 yen at the counter depending on the period granted; online it is less, for example 27,330 yen including the payment fee for a 1-year stay <sup>[129](#o129), [130](#o130)</sup>.

For questions about a change of status or permanent residence, call ISA's Immigration Information Center on 0570-013904 (03-5796-7112 from IP phones or abroad) <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>.

<a id="student-to-work"></a>

### 15.2 Student to work

1. <a id="student-to-work-item-1"></a>**Get a job offer that fits Engineer/Specialist** (see [Engineer/Specialist in Humanities/International Services](#engineerspecialist-in-humanitiesinternational-services)) and apply for a change of status <sup>[122](#o122), [129](#o129)</sup>.
2. <a id="student-to-work-item-2"></a>**If you graduate without a job,** graduates of Japanese universities, and some graduates of senmon gakko and of Japanese language schools who already hold a foreign degree, can switch to Designated Activities to keep job hunting; you can apply before graduation with the school's recommendation and an expected-graduation certificate <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>.
3. <a id="student-to-work-item-3"></a>**For SSW instead,** a student must pass the SSW tests <sup>[40](#o40)</sup>.

While studying, part-time work is limited to 28 hours a week (8 hours a day in long holidays) <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.

<a id="titp-to-ssw-i"></a>

### 15.3 TITP to SSW (i)

1. <a id="titp-to-ssw-i-item-1"></a>**Finish TITP (ii) satisfactorily.** You then need no Japanese or skills test for SSW (i) in the same field; a different field needs that field's skills test <sup>[40](#o40)</sup>.
2. <a id="titp-to-ssw-i-item-2"></a>**If you have no evaluation record,** submit a statement of reasons or an explanatory paper <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>.
3. <a id="titp-to-ssw-i-item-3"></a>**Sign a contract with the new employer and apply** for the change of status <sup>[40](#o40)</sup>.

TITP years do not count as work years toward PR <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.

<a id="ssw-i-to-ssw-ii"></a>

### 15.4 SSW (i) to SSW (ii)

1. <a id="ssw-i-to-ssw-ii-item-1"></a>**Pass the SSW (ii) skills test for your field;** some fields require work experience before you can sit it <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>.
2. <a id="ssw-i-to-ssw-ii-item-2"></a>**Apply for the change.** SSW (i) is limited to 5 years in total; SSW (ii) has no upper limit and allows family <sup>[34](#o34), [156](#o156)</sup>.

<a id="dependant-to-work"></a>

### 15.5 Dependant to work

- **Up to 28 hours a week:** apply for permission to engage in activities outside the status; it is free <sup>[157 (2024)](#o157), [145 (2024)](#o145)</sup>.
- **Full-time on a work status:** the dependant must meet that status's rules, such as a degree for Engineer/Specialist <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>.
- **Children raised in Japan:** a dependant who entered before 18, finished compulsory education and high school in Japan and has a job can change to Long-Term Resident <sup>[157 (2024)](#o157)</sup>.

<a id="visit-to-work-not-allowed"></a>

### 15.6 Visit to work: not allowed

The Immigration Act says a change from Temporary Visitor (the short-stay visa) is not granted unless there are unavoidable special circumstances <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>. To work, leave Japan and come back with a COE-based visa (see [Step 2: The employer applies for your COE](#step-2-the-employer-applies-for-your-coe) and [Step 3: Get the visa at VFS Global in Dhaka](#step-3-get-the-visa-at-vfs-global-in-dhaka)) <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>. Asylum is not a way to stay and work (see [Asylum](#asylum)) <sup>[168 (2019)](#o168)</sup>.

<a id="asylum"></a>

## 16 Asylum

Japan recognises very few refugees: 187 people in 2025, while 11,298 applied that year, and Bangladesh is one of the five most common nationalities applying <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>. Asylum is not a way to work in Japan <sup>[168 (2019)](#o168)</sup>.

<a id="how-few-are-recognised"></a>

### 16.1 How few are recognised

- **2025:** 11,298 applications; of 14,832 cases processed, 183 were recognised at first instance and 9,214 refused (79 of those refused got complementary protection) <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.
- **On review (appeal) in 2025:** 7,702 requests; 8 succeeded and 3,422 were refused <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.
- **2024:** 12,373 applications and 190 recognised <sup>[169](#o169)</sup>.

Most of those allowed to stay in 2025 got complementary protection (474) or a humanitarian stay (525), not refugee status <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>. Complementary protection, in place since 1 December 2023, is for people such as conflict evacuees who are not Convention refugees but need similar protection <sup>[27](#o27)</sup>.

<a id="bangladeshi-applicants"></a>

### 16.2 Bangladeshi applicants

Bangladesh was among the five main nationalities of applicants in 2025 (with Thailand, Myanmar, India and Sri Lanka), and among the five main nationalities appealing refusals in 2024 <sup>[26](#o26), [169](#o169)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many Bangladeshis applied for refugee status in Japan in 2024 and 2025, and how many were recognised? ISA's nationality tables (PDF) could not be read as text. <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>

<a id="the-2024-rule-deportation-after-the-third-application"></a>

### 16.3 The 2024 rule: deportation after the third application

The amended Immigration Act (Law No. 56 of 2023) took effect on 10 June 2024 <sup>[27](#o27)</sup>. Before it, any pending refugee claim stopped deportation, and ISA says some people applied again and again to avoid removal <sup>[170 (2023)](#o170)</sup>. Now these people can be deported while a claim is pending <sup>[27](#o27), [105](#o105)</sup>:
- someone on a third or later application;
- someone sentenced to 3 years' prison or more;
- a terrorist.

A third-time applicant is protected only by submitting material showing substantial reason to recognise them <sup>[27](#o27), [43](#o43)</sup>. In 2025, 1,305 applicants, about 11.6%, had applied before <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>. A deported person is normally barred from Japan for 5 or 10 years, while an overstayer who leaves voluntarily under a departure order faces 1 year <sup>[158](#o158), [27](#o27)</sup>.

<a id="work-while-a-claim-is-pending-and-how-it-was-tightened"></a>

### 16.4 Work while a claim is pending, and how it was tightened

Until 14 January 2018, applicants could generally work 6 months after applying <sup>[168 (2019)](#o168)</sup>. From 15 January 2018 ISA sorts each claim <sup>[171 (2018)](#o171), [172 (2018)](#o172)</sup>:
- **A:** likely refugees or civil-war cases, who quickly get a status allowing work <sup>[171 (2018)](#o171), [172 (2018)](#o172)</sup>;
- **B:** claims clearly not on Convention grounds, who are not allowed to stay <sup>[172 (2018)](#o172), [171 (2018)](#o171)</sup>;
- **C:** repeat claims with the same story <sup>[172 (2018)](#o172)</sup>;
- **D:** all others <sup>[172 (2018)](#o172)</sup>.

Applicants who stopped their original activity before applying (absconded technical interns, students who were expelled, dropped out or graduated) get no work permission and only 3-month stays <sup>[171 (2018)](#o171), [172 (2018)](#o172), [173 (2018)](#o173)</sup>. ISA found that in the first half of 2017 about 80% of TITP-status applicants and 68% of student-status applicants applied after leaving their training or school <sup>[172 (2018)](#o172)</sup>. Applicants who clearly are not refugees face departure and deportation when their stay expires <sup>[168 (2019)](#o168)</sup>.

An applicant can apply to change to "Designated Activities (refugee applicant)" to stay while waiting <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>. A technical intern now gets this change only if the training could not continue even after the supervising body tried to help them return or move <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>.

<a id="brokers-selling-asylum-as-a-work-route"></a>

### 16.5 Brokers selling asylum as a work route

- **The pitch:** a broker says an asylum claim gives a work permit, or that certain work earns a visa. In 2017 two Bangladeshi asylum seekers did Fukushima radiation clean-up after brokers and construction firms told them it would extend their visas; the Justice Ministry said no residence permit was on offer for that work <sup>[N37 (2017)](#n37)</sup>.
- **The facts:** ISA states, in a notice it publishes in Bengali, that applying for refugee recognition does not mean you can work in Japan <sup>[174](#o174), [168 (2019)](#o168)</sup>. Among claims ISA refused, the most common was trouble with acquaintances or neighbours at home (about 44%), two-thirds of it over debts; others gave a wish to work in Japan <sup>[171 (2018)](#o171)</sup>.
- **The cost to you:** working without permission carries up to 1 year in prison or a 2 million yen fine, and the broker or employer up to 3 years or 3 million yen <sup>[105](#o105)</sup>.

<a id="if-your-fear-of-persecution-is-real"></a>

### 16.6 If your fear of persecution is real

1. <a id="if-your-fear-of-persecution-is-real-item-1"></a>**Apply at the regional immigration bureau for where you live,** in person, with the application form, a 4 x 3 cm photo, evidence or a written statement of why you fear persecution, and your passport or residence card <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>. There is no fee and no time limit <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-your-fear-of-persecution-is-real-item-2"></a>**Go during counter hours:** weekdays 9:00 to 12:00 and 13:00 to 16:00 (Tokyo until 15:00) <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-your-fear-of-persecution-is-real-item-3"></a>**Wait.** ISA's standard time is 6 months, but the Japan Association for Refugees (JAR) reports an average of about 2 years 11 months <sup>[43](#o43), [N66](#n66)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-your-fear-of-persecution-is-real-item-4"></a>**If refused,** you can request review; how is explained in the refusal notice <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>.

JAR's office is at TAS Building 4F, 2-5-2 Nishi-Kanda, Chiyoda-ku, Tokyo, phone 03-5379-6001 <sup>[N66](#n66)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does JAR advise Bangladeshi applicants in Bengali, and on which days does it take walk-ins? <sup>[N66](#n66)</sup>

<a id="having-a-child-in-japan"></a>

## 17 Having a child in Japan

A baby born in Japan to two Bangladeshi parents does not get Japanese nationality, so the parents must register the birth in Japan, get the baby a status of residence and register the baby as a Bangladeshi <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-notify-the-birth-at-the-city-office-within-14-days"></a>

### 17.1 Step 1: Notify the birth at the city office within 14 days

1. <a id="step-1-notify-the-birth-at-the-city-office-within-14-days-item-1"></a>**Within 14 days,** the mother or father files the birth notification at the city or ward office of the place of birth or of their address <sup>[175](#o175)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-1-notify-the-birth-at-the-city-office-within-14-days-item-2"></a>**Get the certificate of entry items in the birth notification** (shussei todoke kisai jikō shōmeisho) from the city office; ISA needs it <sup>[158](#o158)</sup>.

<a id="step-2-the-babys-status-of-residence-within-30-days"></a>

### 17.2 Step 2: The baby's status of residence within 30 days

A baby who will stay longer than 60 days must apply to the regional immigration bureau within 30 days of birth <sup>[158](#o158), [166](#o166)</sup>. There is no fee <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[158](#o158), [155](#o155)</sup>:
- the application form for permission to acquire status of residence;
- the certificate of entry items in the birth notification;
- the baby's passport, if issued;
- proof of the parent's relationship and income (for a Dependent child: the supporter's residence card copy, employment certificate and tax certificates);
- a certificate of residence (jūminhyō) from the city office.

No photo is needed for a baby under 1 <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>. A child of a permanent resident can instead apply for permanent residence status within 30 days, free <sup>[161](#o161)</sup>.

<a id="step-3-register-the-birth-with-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-tokyo"></a>

### 17.3 Step 3: Register the birth with the Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo

The embassy issues birth certificates only to Bangladeshi citizens born in Japan <sup>[176](#o176)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-3-register-the-birth-with-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-tokyo-item-1"></a>**Apply online at bdris.gov.bd;** the embassy acts only on online applications <sup>[176](#o176)</sup>.
