Keep the email and phone number you used, because a later correction needs the same ones <sup>[176](#o176)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-3-register-the-birth-with-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-tokyo-item-2"></a>**Pay 250 yen** into the embassy's account: Mitsubishi UFJ Bank, Kojimachi branch (616), ordinary account 0393471, "Embassy of Bangladesh Tokyo Consular" <sup>[176](#o176)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-3-register-the-birth-with-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-tokyo-item-3"></a>**Send or bring these** <sup>[176](#o176)</sup>:
   - the printout of the online application;
   - both parents' online birth certificates;
   - both parents' passport copies;
   - the birth registration paper from the hospital or city office;
   - a family certificate;
   - the original payment receipt;
   - a return Letter Pack with your address.
4. <a id="step-3-register-the-birth-with-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-tokyo-item-4"></a>**Receive the certificate** in about 7 to 10 working days <sup>[176](#o176), [177](#o177)</sup>.

If you register the birth in Bangladesh instead, the place of birth must say "Japan", or the passport cannot show Japan <sup>[176](#o176)</sup>. Corrections cost 500 yen <sup>[176](#o176)</sup>.

<a id="step-4-the-babys-passport"></a>

### 17.4 Step 4: The baby's passport

1. <a id="step-4-the-babys-passport-item-1"></a>**Fill in the e-passport form online** at epassport.gov.bd and choose "Skip Payment"; the embassy collects the fee separately <sup>[177](#o177)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-4-the-babys-passport-item-2"></a>**Pay the fee** into the same embassy account <sup>[176](#o176)</sup>. The embassy's e-passport page lists 17,000 yen for a 48-page, 5-year passport and 21,000 yen for 10 years <sup>[178](#o178)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-4-the-babys-passport-item-3"></a>**Send or bring** the birth registration certificate, copies of both sides of the residence cards of the baby and both parents, both parents' passport copies, the original payment receipt, a photo (a 2L-size colour print for children under 6) and a sheet with your contact details <sup>[177](#o177), [178](#o178)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-4-the-babys-passport-item-4"></a>**Post it if you cannot come:** enclose a red-border Letter Pack (Japan Post's prepaid envelope, 520 yen) for the return; the e-passport page asks for two return Letter Packs for a child under 6 <sup>[177](#o177), [178](#o178)</sup>. A child under 5 need not attend for a first passport <sup>[177](#o177)</sup>; the e-passport page says a child under 6 needs no biometric enrolment <sup>[178](#o178)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-4-the-babys-passport-item-5"></a>**Wait 20 days to 1.5 months;** there are no emergency passports abroad <sup>[177](#o177)</sup>.

The embassy's consular section is open 10:00 to 14:00 on the first Sunday of each month; consular email consular.bdembjp@mofa.gov.bd, passport line 03-3230-1773 <sup>[177](#o177), [176](#o176), [118](#o118)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the baby's 5-year passport fee 17,000 yen (e-passport page, November 2026) or 18,000 yen (newborn FAQ, July 2026)? The FAQ says a preschool or kindergarten certificate does not qualify a child for the student rate <sup>[178](#o178), [177](#o177)</sup>.

<a id="sending-money-home"></a>

## 18 Sending money home

Money sent home from Japan reached Bangladesh at USD 105.9 million in fiscal year 2024-25 and USD 94.7 million in 2025-26 (Bangladesh's fiscal year runs July to June) <sup>[179](#o179)</sup>. Earlier years were USD 22.9 million (2016-17), 31.4 million (2017-18), 49.5 million (2018-19), 49.3 million (2019-20), 79.5 million (2020-21), 69.3 million (2021-22), 113.0 million (2022-23) and 76.6 million (2023-24) <sup>[179](#o179)</sup>. In July-August 2026 Japan was the 22nd-largest source, at USD 19.74 million (7.10 million in July, 12.64 million in August) <sup>[179](#o179)</sup>.

<a id="use-a-bank-or-registered-transfer-company-never-hundi"></a>

### 18.1 Use a bank or registered transfer company, never hundi

In Japan only banks and funds transfer service providers (資金移動業者, "shikin idō gyōsha": remittance companies registered with the Financial Services Agency, FSA, Japan's financial regulator) may send money abroad <sup>[104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>. Using one that is not licensed is illegal, and the FSA says that if you are unsure a provider is legitimate, do not use it <sup>[104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>. Bangladesh's government also asks workers to send money only through legal channels, not hundi (informal transfer through brokers who pay out in taka at home) or other unlicensed agents <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. Money sent legally earns the receiver a 2.5% government cash incentive, sometimes with a further 2.5% from the bank, and the 2026-27 budget keeps the government incentive <sup>[N67](#n67), [180](#o180)</sup>.

You'll need:
- For a bKash wallet (Bangladesh's largest mobile money service): the receiver's bKash number and full name exactly as registered <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>.
- For a bank account: your own or a relative's account in Bangladesh <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>.
- Papers showing the purpose of the transfer, if the bank or company asks <sup>[104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>.

1. <a id="use-a-bank-or-registered-transfer-company-never-hundi-item-1"></a>**Choose a registered company.** bKash names these partners for sending from Japan to a bKash account: Japan Remit, N and P Co Ltd, Queen Bee, Wise, WorldRemit, Western Union and MoneyGram <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>. On the FSA's register, as of 31 October 2026, are <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>:
   - Japan Remit Finance <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>.
   - N&P Japan, Hyakunincho, Shinjuku, 03-6302-1223 <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>.
   - Queen Bee Capital, Roppongi, Minato-ku, 03-6809-2785 <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>.
   - Wise Payments Japan <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>.
   - WorldRemit Ltd. <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>.
   - Western Union Japan <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>.
   - SBI Remit, Otsuka, Bunkyo-ku, 03-6229-0792 <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>. It sends through its partner MoneyGram, including to bank accounts in Bangladesh <sup>[N68](#n68)</sup>.
   - Brastel, Yokoami, Sumida-ku, 050-6860-1059 <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>.
   - City Express Money Transfer Japan, Hyakunincho, Shinjuku <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>.
   - Remitly Japan <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - MoneyGram, on bKash's Japan list, is not on the FSA register under its own name: does a MoneyGram transfer from Japan to a bKash wallet go through SBI Remit or another registered company <sup>[N67](#n67), [N68](#n68)</sup>?
   > - Which Japan companies pay into Nagad (Bangladesh's second mobile money service)?
   > - What does sending JPY 30,000 or 100,000 to Bangladesh cost, fee plus exchange margin, with each of these companies?
2. <a id="use-a-bank-or-registered-transfer-company-never-hundi-item-2"></a>**Check the company is really registered.** Open the FSA's list, "資金移動業者登録一覧" (www.fsa.go.jp/menkyo/menkyoj/shikin_idou.pdf), and find the company's name; it lists 86 providers, each with its Local Finance Bureau registration number, address and phone <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>. For details of a registration, ask the Local Finance Bureau that registered it <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>.
3. <a id="use-a-bank-or-registered-transfer-company-never-hundi-item-3"></a>**Send.** To bKash, give the partner the receiver's bKash number and registered name and hand over the money; up to Tk 2.5 lakh (1 lakh is 100,000) at a time, as many times as you like <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>. To a bank account, use a company or bank with a "drawing arrangement" (a payment link) with a Bangladeshi bank <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>.
4. <a id="use-a-bank-or-registered-transfer-company-never-hundi-item-4"></a>**The family collects.** Banks in Bangladesh credit remittances within 3 days at most <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>. bKash money can be cashed out at BRAC Bank, City Bank and Q-Cash ATMs for Tk 7 per thousand <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>.

<a id="hundi-and-underground-banking"></a>

#### 18.1.1 Hundi and underground banking

Moving money for others as a business ("exchange transactions") is banking business in Japan, so doing it without a licence is unlicensed banking, punishable by up to 3 years in prison, a fine of up to 3 million yen, or both <sup>[183](#o183), [184](#o184)</sup>. Passing money through your own account for a stranger, for example for a 10,000 yen reward, is also a crime (see [Selling or lending your bank account or phone](#selling-or-lending-your-bank-account-or-phone)) <sup>[104 (2024)](#o104), [185](#o185)</sup>. Doing something illegal with your bank account can stop you staying in or re-entering Japan <sup>[104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do hundi operators work among Bangladeshis in Japan (for example around Tokyo's Okachimachi, Ikebukuro or Shin-Okubo), how do they find customers, and have any been arrested?

<a id="never-sell-your-bank-account-when-you-leave"></a>

#### 18.1.2 Never sell your bank account when you leave

Handing over your passbook, cash card or online-banking login when you leave, paid or free, is a crime (see [Selling or lending your bank account or phone](#selling-or-lending-your-bank-account-or-phone)) <sup>[185](#o185)</sup>. Opening an account in order to hand it over can be charged as fraud, with up to 10 years in prison <sup>[185](#o185)</sup>. Accounts were sold for an average of 35,000 yen in 2024, according to the National Police Agency <sup>[N69](#n69)</sup>. Close your account before you go home, or return unused accounts to the bank <sup>[104 (2024)](#o104), [185](#o185)</sup>.

**If something goes wrong:**
- FSA Counseling Office for Financial Services Users: 0570-016811 (IP phone 03-5251-6811), weekdays 10:00-17:00, in Japanese; for English, email equestion@fsa.go.jp <sup>[104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>.
- bKash helpline: 16247 <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>.

<a id="saving-and-pension-in-bangladesh"></a>

### 18.2 Saving and pension in Bangladesh

<a id="nrcta-non-resident-convertible-taka-account"></a>

#### 18.2.1 NRCTA (Non-Resident Convertible Taka Account)

The NRCTA is a taka savings, current or fixed-deposit account for Bangladeshis abroad, funded by remittances, at a bank branch licensed for foreign exchange, under Bangladesh Bank's Circular 41 of 8 October 2026 <sup>[186](#o186)</sup>. A worker can open one before departure with no initial deposit <sup>[186](#o186)</sup>. Principal and interest can be sent abroad freely, but money sent into an NRCTA generally gets no 2.5% incentive unless stated otherwise <sup>[186](#o186)</sup>. A relative in Bangladesh can operate it under a power of attorney, money can go to a nominee if the holder dies, and the account can be kept after coming home for good <sup>[186](#o186)</sup>. Banks may lend in taka against it, including to buy a home for your own use <sup>[186](#o186)</sup>.

<a id="wage-earner-development-bond-wedb"></a>

#### 18.2.2 Wage Earner Development Bond (WEDB)

The WEDB is a 5-year government bond, sold in Tk 25,000, 50,000, 1 lakh, 2 lakh, 5 lakh, 10 lakh and 50 lakh units at Bangladesh Bank, banks' foreign-exchange branches and, abroad, at Bangladeshi banks' branches and exchange houses <sup>[187](#o187)</sup>. A legal wage earner buys it in taka with remittance sent through legal channels, in his own name or a named person's, with no ceiling <sup>[187](#o187)</sup>.
- Profit at maturity: 12% for holdings up to Tk 15 lakh, 11% for Tk 15-30 lakh, 10% for Tk 30-50 lakh and 9% above, paid every six months <sup>[187](#o187)</sup>.
- Cashing early pays less: 8.70% after 6-12 months, rising to 11.20% after 24-60 months <sup>[187](#o187)</sup>.
- Profit and investment are income-tax free, you can name a nominee and borrow against the bond, and the principal can be taken in US dollars <sup>[187](#o187)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does any bank or exchange house in Japan sell the WEDB, or must a worker in Japan buy it through a bank in Bangladesh?

<a id="the-probash-universal-pension"></a>

#### 18.2.3 The Probash universal pension

The National Pension Authority's "Probash" scheme is for Bangladeshis working or living abroad: you pay in foreign currency and the pension is paid in taka <sup>[188](#o188)</sup>. You join at 18 to 50 with your NID (national ID) or passport number <sup>[189 (2024)](#o189)</sup>. You pay until 60 and the pension is paid for life; you can borrow up to 50% of your deposit, but there is no lump-sum withdrawal before pension age <sup>[188](#o188), [190](#o190)</sup>. The monthly contribution is Tk 1,000, 2,000, 5,000, 7,500 or 10,000; on the scheme's estimate, Tk 1,000 a month for 10 years gives about Tk 1,530 a month <sup>[191](#o191)</sup>. From Japan you pay in foreign currency by card or a banking channel, not in taka, and contributions sent directly in foreign currency earn a government incentive <sup>[190](#o190), [189 (2024)](#o189)</sup>. Never give anyone cash for contributions; a late payment has 1 month's grace, then a 1% daily late fee <sup>[190](#o190)</sup>.

1. <a id="the-probash-universal-pension-item-1"></a>**Register** at www.upension.gov.bd: click Registration, choose Probash, and type your NID or passport number, date of birth, mobile number and the captcha <sup>[189 (2024)](#o189)</sup>.
2. <a id="the-probash-universal-pension-item-2"></a>**Fill in** your occupation, yearly income, permanent address, Bangladesh bank account name, number and routing number, and your nominee's ID number, relationship and share; then submit <sup>[189 (2024)](#o189)</sup>.
3. <a id="the-probash-universal-pension-item-3"></a>**Pay** the first contribution and keep the payment slip and pensioner ID <sup>[189 (2024)](#o189)</sup>.

**Help:** National Pension Authority, 43 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000; hotline 16131 or +88 09610 900800 (9 am-5 pm Bangladesh time); support@upension.gov.bd <sup>[188](#o188)</sup>.

<a id="going-home-without-valid-papers"></a>

## 19 Going home without valid papers

Staying past your permitted period without renewing or changing status is a crime in Japan, punishable by up to 3 years in prison, a fine of up to 3 million yen, or both <sup>[192](#o192)</sup>. An employer or broker who makes a foreigner work illegally faces the same penalty <sup>[192](#o192)</sup>. There were 71,229 overstayers in Japan on 1 July 2025 <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.

There are two ways out. A **departure order** (出国命令, "shukkoku meirei") lets an overstayer who comes forward leave by a simple procedure, without detention <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>. **Deportation** (退去強制, "taikyo kyōsei") follows an investigation and can include detention <sup>[193](#o193)</sup>. Which one you get decides how long you are barred from Japan:
- **Departure order, after coming forward yourself:** barred for 1 year from leaving <sup>[106](#o106), [112](#o112)</sup>.
- **Departure order, after being caught but saying you want to leave quickly, before the immigration inspector rules on your case:** barred for 5 years if you come back on a visitor (short-term stay) visa, and 1 year on any other visa <sup>[112](#o112)</sup>.
- **Deported:** barred for 5 years <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>. If you leave at your own cost with permission and immigration grants a "shortening decision", the ban is cut to 1 year, except for a visitor visa; the decision depends on your conduct and the reason for deportation and is not guaranteed <sup>[194](#o194), [106](#o106)</sup>.
- **Deported a second time, or after an earlier departure order:** barred for 10 years <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.
- **Sentenced to 1 year or more in prison (in Japan or elsewhere), or for any drug crime:** barred with no time limit <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.

You qualify for a departure order only if all of these are true <sup>[112](#o112), [106](#o106)</sup>:
- Overstaying is your only violation (someone who entered on a forged passport does not qualify).
- You have not been sentenced to prison for theft or certain other crimes.
- You have never been deported or left on a departure order before.
- You are certain to leave quickly.

<a id="help-from-bangladesh"></a>

### 19.1 Help from Bangladesh

A close relative in Bangladesh can ask the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare to help bring a worker home; the Ministry acts through the Embassy, WEWB (the Wage Earners' Welfare Board) or BMET, free, within 7 working days; the officer in charge is the Assistant Secretary (Welfare), aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>. The relative gives proof that the person is a migrant worker, his workplace and home address abroad, the recruiting agency's name and address and the employer's mobile number <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit"></a>

### 19.2 Step 1: Get a passport or Travel Permit

With a valid passport, go to [Step 2: Surrender to immigration](#step-2-surrender-to-immigration). Without one, get a Travel Permit (a one-way document home, valid 3 months) from the Bangladesh Embassy in Tokyo <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>. A new e-passport at the Embassy costs JPY 17,000 (48 pages, 5 years); without the old passport you bring the original police report of its loss <sup>[178](#o178)</sup>.

You'll need:
- An appointment: you come in person for an interview with the Consular Officer, and must sign or give a thumbprint yourself <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>.
- The completed application form <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>.
- A copy of your passport, if you have one <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>.
- Your Bangladeshi birth certificate and nationality certificate, attested by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs in Dhaka <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>.
- 4 recent passport photos (the Bangla list says 3) <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>.
- A copy of your air ticket <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>.
- The original bank receipt for the fee <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>.
- With a handwritten passport, a police report <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>.
- Under 18: a parent's written permission with copies of their passports or IDs <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit-item-1"></a>**Pay the fee into the Embassy's bank account:** JPY 3,000 regular (3 working days) or JPY 4,500 urgent (same day), to "Embassy of Bangladesh Tokyo Consular", MUFG Bank, Kojimachi branch (616), ordinary account 0393471 <sup>[195](#o195), [196](#o196)</sup>. Keep the original receipt <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit-item-2"></a>**Apply in person** at the Embassy, 3-29 Kioicho, Chiyoda-ku, Tokyo (hours: see [In Japan](#in-japan)) <sup>[115](#o115), [116 (2024)](#o116)</sup>. The Bangla page says the Travel Permit takes 3-5 working days, longer if a police report is needed <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit-item-3"></a>**Travel within 3 months** <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which document list does the consular counter apply: the English one (birth and nationality certificates attested by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs in Dhaka, 4 photos) or the Bangla one (passport copy, ticket copy, 3 photos) <sup>[195](#o195)</sup>?
> - Can a worker with no passport copy and no attested certificates prove nationality another way, such as with an NID?

**If it fails:** consular section, consular.bdembjp@mofa.gov.bd, 03-3234-5801 ext. 102 or 104, or 03-3230-1773 <sup>[195](#o195), [117](#o117), [116 (2024)](#o116)</sup>.

<a id="step-2-surrender-to-immigration"></a>

### 19.3 Step 2: Surrender to immigration

Surrendering (出頭申告, "shuttō shinkoku") is like turning yourself in to the police <sup>[197](#o197)</sup>. It does not make your stay legal: unless the Minister of Justice gives special permission to stay, you are still in violation, generally may not work, and can be arrested at your workplace <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>:
- Your valid passport (or, if you lost it, any ID, and your Travel Permit).
- An air ticket home or a travel agency's booking confirmation. Ask before you buy: the investigation can take long enough to make a ticket bought in advance unusable <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-2-surrender-to-immigration-item-1"></a>**Go on a weekday, in office hours,** to one of the 8 regional immigration bureaus (Sapporo, Sendai, Tokyo, Nagoya, Osaka, Hiroshima, Takamatsu, Fukuoka) or 3 district offices (Yokohama, Kobe, Naha) <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>. Tokyo's is at 5-5-30 Konan, Minato-ku <sup>[133](#o133)</sup>. At any other office you get a slip telling you when and where to report <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>. At an airport office you also get the slip, but generally cannot leave the same day <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-2-surrender-to-immigration-item-2"></a>**Say you want to go home quickly.** If you qualify, the case goes to a departure order rather than deportation <sup>[197](#o197), [106](#o106)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-2-surrender-to-immigration-item-3"></a>**Wait about 2 weeks.** From surrendering to receiving the departure order and leaving takes about 2 weeks, so book the flight with this in mind <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-2-surrender-to-immigration-item-4"></a>**Receive the departure order.** It sets a deadline of no more than 15 days to leave, and may limit where you live and travel <sup>[112](#o112)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-2-surrender-to-immigration-item-5"></a>**Don't work and don't miss the deadline.** Breaking a condition, such as working, can cancel the order; then you face deportation and prosecution, and so does anyone still in Japan after the deadline <sup>[112](#o112)</sup>.

If you want to stay in Japan instead, you can ask for special permission to stay during the deportation procedure, but it is an exceptional, discretionary decision by the Minister of Justice <sup>[197](#o197), [106](#o106)</sup>.

<a id="step-3-leave-japan"></a>

### 19.4 Step 3: Leave Japan

1. <a id="step-3-leave-japan-item-1"></a>**Pay your own way if you can.** To be allowed to leave at your own cost you need a valid passport and a ticket, or enough money to buy one <sup>[198](#o198)</sup>. Japan pays the fare only for people who cannot raise it or must be removed quickly on humanitarian grounds <sup>[198](#o198)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-3-leave-japan-item-2"></a>**Before the airport,** close your bank account and claim the pension refund (see [The lump-sum pension refund](#the-lump-sum-pension-refund)) <sup>[104 (2024)](#o104), [199](#o199)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-3-leave-japan-item-3"></a>**On arrival in Dhaka,** the airport welfare desk gives food and water to people returning on an outpass or from jail (see [At the airport in Bangladesh](#at-the-airport-in-bangladesh)) <sup>[200](#o200)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-are-detained"></a>

### 19.5 If you are detained

**Detention or supervision.** If immigration suspects a deportation ground, it detains you under a detention order unless it places you under supervision measures (監理措置, "kanri sochi"): the procedure continues while you live outside under a supervisor's watch <sup>[193](#o193), [194](#o194)</sup>. Once a deportation order is issued and you cannot be removed at once, the chief examiner decides again between supervision and detention <sup>[198](#o198)</sup>.

**Provisional release.** Provisional release (仮放免, "karihōmen") is allowed only on health, humanitarian or similar grounds; no bond is needed, but you must live where told, report when called and not work <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.

**Visits and calls.**
- Visits: weekdays 9:00-12:00 and 13:00-15:00, once a day per detainee, up to 30 minutes; visitors show a residence card or passport and may not bring phones or cameras <sup>[106](#o106), [193](#o193)</sup>.
- Calls: outside calls are not passed to detainees, so visit or write; some centres let detainees phone out at set times <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.
- Finding someone: immigration generally won't confirm by phone whether a named person is held <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.
- Belongings: pack them into about one suitcase, since airlines usually allow one cabin bag and 20 kg checked <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.

**A passport from inside.** If you have no valid passport, immigration staff pass your passport application to the Bangladesh Embassy at your request <sup>[106](#o106)</sup>.

**Who to contact.** The Embassy's labour wing visits detention centres when needed and helps stranded workers go home (numbers: see [In Japan](#in-japan)) <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>. From Bangladesh, the family can ask WEWB for legal help, directly or through the district administrator's expatriate welfare branch or the DEMO (District Employment and Manpower Office); WEWB writes to the Embassy <sup>[201](#o201)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many Bangladeshis are held at Japan's immigration centres (Ushiku, Omura, the regional bureaus), and how often does the labour wing actually visit them?

<a id="if-a-worker-dies-is-injured-or-loses-the-job"></a>

## 20 If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job

Compensation for work injuries and deaths in Japan comes through Japan's workers' accident insurance (see [If you are injured at work](#if-you-are-injured-at-work)). On the Bangladesh side, WEWB pays grants and helps with the migrant insurance, but only for workers who left with BMET emigration clearance (the "smart card") or joined WEWB <sup>[78](#o78), [77](#o77)</sup>.

<a id="check-you-are-covered"></a>

### 20.1 Check you are covered

Workers with a BMET clearance card are WEWB members automatically; anyone can check clearance or membership at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check <sup>[77](#o77)</sup>. Workers cleared from 1 January 2023 also have Jiban Bima Corporation (JBC, the state life insurer) migrant insurance: Tk 10 lakh, 5 years, ages 18-55, for a Tk 1,000 one-time premium <sup>[79 (2024)](#o79)</sup>.

Students, people on visitor visas and others who went to Japan without BMET clearance can join WEWB through the Embassy <sup>[77](#o77), [18](#o18)</sup>:
1. Fill in WEWB's online membership form at portal.wewb.gov.bd; the application goes through the Embassy's labour wing <sup>[18](#o18), [77](#o77)</sup>.
2. Pay the JPY 8,500 membership fee into the Embassy's welfare fund account: "Welfare Fund for Bangladeshi Wage Earners in Japan", MUFG Bank, Meguro Ekimae branch (104), savings account 0746699 <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.
3. Send the Embassy a copy of the completed form, a copy of your residence card, work permit or visa, a passport copy, 2 photos and the fee receipt <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.

**Help:** labour wing, fslabor@mofa.gov.bd, 03-3234-5801 ext. 201 or 080-4352-4795 <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does WEWB membership taken in Japan bring the JBC insurance, or only the WEWB grants?

<a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill"></a>

### 20.2 If you are injured or fall ill

1. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-1"></a>**In Japan,** claim workers' accident insurance (see [If you are injured at work](#if-you-are-injured-at-work)).
2. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-2"></a>**Going home sick or injured.** On the worker's or family's application, with proof of clearance or membership and a passport copy, WEWB asks the Embassy to bring the worker home and in special cases helps with hospital admission <sup>[202](#o202)</sup>. A free ambulance from Dhaka and Chattogram airports takes injured or sick returnees on request <sup>[203](#o203)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-3"></a>**Treatment aid in Bangladesh.** Returnees with clearance or membership can get up to Tk 2 lakh for treatment; apply within 1 year of return with medical reports, prescriptions, a bank statement and copies of the passport and arrival stamp <sup>[204](#o204)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Is the treatment aid up to Tk 2 lakh, as WEWB says, or Tk 1.5 lakh, as the Embassy's labour wing page says <sup>[204](#o204), [18](#o18)</sup>?
4. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-4"></a>**The insurance.** It pays 100% of Tk 10 lakh for total permanent disability and a share for partial disability: 90% for a whole arm or leg, 75% for an arm at the elbow or leg below the knee, 50% for one eye or one hand, 25% for the lower jaw or four fingers with the thumb, 15% for a thumb or all toes <sup>[79 (2024)](#o79), [11](#o11)</sup>. WEWB checks the claim and JBC pays through WEWB; claim with proof of clearance or membership and a passport copy, free, on 16135 <sup>[79 (2024)](#o79), [205](#o205)</sup>.

<a id="if-a-worker-dies"></a>

### 20.3 If a worker dies

1. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-1"></a>**Call the emergency services:** 119 for an ambulance, 110 for the police <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-2"></a>**Tell the Embassy.** The labour wing helps with burial in Japan or sending the body home <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>. Its Dead Body Repatriation page is still "Under Construction", so phone (see [In Japan](#in-japan)) <sup>[206](#o206)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which papers does the Embassy need to issue its no-objection certificate for a body, and what does it charge?
3. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-3"></a>**Decide: body home, or cremation or burial in Japan.** Sending a body home needs embalming, a flight booking and the embassy's certification, handled by specialised funeral companies; report to the embassy's consular section first <sup>[207](#o207)</sup>. Osaka Prefecture's foreign-patient helpline advised in February 2026 that the family pays the whole transport cost and should get quotes from several companies <sup>[207](#o207)</sup>. One guide puts cremation before repatriation at about 500,000 yen and embalming at about 1.5 million yen, and names Sanyu Life (03 3341 6664), Air Mortuary Service (0120 06 5191) and International Mortuary Services Japan (048 261 3302) <sup>[N70](#n70)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What does it cost now to fly an embalmed body from Tokyo to Dhaka, and who pays in practice for a technical intern or SSW worker: the employer, the supervising organisation, the Embassy or WEWB?
   > - Where can a Muslim be buried in Japan, and at what cost?
4. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-4"></a>**The family in Bangladesh asks for help.** WEWB takes the family's choice (body home or burial in Japan) to the Embassy and follows the case, free <sup>[208](#o208), [209](#o209)</sup>. The Ministry also brings home the bodies of irregular, undocumented and destitute workers: the next of kin applies with what happened, the cause of death, proof of status and the death certificate; free, 7 working days <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>. Probash-Bondhu call centre: 16135, +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[14](#o14)</sup>.
5. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-5"></a>**At the airport: Tk 35,000.** For every body that arrives, the airport welfare desk gives the family a Tk 35,000 cheque for transport and burial, free, collectable within 3 months <sup>[210](#o210), [200](#o200)</sup>. Bring the deceased's passport copy, a heirship certificate from the local chairman or councillor, the NID of the wife, father or mother, the death certificate, the NOC (no-objection certificate), the original airway bill and BMET clearance or membership proof, if any <sup>[200](#o200)</sup>. A free ambulance is available on request <sup>[203](#o203)</sup>.
6. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-6"></a>**The Tk 3 lakh grant.** WEWB pays the family Tk 3 lakh if the worker had clearance or membership and died in the country the clearance was for <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>. If the family took the airport cheque, no separate application is needed; if the worker was cremated or buried in Japan, apply within 1 year to WEWB's Director General or the district welfare centre <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>. It goes to the spouse, children and parents, or, for an unmarried worker with no living parents, to full siblings, paid into the family's bank account by BEFTN (electronic transfer) <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>. The family applies with <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>:
   - A family-members certificate from the union council chairman, mayor or city councillor, countersigned by the UNO (sub-district chief officer).
   - An undertaking on Tk 400 non-judicial stamp paper.
   - The Embassy NOC or the death certificate.
   - A bank statement with the routing number.
   - Attested photos of each family member and the deceased's passport copy.
7. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-7"></a>**The insurance: Tk 10 lakh** for death, claimed through WEWB as in If you are injured or fall ill, item 4 <sup>[11](#o11), [79 (2024)](#o79), [205](#o205)</sup>. If the worker had already been paid for a partial disability, that amount is deducted <sup>[79 (2024)](#o79)</sup>.
8. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-8"></a>**Money owed in Japan.** When the Embassy reports a possible claim for compensation or dues, WEWB asks the family for a power of attorney and papers and follows the case; its legal cell helps prepare them <sup>[211](#o211)</sup>. The next of kin can also apply to the Ministry at aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd with proof of status, the employer's details and the amounts owed; the Ministry writes to the Embassy within 7 working days <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>. A pension refund (see [The lump-sum pension refund](#the-lump-sum-pension-refund)) passes to close relatives only if the worker had sent the claim before dying <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-lose-the-job"></a>

### 20.4 If you lose the job

1. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-1"></a>**Your status in Japan.** An SSW (Specified Skilled Worker) must report the end of the contract within 14 days and can lose the status after 3 months without the work (see [If your job ends: the 14-day report and the 3-month rule](#if-your-job-ends-the-14-day-report-and-the-3-month-rule)). A technical intern trainee changes training site through the supervising organisation and OTIT (see [Technical intern trainees: changing training site](#technical-intern-trainees-changing-training-site)).
2. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-2"></a>**Unemployment benefit in Japan.** A worker with employment insurance (koyō hoken) can claim the basic allowance at Hello Work (the public employment office) <sup>[212](#o212)</sup>. You need 12 months of insured work in the 2 years before leaving the job, or 6 months in 1 year if you were dismissed or the company went bankrupt <sup>[212](#o212)</sup>. It pays about 50-80% of your average daily wage for 90-360 days, within 1 year of leaving the job, and you must register as a jobseeker and be able to work <sup>[212](#o212)</sup>. Bring Hello Work the notice of separation your employer issues; if you quit by choice, you get nothing for 2 months after a 7-day waiting period <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Can an SSW worker or technical intern draw the basic allowance while looking for a new employer, and do Hello Work offices offer Bengali interpreting?
3. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-3"></a>**Unpaid wages:** see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid).
4. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-4"></a>**Job-loss insurance from Bangladesh.** Tk 50,000 if you lose the job and return within 6 months of going, but not if you went entirely at the employer's cost; claim through WEWB with proof of clearance or membership and a passport copy, free <sup>[79 (2024)](#o79), [205](#o205)</sup>.
5. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-5"></a>**Unpaid wages, allowances or insurance, from Bangladesh.** You or your family can apply to the Ministry with what happened, proof of status, the employer's name and address and the amounts owed; it writes to the labour wing within 7 working days, free <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>.

**If a claim stalls:**
- Probash-Bondhu: 16135, +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[14](#o14)</sup>.
- JBC Group Insurance Division: 02-223381423, gid@jbc.gov.bd <sup>[79 (2024)](#o79)</sup>.
- WEWB complaints focal person: 02-222220976, ad.pro@wewb.gov.bd <sup>[213 (2024)](#o213)</sup>.

<a id="coming-home"></a>

## 21 Coming home

Before you leave Japan, file the moving-out notification at your city or ward office and claim back your pension contributions within 2 years of leaving (see [Leaving at the end of the job](#leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job); see [The lump-sum pension refund](#the-lump-sum-pension-refund)) <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

<a id="at-the-airport-in-bangladesh"></a>

### 21.1 At the airport in Bangladesh

At Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet airports, WEWB's Probashi Kallyan (expatriate welfare) desks meet returning workers <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. They give food and water to people returning on an outpass or from jail, help with customs and lost luggage, send sick returnees to hospital, arrange a stay at the Wage Earners' Centre, and take complaints from returnees who were abused or cheated <sup>[200](#o200)</sup>. Workers back because of war, disaster or lay-off can get financial help <sup>[200](#o200)</sup>.
- Dhaka desk: +88 02 8901040, mobile +880 1971 430203, welfaredesk@gmail.com <sup>[200](#o200)</sup>.
- Chattogram desk: +880 2333300957, mobile +880 1912542185, pkd.chattogram@wewb.gov.bd <sup>[200](#o200)</sup>.
- Sylhet desk: 01321158330, mobile 01973-898375, pkdsylhet@gmail.com <sup>[200](#o200)</sup>.

The Wage Earners' Centre near Shahjalal airport (Lonjonipara, Borua, Khilkhet, Dhaka-1229) charges Tk 100 admission and Tk 200 a night; book at wec.wewb.gov.bd or in person with clearance or membership proof, a passport copy and the air ticket; 01310 350555 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>. The Probashi Lounge at Shahjalal airport gives free wifi, phone and printing on clearance or membership proof <sup>[215](#o215)</sup>.

<a id="what-you-can-bring-home"></a>

### 21.2 What you can bring home

Bangladesh's Non-tourist Passenger Baggage Rules 2025 set what a returning worker may bring <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- **Duty-free baggage:** up to 65 kg, plus 35 kg more of clothes, personal items and books, for anyone aged 12 or over <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- **Duty-free items, one of each:** including a laptop, printer, camera, microwave, rice cooker, blender, sewing machine, fan, a TV up to 29 inches, a music system and 1 carton of cigarettes <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- **Phones:** 2 used and 1 new mobile phone, the new one once a year <sup>[217](#o217)</sup>.
- **Jewellery:** up to 100 g of gold or 200 g of silver jewellery, declared, once a year, no more than 12 of one type <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- **Gold bar:** one bar of up to 117 g (10 tola), declared, once a year, at Tk 5,000 duty per 11.664 g; extra or hidden gold is confiscated <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- **Large goods with duty:** a 30-36 inch TV Tk 10,000, a refrigerator Tk 5,000, a window air conditioner Tk 7,000, a split AC up to 18,000 BTU Tk 15,000, a washing machine Tk 3,000 <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- **Not allowed:** a Bangladeshi passport holder may not bring in any alcohol <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>.
- **Cash:** up to USD 10,000 in foreign currency without declaring; above that, declare on the FMJ (Foreign Money and Jewellery) form <sup>[218 (2020)](#o218), [217](#o217)</sup>.

Every passenger fills in the baggage declaration form or submits it online; with nothing to declare, use the green channel <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>. If you missed declaring, you can still declare on the same form within 30 days of arrival <sup>[216](#o216)</sup>. Customs emergency line at Shahjalal: +88 01319-056591 <sup>[217](#o217)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do customs officers still apply USD 5,000, the figure on Bangladesh Customs' own currency page <sup>[219](#o219)</sup>?

<a id="support-after-return"></a>

### 21.3 Support after return

1. <a id="support-after-return-item-1"></a>**PKB rehabilitation loan.** Probashi Kallyan Bank (PKB, the state bank for migrants) lends returnees up to Tk 50 lakh for a project: up to Tk 3 lakh without collateral, up to 10 years at 9% simple interest, decided within 15 working days <sup>[220](#o220)</sup>. You need an updated trade licence, project details and a guarantor with a clean credit record, among other papers <sup>[220](#o220)</sup>. Fees are 0.50% processing and 0.50% documentation, each at most Tk 15,000 <sup>[220](#o220)</sup>.
2. <a id="support-after-return-item-2"></a>**WEWB reintegration.** WEWB's World Bank-funded RAISE (Recovery and Advancement of Informal Sector Employment) project ran from July 2021 to June 2026, giving returnees cash incentives, help with self-employment and loans, counselling and training through district welfare centres <sup>[221](#o221), [213 (2024)](#o213)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which RAISE services continue after June 2026 <sup>[221](#o221)</sup>?
3. <a id="support-after-return-item-3"></a>**For returned technical intern trainees.** IM Japan (International Manpower Development Organization, Japan) says the home government is responsible for helping returnees find work or start a business <sup>[N51](#n51)</sup>. Those who finished Technical Intern Training (iii) can join a distance-learning course for team leaders run by IM Japan and KGS (International Skills and Technology Promotion Foundation) <sup>[N51](#n51)</sup>.
4. <a id="support-after-return-item-4"></a>**Japanese companies in Bangladesh.** About 350 Japanese companies operate in Bangladesh (JETRO, the Japan External Trade Organization, March 2026) <sup>[N71](#n71)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Do any Japanese companies in Bangladesh, BMET or BOESL run a scheme to hire Japan returnees, and is there a returnee association or database?
   > - Does Bangladesh pay returned interns any business start-up grant, as the 2017 government-to-government intern agreement is reported to provide?
5. <a id="support-after-return-item-5"></a>**BRAC Migration Programme.** BRAC, a development organisation founded in Bangladesh in 1972 and the organisation behind this guide, gives returnees airport help, counselling, help to start earning, and legal assistance <sup>[222](#o222), [223](#o223)</sup>.
6. <a id="support-after-return-item-6"></a>**Children's scholarships.** WEWB pays scholarships to migrants' high-achieving children (Tk 27,500 a year at SSC, Secondary School Certificate, level and Tk 34,000 at HSC, Higher Secondary Certificate, level) and reserves 0.5% of HSC admission seats for them <sup>[213 (2024)](#o213)</sup>.
7. <a id="support-after-return-item-7"></a>**If your family is threatened** or its property seized, WEWB acts through the Deputy Commissioner or Superintendent of Police <sup>[213 (2024)](#o213)</sup>.
8. <a id="support-after-return-item-8"></a>**Savings.** An NRCTA can be kept after you come home for good (see [Saving and pension in Bangladesh](#saving-and-pension-in-bangladesh)) <sup>[186](#o186)</sup>.

<a id="contacts"></a>

## 22 Contacts

<a id="in-japan"></a>

### 22.1 In Japan

- **Bangladesh Embassy, Tokyo:** 3-29 Kioicho, Chiyoda-ku, Tokyo 102-0094; Nagatacho Station exit 9a/9b (about 9 minutes' walk) or Kojimachi Station exit 1 (about 6 minutes); tel 03-3234-5801, fax 03-3234-5802 <sup>[115](#o115)</sup>. General email bdembassy.tokyo@mofa.gov.bd; consular consular.bdembjp@mofa.gov.bd <sup>[117](#o117)</sup>. Open Monday-Friday except holidays, 9:00-17:00; phones are not answered 13:00-14:00 <sup>[116 (2024)](#o116)</sup>. Passport, birth registration and attestation counters 9:00-17:00, ext. 102 and 104, or 03-3230-1773 direct <sup>[116 (2024)](#o116), [118](#o118)</sup>. Special consular service (not visas) on the first Sunday of each month, 10:30-14:30 <sup>[116 (2024)](#o116)</sup>. Since August 2025 the embassy gives NID (national identity card) services: apply online at services.nidw.gov.bd, then give your biometrics and submit the documents at the embassy <sup>[N72](#n72)</sup>.
- **Embassy emergency hotline (24/7, for Bangladeshis in distress, not for routine passport, visa or consular queries):** 070-1260-1984, 080-7004-1971 or 080-4065-6601 <sup>[115](#o115), [116 (2024)](#o116)</sup>; the Phone Numbers page also shows 090-3558-1968 <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>.
- **Embassy labour welfare wing:** Counsellor, 03-3234-5801 ext. 201, zakir2kbd@gmail.com <sup>[115](#o115)</sup>; First Secretary (Labour), fslabor@mofa.gov.bd <sup>[117](#o117)</sup>; mobile 080-4352-4795 <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>. It handles legal aid for unpaid wages, visits to workplaces, hospitals and detention centres, repatriation of stranded workers and help with bodies <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.
- **Immigration Services Agency information centre:** 0570-013904; from an IP phone or abroad 03-5796-7112; weekdays 8:30-17:15 <sup>[133](#o133)</sup>. Languages include English, Nepali and Sinhala, but not Bengali <sup>[133](#o133)</sup>. It gives general guidance only and cannot tell you how a case is progressing <sup>[133](#o133)</sup>.
- **FRESC (Foreign Residents Support Center):** Yotsuya Tower 13F, 1-6-1 Yotsuya, Shinjuku-ku, Tokyo 160-0004; 0570-011000 <sup>[194](#o194)</sup>.
- **OTIT (Organization for Technical Intern Training) native-language hotline:** English 0120-250-147 (toll free), Tuesday, Thursday and Saturday; there is no Bengali line <sup>[17](#o17), [15](#o15)</sup>. If you are being hit or threatened, call your OTIT regional office and press 1 for a dedicated operator <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Police:** 110. **Fire and ambulance:** 119. Both free <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.
- **Japan Pension Service (Foreign Business Group):** +81-3-6700-1165, in Japanese <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.
- **FSA counselling office:** 0570-016811; English by email, equestion@fsa.go.jp <sup>[104 (2024)](#o104)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which emergency number does the Embassy answer now: 090-3558-1968, shown on its Phone Numbers page (June 2026) although that page's call button dials 070-1260-1984, or the three numbers on its Contact and Address pages <sup>[118](#o118), [115](#o115)</sup>?

<a id="japans-offices-in-bangladesh"></a>

### 22.2 Japan's offices in Bangladesh

- **Embassy of Japan, Dhaka:** Plot 5 & 7, Dutabash Road, Baridhara, Dhaka-1212; +880-2-2222-60010; eojbd@dc.mofa.go.jp; open Sunday-Thursday 9:00-17:00 (lunch 12:30-13:30) <sup>[224 (2020)](#o224), [225 (2021)](#o225)</sup>.
- **Embassy visa section:** consular@dc.mofa.go.jp, +880-2-2222-60010; since 3 November 2024 it takes no visa applications directly <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.
- **VFS Global (Japan visa applications):** Delta Life Tower, 4th floor, Road 90, Plot 37, Gulshan-2, Dhaka-1212; 09606 777 910; info.japanbd@vfshelpline.com; visas take at least 7 working days <sup>[9 (2024)](#o9)</sup>.
- **JICA (Japan International Cooperation Agency) Bangladesh Office:** 3rd floor, Bay's Galleria, 57 Gulshan Avenue, Gulshan-1, Dhaka-1212; (880-2) 226601304-07; bd_oso_rep@jica.go.jp <sup>[226](#o226)</sup>.

<a id="in-bangladesh"></a>

### 22.3 In Bangladesh

- **BOESL (Bangladesh Overseas Employment and Services Limited, the state recruiting company, which recruits technical interns for Japan):** Probashi Kallyan Bhaban (4th floor), 71-72 Eskaton Garden, Ramna, Dhaka-1000; hotline 01765411653; info@boesl.gov.bd <sup>[90](#o90), [91 (2023)](#o91)</sup>.
- **BMET:** Director (Emigration), +8802222222415, diremi@bmet.gov.bd <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Complaints against agencies: application with proof to BMET's Director General, free, 30 working days <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
- **WEWB:** membership check at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check <sup>[77](#o77)</sup>; complaints focal person 02-222220976, 01817-506712, ad.pro@wewb.gov.bd <sup>[213 (2024)](#o213)</sup>.
- **Probash-Bondhu call centre (WEWB), 24/7:** 16135 toll free in Bangladesh, +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[14](#o14)</sup>.
- **PKB:** helpline 16238, from abroad +880 9677-787878; loans 01716-175560, migration@pkb.gov.bd <sup>[80](#o80), [83](#o83)</sup>.
- **JBC Group Insurance Division:** 24 Motijheel C/A, Dhaka-1000; 02-223381423, 41052772; gid@jbc.gov.bd <sup>[79 (2024)](#o79)</sup>.
- **Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare:** complaints from abroad to minister.expat@gmail.com <sup>[227](#o227)</sup>; deaths and compensation aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>; agency complaints sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030255 <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>; grievance officer 02-41030224, adsadmin@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>.
- **Airport welfare desks:** Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet (see [At the airport in Bangladesh](#at-the-airport-in-bangladesh)) <sup>[200](#o200)</sup>.
